# Nanogram iOS changelog (private)

Newest first. Internal notes for each TestFlight build; testers see the What to Test notes instead.

## Unreleased

- **Build fix pins**: quickjs 1bc6f4dc (forward declaration of js_set_uncatchable_pending; clang stopped on it), widget-sandbox e358184b, nanogram-deps 64a5d41e.
- **`device_signing.py profiles` uses the App Store Connect key** when Xcode has no Apple ID signed in (it failed with "No Accounts"), and **picks the connected iPhone**: it took the first physical device in `devicectl list devices`, an unavailable iPad, and xcodebuild failed to find the destination. It now prefers a connected iPhone, then any connected device, then any physical one (`physical_devices`, tests in `FirstPhysicalDeviceTests`).
- **Store build review fixes (iOS + Mac shared core)** (2026-11-14, not built; sources parse, policy table test updated): `StorePurchasePolicy` has a new blocked entry `.tonTopUp`, and every `ton_topup_url` opener (Gift buy, resale, emoji game stake, withdrawal, chat paid-message alert, appearance screen, suggested-post funds) now goes through `openTonTopUp(context:)` (BalanceNeededScreen module), which shows "TON can't be bought in this app" in store builds instead of opening Fragment. Premium accounts keep premium stickers, premium emoji in Recents, message effects and custom-emoji reactions in store builds: the keyboard filters use `isPremiumDisabled && !hasPremium`, the reaction picker allows custom emoji for Premium accounts. The TON balance screen keeps "Withdraw via Fragment" (owned funds, not a purchase). Store listing docs corrected: advertising is Yes (sponsored messages), official app is 13+, privacy label adds Contact Info, Financial Info, Purchases, Diagnostics and Usage Data, privacy policy and review notes match the code (diagnostic events, no Firebase, on-device switches fail closed, link hygiene off by default), account-deletion path, encryption wording, Watch keyword note.
- **Final fork pins (reconciliation, 2026-11-14)**: tgcalls 198c75ff (fork branch renamed to `nanogram`; livestream audio-decoder guard), webrtc c0e14474 (Mac BoringSSL identity sources, jni_zero include), twidget 07845c84 (repaired Apple layer), nanogram-deps c759d0f3. Every klaipher fork now has exactly one branch, `nanogram`, set as default; 162 old branches were deleted on GitHub (none held unique work) and klaipher/tg_owt, klaipher/rlottie and the unused klaipher/tlottie were archived.
- **Store builds sell no Telegram purchases: StorePurchasePolicy (iOS + Mac shared core; Android has its own entry)** (2026-11-14, not built or run; syntax checks only, tests run in the combined build). One policy object, `StorePurchasePolicy` (`submodules/InAppPurchaseManager/Sources/StorePurchasePolicy.swift`), decides per entry point (`allows(.premiumSubscription / .premiumGiftPurchase / .premiumGiveawayPurchase / .starsTopUp / .starsGiftPurchase / .paidBoostPurchase / .storeKitProductRequests / .receiptRestore)`); viewing Premium status, using Premium features, spending owned Stars, receiving or converting gifts, gift codes and physical-goods bot payments are always allowed. `StorePurchasePolicy.current` is a store build unless `NANOGRAM_PURCHASES_ENABLED` is defined, which is the one switch for a future non-store build. Entry points found and handled: (1) `PremiumConfiguration` (`AccountContext/Sources/Premium.swift`): `isPremiumDisabled`, `areStarsDisabled`, `showPremiumGiftIn*`, `giveawayGiftsPurchaseAvailable`, `starsGiftsPurchaseAvailable` are forced off, whatever the server says; the existing limit sheets, upsells, privacy rows, Stories and attach-menu gift buttons already follow them. (2) `InAppPurchaseManager`: no `SKPaymentQueue` observer, no `SKProductsRequest` (and so no retry loop), `buyProduct` fails with `notAllowed`, `restorePurchases` returns at once; `AccountContext.inAppPurchaseManager` is `nil` in store builds, so the Premium intro, Stars purchase, gift and giveaway screens get no store products. (3) `PremiumIntroScreen`: the subscribe/upgrade button and its panel are hidden (an unused gift code still shows Apply); `tg://premium_offer` and every upsell open the feature description only. (4) `StarsPurchaseScreen` (single factory `makeStarsPurchaseScreen`, so every top-up opener: gifts, paid reactions, paid media, paid messages, subscriptions, `tg://stars_topup`, the Stars balance screen): shows "Stars can't be bought in this app" and closes. (5) `GiftOptionsScreen`: no Premium gift options. (6) `PremiumBoostLevelsScreen` and `ReplaceBoostScreen`: no gift-Premium-for-boosts action. (7) Chat-list notices `gracePremium` and `xmasPremiumGift` are skipped; the annual/upgrade/restore notices already need store products. (8) Send Report info has a `Store build:` line. Known cost: with `isPremiumDisabled` forced, other people's Premium badges in chat lists and profiles are hidden too (Telegram's own review mode does the same); a separate flag for badges is the follow-up. Also still reachable: the sign-up-requires-Premium payment screen (`AuthorizationSequencePaymentScreen`) cannot sell anything and has no way forward; `tg://restore_purchases` does nothing.
- **Review fixes for the on-device switches and secret-chat delete (iOS + Mac shared core)** (2026-11-14, not built or run; syntax checks only, tests run in the combined build). (1) Block + Delete on a secret chat sent no flush: the second `removePeerChat` queued a `terminateNow` and marked the chat terminated before `clearHistory` ran. `secretChatTerminationPlan` now has `.foldIntoQueuedTermination` (a terminate is already in the SecretOutgoing log): it adds nothing and only folds `reportSpam` into the queued terminate. (2) While a terminate is queued, incoming secret messages are dropped in both processing stages (`secretChatTerminationQueued`), like on a terminated chat. (3) With the on-device translation switch on, nothing falls back to the server: `localOnly` on `translateMessages` fails a missing local result (no model, dismissed download, no language pair) instead of calling Telegram; rich messages are skipped; the server-configured system translator (switch off) keeps the upstream fallback. (4) With on-device transcription on, the Premium server retry is gone (`shouldRetryTranscriptionOnServer` removed). (5) Switch texts no longer promise more than they do, and give model sizes (about 50-100 MB). (6) `shouldTranslateOnDevice` moved below the doc comment it had split. Tests: `SecretChatTerminationPlanTests` (sequence-based layer, handshake, fold), `TranslationFallbackTests`. Not done: progress or error UI for the model download, `LanguageAvailability`/`prepareTranslation` from the switch, a toast on a failed local translation.
- **Server config reading fixes (iOS + Mac shared core)** (2026-11-14, not built; syntax-only checks, tests run in the combined build; from the server-switches review). (1) `call_ring_timeout_ms` is now read from `help.getConfig` (`Api.Config.callRingTimeoutMs`, stored in `ManagedConfigurationUpdates` under a small preferences entry, `CallRingTimeout.store/current`); 4ff25ba7 read it from the app config JSON, where the server never sends it (core.telegram.org/api/config lists it only as a config field), so the 90 s default always applied. Bounds (1 s to 600 s), the 90 s default and the 10 s grace are unchanged; the app-config read is gone. (2) `SearchStickers.swift`: key `"stickers_premium_by_emoji_num "` had a trailing space, so the `&&` guard always failed and the emoji sticker-suggestion config (cache time, normal-per-premium, premium count) always used 86400 / 2 / 0; the server values now apply. (3) `starref_start_param_prefixes` is read as an array of strings (a single string is still accepted), default `["_tgr_"]`; any prefix matches (`StarRefStartParamPrefixes`, used by `UrlHandling`). (4) Poll "add option" in a chat used 20 as the fallback for `poll_answers_max` while the composer used 10; both now fall back to 10 (the server value wins; Mac and Android fall back to 10 and 12). Tests: `CallRingTimeoutTests` (bounds), `StarRefStartParamPrefixesTests`. Mac needs the same `starref` and `hidden_members_group_size_min` fixes in its own files (separate task).
- **On-device transcription and translation switches, secret-chat delete for everyone (iOS + Mac shared core)** (2026-11-14, not built or run; syntax checks only, tests run in the combined build). (1) Settings > Nanogram Features > ON-DEVICE: "On-Device Voice Transcription" (default OFF now; d6d49452/070ce28e had it on for everyone; off means server transcription with Premium rules, like upstream; stored under a new key so the old default does not carry over; the Debug Settings row moved here) and "On-Device Translation" (default OFF; on uses the Translation framework for messages, polls and voice transcriptions, the system prompt downloads the language model on first use, a missing result falls back to the server). (2) Secret chat delete for everyone now flushes history to the peer before the chat is marked terminated (`secretChatTerminationPlan`; Mercurygram 01230d2c1, same bug). (3) Review findings of 8b43ddcb checked in the code: energy counters from zero, watch reply cap 60 KB, copy hygiene in the link menu, `from:` debounce are all in. Attribution lines for search operators and link hygiene (Mercurygram commits) added to NANOGRAM-CHANGES.md. Check in the build: transcription off shows the Premium/trial flow; on-device translation of a Spanish chat shows the download prompt once; delete a secret chat for everyone on a test pair and see the peer's chat cleared.
- **Review fixes: energy, watch report, link copy, from: lookups** (2026-11-14, not built or run). (1) The launch window now starts from zero counters (`EnergyCounters.zero`), so task energy, billed energy, instructions, cycles, disk writes and wake-ups show in energy.txt and the Debug rows; before, every one of them was empty. (2) The watch's `sendMessage` reply is cut to 60 KB (`WatchReportMessage.maximumReplyBytes`), under WatchConnectivity's reply limit (about 64 KB, undocumented); the file copy keeps 400 KB. (3) "Copy Link" in the chat link menu (both copy actions) now strips tracking parameters when the setting is on; the "Copy Link" actions in message context menus copy t.me links we build and stay as they are. (4) `from:` waits 0.6 s after the last keystroke before asking the server for a name, drops the lookup of the half-typed name, and asks again 30 s after a miss or failed lookup instead of remembering it for the life of the chat screen. (5) energy.txt and the tester guide now say rates are per wall-clock minute including background time. Open: section 10 (on-device transcription and translation switches) is not done; secret-chat delete-for-everyone needs a test pair.

- **Settings > Nanogram Features, search operators, link hygiene, and the Mercurygram review (iOS)** (2026-11-14, not built or run; syntax and stubbed type checks only, tests run in the combined build). New Settings row "Nanogram Features" (after Power Saving) opens an ItemList screen with four sections. SEARCH: "Search Operators", default ON: the search field inside a chat understands `from:@name` (or `me`), `before:`, `after:`, `date:` (`2026-11-01`, `2026-11`, `2026`, `today`, `yesterday`) and `type:` (photo, video, file, link, voice, music, gif, round, poll, pinned); unknown or unparsable tokens stay ordinary text; secret chats are left alone; the search highlight and the "see all results" list use the text without the operators. LINKS, both default OFF: "Remove Tracking from Links" strips known tracking parameters (utm_*, fbclid, gclid, igshid, si on YouTube/Spotify and so on) when a link is opened and when it is copied from the link menu in a chat, never from message text; "Confirm Telegram Links" asks before a t.me or tg:// link opens from a tap in the app. CALLS and WIDGETS AND SHORTCUTS: the user-facing switches moved here from Debug Settings (System Noise Suppression, Advanced Ducking, Group Noise Suppression, Thermal Video Limits, Widgets/Live Activities/Shortcuts); they use the same stored values, so nothing resets. Also: extending an indefinite live location can no longer overflow (`RequestEditMessage`). New module `submodules/NanogramFeatures` (pure parser and link rules, host tests), settings `NanogramFeaturesSettings` (shared data key 24), grammar and parameter list in `docs/specs/search-operators.md` and `docs/specs/link-tracking-params.txt` (the Mac and Android apps follow them). The coverage mirror of Mercurygram's bug fixes and optimizations is `docs/nanogram/mercurygram-port.md`: 28 items checked, one hardened, one open (secret-chat delete-for-everyone needs a test pair), the rest already had, not applicable or declined with reasons.
- **CPU and energy numbers in Send Report, and the Apple Watch's part (iOS + watchOS)** (2026-11-14, not built or run; syntax and type checks only, tests run in the combined build). Settings > Send Report now holds `energy/energy.txt`: the main app process's CPU time, task energy (`task_info` `TASK_POWER_INFO_V2`), billed energy, instructions, cycles, disk writes and wake-ups (`proc_pid_rusage` `RUSAGE_INFO_V4`, looked up with `dlsym` because the header is macOS-only), battery level and state, Low Power Mode and thermal state. Per performance experiment the numbers cover the window since its switch last changed (a changed switch starts from zero after the relaunch, the other state's last window stays beside it, the window carries over relaunches; below 30 s no rates, over 10% charging "Not comparable"), kept in `UserDefaults` (`nanogram.energyLedger`); the Debug Settings info row shows the short version. New modules `submodules/EnergyNumbers` (one source, `Telegram/WatchApp/Shared/EnergyNumbers.swift`, compiled into the phone module and the watch target) and `submodules/WatchReport`. The watch has no button: it keeps its own numbers (per `WatchFeature` flag) and its app log lines of the launch (`OSLogStore`), answers the phone's `sendMessage` request with a report, and hands the phone a copy with `transferFile` (at most every 30 minutes, when the scene goes inactive and after a background refresh). Send Report asks the watch (6 s), else uses the last transferred copy, and puts the files under `watch/` with the watch's own `manifest.txt`; no paired watch, no watch app, or no copy yet are said in the README and the report is sent without it. Tester guide and "What to Test" text updated (energy numbers replace "battery dropped X%" where the device reports them; battery % stays the fallback). Tests: `EnergyNumbersTests`, `WatchReportTests`, `DebugReportTests`, watch `WatchDiagnosticsTests`. Regenerated `tgwatch.xcodeproj`. Commit: see git log.
- **Voice transcription review fixes (2026-11-14, not built; syntax and Swift 6 type-check of the analyzer file only)**. Retrying on the server no longer leaves the device task undisposed (a debug build asserted "Leaked disposable" for Premium accounts when no on-device model existed). The locked state for the server trial cooldown no longer shows on a voice message when on-device transcription is on. On the on-device path a voice message that is not downloaded yet is fetched first instead of collapsing the button silently.
- **Send Report review fixes (iOS)** (not built or run; syntax-checked only; `submodules/DebugSettingsUI`). Crash reports are written as plain text (new `.plainText` treatment) so the phone mask no longer rewrites stack offsets of 8 or more digits. Parts are now kept in the order info, crashes, call logs, group call logs, critical logs, MetricKit, then the big app and extension logs, so a full app log cannot use up the 40 MB before the call logs. The info file again carries the Media capabilities block. The privacy note and README say what is masked (text logs only) and that message text is left out only while Remove Sensitive Data is on. A file that vanishes while the report is made (rotated log, pruned MetricKit file) is skipped and listed in the README instead of discarding the report. Tests: `testCallLogsSurviveFullAppAndNotificationLogs`, `testCrashFramesKeepTheirOffsets`. Not done: `health-energy.txt` with per-experiment CPU and energy per minute since the switch last changed, and its tester-guide text (follow-up).
- **Review repairs for the merged twidget and webrtc BUILD (2026-11-14, not built; syntax-only checks)**. twidget pinned at 07845c84: iOS dashed and dotted side borders clip to the side again, template and structure patches rebuild the render tree when it cannot be kept. `third-party/webrtc/BUILD`: `:libsrtp` gets `-DHAVE_NETINET_IN_H` and `-DHAVE_ARPA_INET_H` (datatypes.h hit `#error` without them), `:webrtc_rnnoise` gets the fork root as include so `rnn_vad_weights.cc` finds its header. `TwidgetForkTests` compiles against the fork: the calc_node block of `fork_test.cpp` is dropped and tabular-nums checks the `tnum` feature. VENDOR.md brought up to date.
- **Guard for background upload task identifiers** (IOS-BGTASK-1, 2026-11-14; test only, no behaviour change). The shipped plist is the `TelegramInfoPlist` template in `Telegram/BUILD`, which already lists `{telegram_bundle_id}.upload.*` (upstream); an earlier edit of the unused `Telegram-iOS/Info.plist` was reverted. `tools/dev/tests/test_background_task_identifiers.py` checks every continued-task prefix in `SharedWakeupManager.swift` against that template. The GPU resource for continued tasks needs the `com.apple.developer.background-tasks.continued-processing.gpu` entitlement, which the app does not have; story export keeps its foreground path.
- **One "Send Report" button for testers** (2026-11-14, not built or run by its author; syntax-checked only, tests run in the combined build). Settings > help section (after Tips) gets **Send Report**, and the top of Debug Settings > LOGS has the same row (visible without the 10-tap menu on purpose: testers already look under Settings for Ask a Question; the debug row is for a tester who is in the debug menu anyway). One zip through the existing destination flow (chat, email or other apps; a chat send names the chat and the account first): `README.txt` listing every part, `info/device-and-experiments.txt` (app, build, OS, device and chip, Log to File and redaction state, Low Power Mode, thermal state, experiment switches and stats from `performanceExperimentsReport`), `crashes/` (CrashReportStore text), `metrickit/` (MetricKit metric and diagnostic JSON, hangs included, plus `disk-writes.json`), `critical-logs/` (app and notification-extension short logs, incl. the call-ring `nse call` lines), `logs/app`, `logs/notification-extension`, `logs/share-extension`, `logs/other-extensions` (widget, Siri, broadcast, notification content), `calls/` (5 newest 1:1 call logs), `group-calls/` (3 newest), `cpu-profiles/`. Caps: newest first, 8 MB per file (tail kept, cut marker), 16 MB per part, 40 MB in all; omitted files are named in the README; the info part is always kept. Phone numbers written with a leading + and emails are masked in every text part (second net after Remove Sensitive Data); JSON and binary reports are copied unchanged. The one-line note about what is included is the action sheet's text (`debugReportPrivacyNote`). The developer's fine-grained Debug Settings > Send Logs menu stays (renamed "Send Logs (developer)"); guides and What to Test name only Send Report. Not in the report: watch logs (the watch app keeps none reachable from the phone), storage stats and the chat menu's media-download logs. New: `DebugReport.swift`, `DebugReportSources.swift`, `DebugReportTests`; `SharedAccountContext.presentSendReport` (harness stub added); `DebugLogExportKind.report`. Docs: `docs/testing/a15-a16-performance-experiments.md`, `tf-what-to-test-a15-a16.txt`.
- **Hardware codec paths behind one capability probe (iOS 26 floor, stream 2; not built, syntax-only checks, tests run in the combined build)**. New module `submodules/MediaCapabilities`: `MediaCapabilities.shared` caches `VTIsHardwareDecodeSupported` per codec (H.264, HEVC, HEVC with alpha, VP9 after `VTRegisterSupplementalVideoDecoderIfAvailable` on 26.2+, AV1) and the hardware list of `VTCopyVideoEncoderList`; `VideoDecodePolicy` holds the pure rules. `internal_isHardwareAv1Supported` (iOS and Mac definitions) is now that one probe. Fix: the passthrough and VideoToolbox-hwaccel open sites (`FFMpegFileReader`, the three `SoftwareVideoSource` readers) used the AV1 hardware path unconditionally, so an A12 to A16 chip (no AV1 decoder; true only on A17 Pro, M3 and newer) got a decoder that cannot decode; they now fall to dav1d unless the probe says hardware. Log line per launch and (codec, path): `MediaPlayer decode codec=av1 path=software`. Send Logs appends "Media capabilities" with hardware decode and encode per codec, so A13/A16/A17 reports differ visibly. VP9 stickers unchanged (already: 26.2 runtime check, hardware session check, libvpx fallback, 8-session cap, counters); the sticker probe stays in `VP9Hardware` because the Mac package symlinks that file. Images already decode through ImageIO (`CGImageSourceCreateThumbnailAtIndex`, JPEG, HEIC, AVIF, JXL); libjxl remains only as the fallback after ImageIO fails, its removal needs a build to check size and link, so it is left. IOS-VIDEO-1 (preview hardware decode) stays dark: the iOS 26 floor does not change its risk (VideoToolbox session limit next to a running player), the iPhone 14 testers decide. Tests: `MediaCapabilitiesTests` (probe caching, encode list, report, passthrough and hwaccel rules, one log line per pair). Mac: `MediaPlayer/Package.swift` lists the new `MediaCapabilities` package.

- **On-device voice-message transcription is the default** (IOS-SPEECH-2, 2026-11-14, not built; tests run in the combined build). The transcribe button shows on every voice message for every account and transcribes with SpeechAnalyzer on the device (no Premium, trial or server). The model of the current locale is downloaded when voice messages first appear (`prepareOnDeviceTranscription`; `ensureSpeechModel` keeps one in-flight download task per locale, and a tap during that download awaits the same task) instead of dropping to SFSpeechRecognizer; the legacy recognizer stays for unsupported locales, the server (Premium or boosted groups only) takes over when the device produced no text (`shouldRetryTranscriptionOnServer`). `isSpeechAnalyzerEnabled` is on unless the default is set to false; `ExperimentalUISettings.localTranscription` defaults to true under the new key `localTranscriptionOnDevice` (older saved values reset). Details in `NANOGRAM-CHANGES.md`. Tests: `TranscriptionSupportTests` (default on, retry policy, model action), `DebugControllerEntriesTests.testOnDeviceTranscriptionIsOnByDefaultAndTheChoiceIsKept`. Not yet compared for accuracy on device against SFSpeechRecognizer.
- **Build baseline for iOS 26.0: CPU, native libraries, tests, Rust (2026-11-14, not built; syntax-only checks, tools tests run)** (stream 1). Every `minimum_os_version` in the repository now follows `Telegram/BUILD` (151 test and harness targets were still 15.0, 12.0, 11.0 or 9.0; `tools/dev/set-min-os.py` rewrites them, `--check` reports drift, run it after every upstream sync). With the 26.0 floor clang and swiftc default to apple-a12, so no `-mcpu` flag: inline LSE atomics, CRC32, RCPC, FP16. Script-built native libraries no longer build for apple-a7: FFmpeg, Opus, libwebp, libjpeg-turbo, TDLib (iOS arms, plus the x86_64 simulator arm of libwebp), dav1d (new generated device crossfile at 26.0 and the simulator crossfile; the submodule's own device crossfile stays unused) and libvpx (`--extra-cflags` min-version for the device, `--target=arm64-apple-ios26.0-simulator` for cflags and ldflags on the simulator because the simulator patch's own ios7.0 target overrides a min-version flag) take 26.0; the macOS arms stay at 14.0. Rust (wallet-engine, mtproto-engine, tlottie) gets `-Ctarget-cpu=apple-a12` through `extra_rustc_flags` (target configuration only, not host tools). Deprecations: `-Wno-error=deprecated-declarations` for C-family code and `-Wwarning DeprecatedDeclaration` through `--@build_bazel_rules_swift//swift:copt` in `.bazelrc` (the native `--swiftcopt` no longer exists in Bazel 8 and 9; rules_swift 4.2.2 appends this copt after the target's flags, so the order works), so APIs deprecated before iOS 26 do not fail the many `-warnings-as-errors` modules; the Swift flag only works after `-warnings-as-errors`, which the first build must confirm. Outline atomics (SHARED-APPLE-CPU-1) is resolved by the floor; the withdrawn config stays withdrawn. The simulator CPU default is not changed. Watch app unchanged (already 26.0). Tests: `tools/dev/tests/test_min_os.py` (all BUILD literals and script versions equal Telegram/BUILD, Mac arms stay at 14.0), `test_outline_atomics.py` (floor gives inline `ldadd`, no apple-a13 features). The build must verify: `otool -l` LC_BUILD_VERSION minos 26.0 on the app, a framework and the extensions; aquery or action log showing `apple-a12` for one clang, one swiftc and one rustc action; `otool -tV | grep -c ldadd` versus calls to `___aarch64_*`; the swiftc command line order of `-warnings-as-errors` and `-Wwarning`; the deprecation warning count; the Mac parity gate and `test_ffmpeg_flags_parity.py`; dav1d and libvpx outputs `vtool -show-build` minos 26.0 (also the simulator arm of libvpx).
- **iOS 26.0 is the minimum (owner decision 2026-11-14; not built, syntax-only checks)** (stream 4, own-code fallback cleanup). `Telegram/BUILD` `minimum_os_version` 15.0 -> 26.0 (the app, every extension and the other targets share the variable; the watch app was already 26.0). Supported devices: iPhone 11 / A13 and newer, iPad 8th generation / A12 and newer. Removed the iOS < 26 availability gates in Nanogram-owned code only: `GlanceCoordinator` (4), the WidgetKit `Glance*` files, `NanogramAppIntents`, `NanogramGlance/GlanceActivity`, `LocalAudioTranscription` (only the 26.0 analyzer gate Nanogram added), `Camera` policy files (tablet geometry 26.0 and 17.0 camera temperature, multitasking 16.0, Bluetooth high-quality audio 26.0 together with its `canImport(AlarmKit)` guard). Review fix: the upstream checks in `SharedWakeupManager` (2 guards), `Camera.swift` (protocol attribute) and `LocalAudioTranscription` (13.0 and 16.0 blocks) were put back, they cost nothing at run time and would conflict at every sync. Upstream files keep their `#available` checks on purpose (merge conflicts at every release); macOS gates were not touched (none of these files has one). Hardware-dependent gates stay at run time (AV1 hardware decode on A17 Pro and newer, Apple Intelligence). The pre-26 icon fallback comment in `Telegram/BUILD` stays (actool output unchanged).

- **Fork pins moved to the repaired heads** (2026-11-14, not built; syntax-only compiles): twidget d7527b40 (merged Apple layer and litehtml compile for iOS and macOS) and webrtc c48d71d7 (jni_zero include fix; carried files checked); shared tooling 6e68c485 records both. Same pins on all three apps; the 'twidget shim/Cocoa' files can be deleted in a later change.
- **Review fixes for the A/B guide and the watch widget docs** (2026-11-14, not built; tests run in the combined build). The performance report now labels each experiment with its state at launch (`ExperimentalPerformanceFlags.launchState`), not a switch flipped since, so sending the report after flipping no longer mislabels run A (test `testLinesReportTheStateAtLaunchNotTheSwitchFlippedSince`). Guide: report path, accurate privacy sentence, tap gap under 0.4 s, step reference, scrub test needs a self-sent video and n>0 samples. `OWNER-STEPS.md`: `tg testflight --notes <file>` (no `--watch`), watch checks need the TestFlight build. Two stale comments in the watch app fixed.

- **Apple Watch unread widget ships (IOS-WATCH-WIDGET-1)** (2026-11-14, not built or run by its author; tests run in the combined build). `WatchWidgetExtension` is now in `project.yml` and the committed `tgwatch.xcodeproj`, embedded as `PlugIns/WatchWidgetExtension.appex` in the watch app (`org.klaipher.Nanogram.watchkitapp.widget`); `prebuilt_watchos_compile.sh` builds it and fails if it is missing, the Bazel rule passes the watch id as `TG_WATCH_BUNDLE_ID` so the widget id follows it. `prebuilt_watchos_patch.sh` patches the versions of both, signs the extension with its own profile and the app with its profile (the entitlements, with the App Group, come from the profiles); no profile means an ad-hoc signature with `WatchWidget/*.entitlements` (the filegroup `//Telegram/WatchApp:entitlements`), which the App Store export keeps. A WatchApp profile without a `WatchWidget` one, or a profile without the group, fails with "run `python3 tools/dev/device_signing.py profiles`" (`tg`, `Make.py` and the patch script all check). `device_signing.py profiles` adds the `WatchApp` and `WatchWidget` targets with the group `group.org.klaipher.Nanogram.watch` (Xcode registers the group and assigns it); `Make.py` gains `--watchWidgetProvisioningProfile` and `BuildConfiguration` the `.watchkitapp.widget` -> `WatchWidget` mapping. `widgetSnapshot` and `backgroundRefresh` now default to on (`WatchFeature.shippedOn`): the widget shows total unread and the top two chats on accessory/Smart Stack families, and the app refreshes the summary in the background about every 30 minutes. New and changed Python tests in `tools/dev/tests/test_device_signing.py` (29 pass); Swift tests updated (`CoreWidgetSnapshotTests`, `CorePushAndRefreshTests`, `CoreAudioRoutePolicyTests`). Owner steps are now only the checks in `WatchWidget/OWNER-STEPS.md`.

- **Upstream sync base is release-13.2**: `tools/dev/upstream-sync/upstream-sync.json` still named release-13.1.2 (`0650eac5`) after the 13.2 merge, so the next dry run would have diffed against the wrong release. The base is now `5083532f` (Import upstream Telegram-iOS release-13.2), whose tree equals upstream's release-13.2 tree (`3a85c156`, checked against GitHub). Upstream status on 2026-11-14: iOS release-13.2 is the newest release; Android 13.2.2 (7303) is the newest; macOS upstream master is still 13.2.0 (17 unreleased commits after our merge, taken at the next release).
- **A/B test of the three dark performance experiments on iPhone 14 and 14 Pro** (iOS-QOS-1, IOS-SIMD-1, IOS-VIDEO-1; not built or run by its author, tests run in the combined build). Debug Settings (Settings tab icon tapped 10 times, present in TestFlight builds) gets a PERFORMANCE EXPERIMENTS section: one switch per experiment (`nanogram.experiment.<name>` in UserDefaults, default off, applies after force-quit and reopen; a toast says so), the device name and chip (`performanceExperimentsDeviceChip`, iPhone 13 to 15 non-Pro/Pro identifiers, others show the identifier) and the measured numbers. New `PerformanceExperimentStats` (SwiftSignalKit and SwiftSignalKit2, durations only): `threadPoolQueueWait` (ThreadPoolTask creation to start), `av1FrameDecode` (software AV1 frame decode wait in `FFMpegMediaVideoFrameDecoder`, set by the three software open sites; not for hardware decoders), `scrubPreviewFrame` (`MediaPlayerFramePreview` request to image). "Send Logs" appends a "Performance experiments (device <identifier>)" block with each switch state and `n`, average and max per metric as additional info; toggles are also written to the log (tag `PerfExperiments`). Entry ids 160 to 164, section after PERFORMANCE. Tests: `PerformanceExperimentsRowsTests`, `PerformanceExperimentStatsTests`. Tester guide `docs/testing/a15-a16-performance-experiments.md` (device check, opening the debug menu, one script per experiment, A then B with relaunch, what to report, the owner's decision rule: at least 3 testers per chip, 15% lower average time, no worse smoothness or stutter, battery at most 1 point worse, no new "hot"); ready TestFlight What to Test text in `docs/testing/tf-what-to-test-a15-a16.txt`. Guide in short: 1) check Settings > General > About, 2) tap the Settings tab icon 10 times, 3) per switch run the script off, then on after a relaunch, 4) report smoothness 1 to 5, stutter, warm/hot, battery drop over 10 minutes and the Send Logs export. Open: testers need an AV1 test clip from the owner (Telegram does not show the codec); the AV1 thread cap is the performance-core count, which is 2 on A15 and A16.

- **Time Sensitive Notifications on**: both App IDs (`org.klaipher.Nanogram`, `.NotificationService`) have the capability (App Store Connect API: `USERNOTIFICATIONS_TIMESENSITIVE`), so `Telegram/BUILD` now grants `com.apple.developer.usernotifications.time-sensitive` to the app and the NotificationService extension (`time_sensitive_notifications_fragment`), and `tools/dev/device_signing.py` adds it to the generated signing project. The ringing call notification (already marked Time Sensitive in `IncomingCallPushPresentation`) now gets through Focus modes and the scheduled summary. App ID cleanup the same day: the unused `org.klaipher.TelegramDev` and `org.klaipher.Fastgram` App IDs were deleted (the Fastgram main ID stays: Apple keeps IDs once used by an App Store Connect record).
- **Ring cleanup is per call, not per caller** (2026-11-14, not built; tests run in the combined build). Review fixes to the entry below: (1) a missed-call push no longer removes every ring of that caller: each delivered ring's `call_id`/`call_ah` is probed with `phone.receivedCall` and only rings the server answers `.ended` for are removed (`IncomingCallNotificationRouting.calls`, `removeEndedIncomingCallRings`), so a late missed push of call 1 cannot take down the ring of a call 2 that just came in; (2) the service-message cleanup removes by the `callId` of the fetched `phoneCall` action (`phoneCallServiceMessageCallId`) instead of by caller; (3) the ring watch's after-delivery closure no longer captures the handler, so the handler's deinit (`shouldKeepConnection = false`) runs before the watch sets it to true and the connection is not paused under the first probe; (4) an expiry reported by `performExpiringActivity` before the watch exists is applied right after `begin`, so `watch end expired` is logged; (5) doc comment of `makeIncomingCallNotificationContent` is back on the function. Rings without call keys are left to the 90 s sweep. Tests: `IncomingCallNotificationRoutingTests.testCallsListsEachRingOfTheCallerWithItsOwnCallKeys`, `IncomingCallPushPresentationTests`.
- **Incoming call ring stops when the call ends elsewhere** (2026-11-14, not built or run by its author; tests run in the combined build). With the app killed, an incoming 1:1 call rings as the extension's notification (no filtering entitlement, so no CallKit). Declining or answering on another device sent this phone no push at all (`updatePhoneCall(phoneCallDiscarded)` is a live update only), so it kept ringing until the user opened the app. Now: (1) after the ring is delivered, the extension stays alive for the ringtone's 30 s and asks the server every 2 s (`phone.receivedCall`, `IncomingCallRingWatch`, `_internal_probeIncomingCall`); a 400 (`CALL_ALREADY_DECLINED`, `CALL_PEER_INVALID`) removes the ring by its `call_id` (any other failure, timeout or flood wait leaves the ring); (2) a missed-call push (`PHONE_CALL_MISSED`, `VIDEO_CALL_MISSED`, `CONF_CALL_MISSED`) removes that caller's rings at once; (3) a call the acknowledgement already reports as over is not rung (passive copy, no sound, no Answer/Decline, taken down after delivery like a quiet placeholder); (4) a repeated push of a call that already rings (same `call_id`) is shown quietly, never ringing twice; the CallKit branch never starts a watch, so nothing changes when the filtering entitlement arrives. The 90 s sweep and the app's own cleanup stay as the fallback. No Time Sensitive entitlement change. Diagnostics, always on in the short (critlog) log of `logs/notification-logs`, ids only: `nse call <id> push|ack|ring delivered|quiet|probe #n|ring removed by <watch|missed-push|read-history|service-message|stale>|watch end <ended|window|expired>` and `nse call - push after ring loc=<key> +<s> since ring` for every push arriving while a ring of that caller shows. "Send Critical Logs" now also attaches the extension's critlog files (`nse-critlog-*`): "Send Notification Logs" and "Send All Logs" never included them (only `log-*` files). Files: `submodules/TelegramCore/.../Calls/ProbeIncomingCall.swift` (new; `AcknowledgeIncomingCall.swift` untouched), `NotificationsPresentationData` (`IncomingCallNotifications.endsRing/pushAction`, `IncomingCallNotificationRouting.identifiers(callId:)`), `Telegram/NotificationService/Sources/IncomingCallRingWatch.swift` (new), `IncomingCallPushPresentation.swift` (`ringDecision`, `quieted`), `ServicePushPresentation.swift`, `NotificationService.swift`, `DebugController.swift`. Tests: `ProbeIncomingCallTests`, `IncomingCallRingWatchTests`, `IncomingCallPushPresentationTests`, `IncomingCallNotificationsTests`. Open points the test below answers: what the server pushes after a decline or answer elsewhere (possibly nothing), what `receivedCall` returns after an answer elsewhere, how long iOS keeps the extension alive after delivery (the watch logs `watch end expired` when it is cut short), and whether removing a delivered notification stops its ringtone. If it does not, the next step is replacing the ring with a silent copy under the same identifier.

  **Owner test (one build, one call each time):**
  1. On the phone: swipe Nanogram out of the app switcher, lock the phone. Phone and Mac signed in to the same account; call it from a second account.
  2. Decline on the Mac. Expected: the phone's ring stops and its notification disappears within about 2-4 s. Note whether the sound stopped when the banner went.
  3. Repeat and answer on the Mac, then hang up. Same expectation.
  4. Repeat; the caller hangs up before anyone answers. Expected: ring gone at once, then the missed-call banner.
  5. Repeat; let it ring out. Expected: sound ends by 30 s, banner gone with the missed-call push (or at the next sweep at 90 s).
  6. Phone: Settings, Send Report. Open `critical-logs/nse-critlog-*` in the zip (its README lists every part): every call has `nse call <id>` lines (`push`, `ack`, `ring delivered`, `probe #n ... ended(rpc 400 ...)`, `ring removed by watch`, `watch end`). A `watch end expired` line means iOS cut the extension short; `push after ring` lines show what the server sent after each ending.

- **widget-sandbox 80818b2c -> 7dfed64b** (not built or run by its author; on iOS this bump changes only the corpus and test data, WidgetRuntime does not enforce the new 1024 nesting cap): widget-sandbox 7dfed64b (fork `nanogram`, merges Telegram v0.6.5: the v0.6.4 QuickJS security release, the v0.6.5 1024 nesting-depth cap and loop-based deep-DOM walks in the validator and engine, new bundles; 6 new attack pages: deep-nesting, paint-backdrop-replay, paint-filter-depth and three render-* pages, so the corpus has 133 attack pages). iOS renders widgets with its own WidgetRuntime (C++ DOM, 50,000-node cap, parser maxDepth 256) and takes only the corpus, fixtures, profile.json, PluralRules and the Intl part of SandboxEngine.swift, so the cap and the loop-based walks do not reach iOS. `WidgetCorpusTests` now expects 133 attack pages and `InstanceTests.testAttackCorpusRequestsNothing` 133 (it said 113, stale before this). QuickJS does not move (Telegram's pin b526a817 is inside our 7613b6b7). `shared/` = nanogram-deps 3ada4356. Push order: klaipher/quickjs `nanogram` 7613b6b7 first (it is not on GitHub yet; without it a recursive submodule update fails, `ng setup`/`--depth 1` included), then klaipher/widget-sandbox `nanogram` 7dfed64b, then nanogram-deps `main` 3ada4356, then the app repositories. The build must watch `deep-nesting`, `paint-filter-depth` and `paint-backdrop-replay` in the InstanceTests attack scan for stack depth and time (no iOS cap stops a 1,100-level script-built nesting page or a 600-deep filter page).

- **Widget painter: at most 16 nested filters** (not built or run by its author; `LayerTreePainterTests` run in the combined build): widget-sandbox v0.6.5 `paint-filter-depth` has every client draw at most 16 nested filters (2,000 nested layers overflowed a stack on Android). iOS's `LayerTreePainter` had no limit: each nested `filter` made a bitmap inside the one around it, up to the 256-level litehtml depth. A filter past 16 open levels now draws its content unfiltered (`canNestFilter`, `kMaxNestedFilters`, as Android's `MAX_NESTED_FILTERS`). The nesting-depth cap (1024) needs no change: WidgetRuntime's render already cuts elements past 256 levels (`depth` limit), and its DOM walks do not recurse. Tests: `testSixteenNestedFiltersAreAllowedAndNoMore`, `testNestedFiltersPastTheCapDrawUnfiltered`. A backdrop's source replay inherits the open filter count, so the outer and replayed bitmaps together stay within 16 (test `testBackdropInsideSixteenFiltersReplaysWithoutFurtherFilters`, unrun). Still missing on iOS (needs the twidget/widget-sandbox fork, not changed here): the engine's 16 heavy-box paint limit (`Paint limit exceeded`).
- **Removed three vendored libraries nothing built** (2026-11-14, not built or run by its author; no behaviour change): `third-party/libjxl` (1,579 files; its only reference was a commented-out dep in `submodules/MozjpegBinding/BUILD`, and `USE_JPEGXL` is never defined, so the JPEG XL functions were already stubs), `third-party/XMLCoder` (52 files) and `third-party/Swift2D` (7 files). Checked first in the iOS tree, the Mac repo (`Package.swift`, `project.pbxproj`, `core-xprojects`, `docs/mac-core-paths.txt`, `tools`, `scripts`: no hit) and the Android tree (only a prose mention in `docs/shared-libraries-plan.md`). Also removed: the libjxl script from `test_outline_atomics.py`, the commented dep, libjxl mentions in `docs/build-and-test.md` and `docs/bazel-build-profiling.md`. `upstream-sync.json` gains `removed_libraries` (and `sync.py` reports files a release changes there) so the next upstream sync does not bring them back silently. Spec: `docs/research/vendored-unlinked.md` (Round J).
- **tgcalls pin 73cc1f8d -> 7afc0b25** (fork `nanogram-shared`): ports upstream defea4a, so a group-call incoming video description with an empty ssrc group no longer reads `ssrcs[0]` of an empty vector (possible crash). `shared/` moves to nanogram-deps a17304db. The Mac and Android pins move to the same commit.

- Shared: `third-party/nanogram-rust/Cargo.lock` is seeded from the merged locks with no downgrades against any of the three source locks (mtproto-engine da4b2170, wallet-engine 44ae85c7, tlottie 92df98dc). Deltas against mtproto-engine's own lock, which iOS keeps using: flate2 1.1.10 to 1.1.11 and crc32fast 1.5.2 to 1.5.5 (both newer, taken from the merged lock); bitflags 2.13.1 to 2.13.2, cc 1.4.2 to 1.4.7, cfg-if 1.0.4 to 1.0.5, find-msvc-tools 0.1.10 to 0.1.13, log 0.4.33 to 0.4.34, rustix 1.1.4 to 1.1.5 and unicode-ident 1.0.24 to 1.0.26 are raised to mtproto-engine's versions (the first draft had them lower, a downgrade for mtproto-core and mio). Checked with `cargo check --locked --release` in scratch. Also: the stale MTProtoEngineFFI.xcframework mentions are gone from `submodules/MTProtoRustEngine` (BUILD comment, .gitignore), and the `native-libs.json` `rust_lto` text names the one nanogram-rust staticlib.
- Shared: new crate `third-party/nanogram-rust` (staticlib of tlottie, wallet-engine and mtproto-ffi with fat LTO, merged `Cargo.lock`) for the Mac's single Rust archive; `submodules/MTProtoRustEngine/Package.swift` takes the FFI module from the Mac's `packages/RustLibraries` instead of its own xcframework (Mac only; iOS builds it with Bazel). `tools/dev/native/module-settings.json` and `module_settings.py` describe the Mac Rust LTO that way. wallet-engine keeps `-Copt-level=3`. iOS unchanged; whether Make.py's `lto=fat` reaches the `rust_static_library` targets is still to be checked with `bazel aquery`. Not built.
- Shared: the Mac now builds `submodules/OpenSSLEncryptionProvider` against the shared BoringSSL, so its Swift package defines `TELEGRAM_USE_BORINGSSL` like the iOS BUILD target (BoringSSL has no `BN_set_flags`/`BN_FLG_CONSTTIME`). `tools/dev/native/native-libs.json` and `docs/mac-core-paths.txt` (new `lib boringssl` line) match the Mac repository. No iOS behaviour change. Not built.
Release optimization audit (branch `build/release-opt`; not built or run by its author). Verification is in `docs/bazel-build-profiling.md`.

- **IOS-OPT-1** `third-party/wallet-engine/BUILD`: `_RUST_RELEASE_FLAGS` `-Copt-level=z` to `-Copt-level=3` (kept `-Ccodegen-units=1`, `-Cpanic=abort`; fat LTO comes from `Make.py`). Upstream's `z` ran PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA512, ed25519 and sha2 size-first. Keep only if mnemonic-to-key time improves and the size growth is tens of KB. The list has no select(), so it also applies to Debug builds of the first-party wallet crates (was z, now 3 with codegen-units=1) and to the bindgen cdylib; `bazel aquery` shows both `--codegen=opt-level=3` and `-Copt-level=3`; the crate_universe dependencies get neither.
- **IOS-OPT-2** `Telegram/prebuilt_watchos_compile.sh`: `GCC_OPTIMIZATION_LEVEL=3` on the xcodebuild line. The watch app's C packages (COpus, COgg, COpusHelpers, WebPCore; Lottie is the prebuilt Rust TLottieKit, not reached) were built `-Os` by Xcode's SwiftPM defaults in Release; command-line settings reach package targets. New opt-in `TG_WATCH_KEEP_DD=1` keeps the script's DerivedData so the `*-common-args.resp` files can be checked (it is normally deleted on exit). `-ffast-math` stays off, upstream Package.swift files untouched.
- **IOS-DOC-1** `tools/dev/native/module_settings.py`, `native-libs.json`, regenerated `docs/module-build-settings.md` and `module-settings.json`: removed the stale "iOS has no order file" claim (iOS uses order files for MtProtoKit, SwiftSignalKit, Postbox, TelegramCore, TelegramUI); the Rust LTO note and table row now say Mac Rust is fat LTO too (MAC-OPT-1..3, which landed on the Mac `build/release-opt` branch). `module_settings.py` and `native-libs.json` are byte-identical to the Mac copies (the Mac side is MAC-DOC-2); `check_native_libs.py --sibling` passes in both directions. Merge both branches together. The regeneration also refreshed the module counts (903 iOS Swift modules, 44 in Swift 6 mode).

Build-system modernization (branch `build/modernize`; not built or run by its author; the checks to run are in `docs/bazel-build-profiling.md`). No change to what the app does.

- **Native-library genrules** (`third-party/{dav1d,libvpx,opus,webp,libjxl,td,libjpeg-turbo}/BUILD`, `submodules/ffmpeg/BUILD`). Every architecture table now ends in `exit 1` for an unknown `$(TARGET_CPU)` (dav1d, libvpx, opus, webp, libjxl, td and ffmpeg only echoed it and went on with an empty arch). Each genrule deletes its scratch tree after the outputs are copied (`rm -rf "$BUILD_DIR"`, ffmpeg also its `FFMpegSource` copy): under `--strategy=Genrule=standalone` nothing else removed the source copies, CMake/meson trees and object files from `bazel-bin`. Codec sources are therefore no longer visible to lldb from there. The `SOURCE_PATH` lines are untouched (the shared-codebase migration owns them).
- **Pinned tools extracted once** (`MODULE.bazel`: `@cmake`, `@ninja`, `@meson` as `http_archive` with `build_file_content`; `third-party/{dav1d,webp,libjpeg-turbo,libjxl,td,yasm,flatc}/BUILD`). CMake 4.1.2 to 4.5.0, Ninja 1.12.1 to 1.13.2, Meson kept at 1.6.0 (1.12 needs Python 3.10+, the genrule uses the system Python 3.9) (sha256 of each archive checked against the upstream release). The CMake archive is trimmed to the CLI plus `share/cmake-4.5` without `Help` (about 1.8k files instead of 9.3k per action). The per-action `mkdir/tar -xzf/unzip` blocks and the hard-coded `cmake-4.1.2-macos-universal` paths are gone; the tool directories (absolute, the build scripts `cd`) are prepended to `PATH`, dav1d's `EXTRA_PATH` was appended after `$PATH` before (Linux dav1d keeps the host tools, the pinned ninja is a macOS binary). yasm no longer prefers a host `cmake`. The libjpeg-turbo and flatbuffers `http_file` entries are unchanged.
- **Ninja generator** for the CMake-built libs (`build-{webp,libjpeg-turbo,libjxl,td}-bazel.sh`, td's generator pass and tde2e pass, yasm, flatc): `-G Ninja`, `cmake --build . --parallel N [--target ...]` instead of `make -jN`. `tools/dev/tests/test_net_crypto_builds.py` follows the new td commands.
- **Xcode/SDK identity in action keys** (`build-system/Make/BuildEnvironment.py`, `Make.py`): `--action_env=NANOGRAM_XCODE_ID=...` from `xcodebuild -version` and `xcrun --sdk iphoneos|iphonesimulator --show-sdk-build-version` of the `xcode-select` Xcode (DEVELOPER_DIR ignored, as the genrules do). Genrules get only `--action_env` as environment, so an Xcode update could serve stale ffmpeg/libvpx/dav1d/td from the disk cache. Empty when the tools fail (then nothing is added). Tests: `build-system/Make/tests/test_xcode_identity.py`.
- **ThinLTO linker cache for release links** (`Make.py` `common_release_args`): `-Wl,-cache_path_lto,~/.cache/telegram-ios/lto-cache`, `-prune_after_lto 1209600`, `-max_relative_cache_size_lto 5`. Self-pruning; documented in `docs/cache-cleanup.md`. The path is the user's home, so ObjcLink action keys differ between machines that share a remote cache.
- **rules_rust pipelined compilation** (`.bazelrc`): `--@rules_rust//rust/settings:pipelined_compilation=true`. To be measured on a cold build; revert if it does not help.
- **Bazel 9.3.2** (`.bazelversion`, `versions.json` with the official darwin-arm64 sha256, doc and BUILD comment paths), apple_support 2.12.0, rules_cc 0.2.28, bazel_features 1.55.0. `MODULE.bazel.lock` was not regenerated here (no Bazel runs in this change): run `bazel mod deps --lockfile_mode=update` once and commit it before any `--lockfile_mode=error` (`--lock`) build.
- **Build profiling**: `--experimental_profile_include_target_label` and `--experimental_profile_include_primary_output` in `.bazelrc`; `tg profile [--deep] [--keep N]` copies and summarises the last profile (`docs/bazel-build-profiling.md`, `build-output/profile/` is git-ignored). `tg build` honours `TG_BAZEL_ARGS`.
- **SHARED-APPLE-CPU-1: outline atomics blocked, config withdrawn** (`docs/performance/outline-atomics.md`). `-moutline-atomics` for arm64-apple-ios makes clang call `___aarch64_ldadd4_acq_rel` and friends, but Xcode's `libclang_rt.ios.a` defines them as `__aarch64_...` (one underscore fewer), so the device link fails with undefined symbols. Removed the `outline_atomics` `.bazelrc` config and the `NANOGRAM_OUTLINE_ATOMICS` branches of the six native-library scripts (the simulator arm64 branches were no-ops that only invalidated genrule caches). Unblock options: a forwarding `.S` shim proven by a full `tg build --device`, or `-mcpu=apple-a11/a12` after an iOS floor raise. Tests: `tools/dev/tests/test_outline_atomics.py` (config stays withdrawn; optional xcrun link probe documents the failure).

Modernization stream ios-media-ui (specs from docs/specs modernization review; status lines for the iOS spec doc are owned by the ios-platform stream). No builds or tests were run; commands are in the stream report.

- **IOS-CI-1** (329b25e1): one process-wide `CIContext` (`SharedStickerCIContext`, same `options: nil` as before, so colour is unchanged) for sticker cutout (two sites), cutout outline and sticker generation. Status: done. Test: `ImageObjectSeparationTests`.
- **IOS-IO-1** (80d78bbb): `readMediaFileData(path:complete:)` maps the file (`.mappedIfSafe`) only where the call site knows the resource is complete; 17 of the 31 `options: []` reads in `PhotoResources.swift` use it. Reads of possibly partial files (`size`/`availableSize` guards only) keep the copying read. Status: done for the complete-guarded sites. Test: `PhotoResourcesTests`.
- **iOS-CONC-2** (b8a81c3a): wallpaper `BlurView` hops to main with `async` and the unused `sync(on:)` helper is gone. TGCache/PGVideoMovie left as is: the verdict says TGCache has no Swift callers and needs its synchronous return. Status: done (BlurView only).
- **iOS-METAL-STICKERS-1** (b1c0f456): `AnimationCompressor.SharedContext.renderCommandQueue`, one queue per device shared by all `CompressedImageRenderer`s; the DCT compression queue stays separate so compute jobs do not queue ahead of drawing. Status: done. Test: `AnimationCompressionTests`.
- **iOS-STICKERS-1** (f9d6f041): `AnimatedStickerQueuePool`, 3 serial `.userInitiated` queues assigned round-robin to nodes created while Debug Settings > "Stickers: parallel queues" (`ExperimentalUISettings.stickerParallelQueues`) is on; default off keeps the single shared queue. `AnimatedStickerBackgroundGate` parked timers are now lock-protected. Status: done behind flag, default off. Not done: parsing the lottie instance off the pool for invisible nodes. Test: `AnimatedStickerQueuePoolTests`.
- **iOS-STICKERS-2** (bff3527a): `AnimatedStickerStatusPublisher` skips the Promise set while nobody observes `status`, remembers the latest value and hands it to the first subscriber. Status: Promise skip done; per-tick coalescing of main hops not done (verdict: speculative). Test: `AnimatedStickerStatusPublisherTests`.
- **iOS-GIFGRID-1** (6ddcdbb2): `BatchVideoRenderingContext` with Debug Settings > "GIF grid: parallel decode" (`gifGridParallelDecode`) on decodes one pass per tile on a pool of 2-4 serial queues (no global `isRendering` barrier); default off keeps the single queue. Status: done behind flag, default off. Not done: separate reader-open step. Test: `BatchVideoRenderingTests`.
- **Review follow-ups (ios-media-ui)**: iOS-STICKERS-2 status publisher now uses a main-queue subscriber bag, so a new subscriber gets only the current status once (no stale Promise replay, no duplicate flush); iOS-METAL-STICKERS-1 shared render queue created with `maxCommandBufferCount: 256`; iOS-GIFGRID-1 parallel mode skips tiles whose decoder failed; iOS-CI-1 shared `CIContext` has `cacheIntermediates: false` and clears caches on memory warning.

Modernization stream ios-platform (branch `mod/ios-platform`, merge 47ba7f97; not built or run).

- **Dead deprecated branches** (IOS-DEPR-1). Removed the `UIWebView` fallback (and its unused URL protocol) from the document gallery and the `UILocalNotification` call-ring/cancel fallbacks from `SharedNotificationManager`; both sat behind always-true checks (minimum iOS 15). The unbuilt legacy `Telegram/Watch` extension (replaced by `Telegram/WatchApp`) is deleted (nothing referenced it).
- **Live location while stationary** (IOS-LOC-1, experiment, default off). `DeviceLocationManager` can run live location alone (Always access, no compass) on `CLLocationUpdate.liveUpdates(.otherNavigation)` with a `CLBackgroundActivitySession` (and `CLServiceSession` on iOS 18), which stops delivering fixes while the device is stationary so GNSS can rest; the 10 m distance filter is re-applied in code. A screen needing the heading, and iOS 15-16, keep `CLLocationManager`. Enabled by the launch argument/default `NanogramStationaryLocation`. The send rate is unchanged (10 m filter); accuracy while moving is chosen by the `liveUpdates(.otherNavigation)` configuration instead of `desiredAccuracy`, so energy while moving must be measured against the default before this can default on. The update source is injectable and `LiveLocationUpdates` stops itself on release. Tests: `//submodules/DeviceLocationManager:DeviceLocationManagerTests`.
- **Microphone permission** (IOS-DEPR-3). `DeviceAccess` (new `MicrophonePermission`) and the video-message capture controller use `AVAudioApplication.shared.recordPermission` / `requestRecordPermission` on iOS 17+ and keep the `AVAudioSession` calls for iOS 15-16; two deprecation warnings fewer.
- **Voice transcription** (IOS-SPEECH-1). `LocalAudioTranscription` reads the converted m4a in place instead of copying it into `NSTemporaryDirectory` (the copy was never deleted), and an empty transcription gets confidence 0 instead of NaN (`averageTranscriptionConfidence`). New iOS 26 path through `SpeechTranscriber`/`SpeechAnalyzer.analyzeSequence(from:)` with `AssetInventory` model download in the background (missing model or locale falls back to SFSpeechRecognizer on-device); off unless the launch argument/default `NanogramSpeechAnalyzer` is set (engine choice and the fallback are pure functions with tests). Still behind the experimental `localTranscription` switch. Tests: `//submodules/Media/LocalAudioTranscription:LocalAudioTranscriptionTests`.

Modernization stream watch (branch `mod/watch`, merge bfb42051; not built or run).

- **Watch app modernization** (branch mod/watch, specs below; owner switches default off; nothing built or run, tests written first; commits fea98118, f4907aa7, 4e1378e3, 174ccff4, 241c7c84, 767fd10e, 9c87b80a, 5418dd88, 9bcf26e8, 545f5b9f, 6c30fd9d). Switch = `UserDefaults` bool `nanogram.watch.<name>` (`WatchFeature`); launch argument `-nanogram.watch.<name> YES` turns one on.
  - **IOS-WATCH-AUDIO-1 + WATCH-AUDIO-1** (`longFormAudio`): `AVPlayerBackend` sets `.playback`/`.default` with `RouteSharingPolicy.longFormAudio` and activates with `activate(options:)` (route picker); a `DeferredPlayGate` holds `play()` until activation ends and a stop cancels it. `MPNowPlayingInfoCenter` and `MPRemoteCommandCenter` (play, pause, toggle, skip 15 s) are registered while a track plays and removed at the end. `WKBackgroundModes: [audio]` added in project.yml (static). Scope correction from the verdict: this controller handles music files only; voice notes use `VoicePlaybackController`.
  - **IOS-WATCH-ATS-1**: `NSAppTransportSecurity` removed from project.yml and Info.plist; a test fails if `URLSession`, `AsyncImage` or `WKWebView` appear in the app sources.
  - **WATCH-MEM-1** (`chunkedVoiceDecode`): `OpusChunkedDecoder` (page pump over the mapped file, `opus_decode_float` straight into the destination `AVAudioPCMBuffer`, pre-skip trimmed from the first packets, duration from the last granule position). `VoicePlaybackController` plays a 4800-frame first chunk, then 48000-frame chunks with 3 buffers in flight; `AVEngineBackend` schedules successive buffers (generation counter drops callbacks of a stopped stream). A mid-note decode error ends the note where it is. Package tests compare chunked output sample for sample with `decodePCM`.
  - **WATCH-GPU-1** (`offMainStickerRender`): `LottieFrameRenderer` actor, one render at a time, a newer due frame replaces a waiting one, generation token drops results after tear-down; Low Power Mode 24 Hz. The double copy named in the audit is already gone with tlottie (it renders into the CFData behind the CGImage).
  - **WATCH-LIST-1** (`messageWindowTrim`, `fileProgressThrottle`): `MessageWindow.trim` (never drops a pending or failed send; resets `reachesChatTail`/`hasOlder`), called after `loadOlder`/`loadNewer` with a 120-message limit; `FileUpdateGate` passes a progress-only update once per 5% step (completion, path, active flag and sizes always pass); sorted array cached per `MessageWindow.revision`. Needs a device check of scroll preservation after trimming (plain VStack, `proxy.scrollTo` handlers).
  - **WATCH-VIDEO-1** (`progressiveVideoStart`): BLOCKED, reverted to the full-download path. The loader was built on `AVAssetResourceLoader`, which does not exist on watchOS (typecheck on the watchOS SDK: 16 errors), so it broke the whole app target. `ProgressiveVideoLoader`, `AVDataRequestSink`, `ProgressiveVideoSession` and the player-view wiring are removed; `ProgressiveVideoPolicy` and `ByteRangeServing` (pure) stay with their tests, unused by any player. The flag exists but does nothing. A redesign (for example a loopback HTTP server) is open.
  - **Review fixes (watch)**: WATCH-MEM-1 use-after-free in `OpusChunkedDecoder.scan` (the Ogg stream was cleared before `pkt.packet` was read, so every voice note failed to decode with the flag on; now `defer`); `TDClient.registerDeviceCompletion` takes `os.Logger` (TDShim's deprecated `Logger` protocol made the name ambiguous: compile error in the default build); WATCH-LIST-1 `trim(droppingFrom: .newest)` stops at the first unsent message so the kept range stays contiguous (a failed send no longer leaves a gap that `loadNewer` cannot refill); `messageRows(presorted:)` skips the second sort and `FileUpdateGate.forget` is called in `cancelFileDownload`; WATCH-INT-1 `WatchAppDelegate.handle` completes snapshot tasks with `setTaskCompleted(restoredDefaultState:estimatedSnapshotExpiration:userInfo:)` (the delegate adaptor is installed with every flag off). Not fixed: scroll position after `.oldest` trim (device check before `messageWindowTrim` goes on); `WKBackgroundModes: audio` stays in the default Info.plist (accepted, see NANOGRAM-CHANGES).
  - **IOS-WATCH-WIDGET-1** (`widgetSnapshot`): WidgetKit extension (`WatchWidget/`, accessory circular/rectangular/inline/corner, `TimelineEntryRelevance` for the Smart Stack, names hidden under `.privacy` redaction), `Shared/WatchUnreadSnapshot` JSON in the App Group, `WidgetSnapshotPublisher` (3 s coalescing, one write per 15 s, same-content writes skipped), `ChatListStore.unreadInputs()`. Out of the default build: only `project-widget.yml` has the target. Owner steps in `WatchWidget/OWNER-STEPS.md`.
  - **WATCH-INT-1** (`backgroundRefresh`, `pushRegistration`): `BackgroundRefreshCoordinator` (request on inactive, at most one pending, 30 min preferred spacing, 10 s sync window, then summary written from the cache) and `WatchAppDelegate` (`WKApplicationDelegateAdaptor`: refresh task handler, APNs token callbacks, notification permission request). `RegisterDeviceQuery` is a hand-built `TdQuery` (TDShim is trimmed and its `run` is private). Not done: notification service extension, foreground presentation delegate, `processPushNotification`.
  - **WATCH-API-1**: `.handGestureShortcut(.primaryAction)` on record start, record stop, Send and both Replay buttons (one per state/screen); `.sensoryFeedback(.start/.stop)` from `RecordHaptic.forTransition`, `WKInterfaceDevice.play(.success)` on send. Voice-note bubbles get no shortcut (several per screen).
  - **WATCH-I18N-1** (`localizedTdlibParameters`): `TdlibSystemInfo` (language code of the first preferred language, `CFBundleShortVersionString`, `WKInterfaceDevice.systemVersion`). Server-visible.
  - **WATCH-SIMD-1**: `vDSP_rmsqv`, a lock-guarded index-based `SampleCarry`, one reused converter output buffer, meter updates at most every 50 ms; end-to-end sink test pins duration, waveform and decodability.
  - Test commands (not run): `tools/dev/tg watch test` (all `Core*`/`Media*` suites above in `Telegram/WatchApp/Tests/WatchAppTests`), `cd Telegram/WatchApp/Packages/OpusKit && swift test` (`OpusChunkedDecoderTests`). Open for the main session: run both, then one device build; per switch device checks are listed in `NANOGRAM-CHANGES.md`.

- **Review fixes: opus options in one shared file, Mac opus keeps NEON, watch libopus linked** (W2). `third-party/opus/opus-cmake-args.sh` holds the CMake option list (`OPUS_COMMON_ARGS`); `build-opus-bazel.sh` sources it (and the `opus_build` genrule lists it in `srcs`), and the Mac `core-xprojects/libopus` build sources it through `submodules/telegram-ios`, so the two apps cannot drift (the ffmpeg-common-flags.sh pattern). It adds `-DOPUS_PRESUME_NEON=ON`: the Mac build has no toolchain file, so `CMAKE_SYSTEM_PROCESSOR` is `arm64`, which opus's CMake does not treat as aarch64, and Darwin has no runtime CPU detection, so the NEON code was compiled but never called. iOS already gets aarch64 from its toolchain file; the option is ignored on x86. Floating point stays (owner-visible change: the Mac opus moved from fixed point to float with the shared source; NEON now presumed as in the old configure build). The `native-libs.json` flag probe reads the shared file on both sides. `opus` is in `moved_libraries` of the upstream-sync config (upstream still vendors `third-party/opus/opus-1.5.1.tar.gz`) and `docs/upstream-sync.md`; `docs/mac-core-paths.txt` gets `lib opus` and `share third-party/opus`. The watch app's `OpusKit/Sources/COpus/libopus` (312 byte-identical files) are now relative symlinks into `third-party/opus/opus`; only `config.h` stays hand-written. Not built (risk: SwiftPM following the symlinks for libopus, as it already does for ogg and libwebp).

- **Opus and libwebp build with CMake + Ninja; opus is one source with Android** (W2). `third-party/opus/opus` is a submodule of xiph/opus at v1.6.1 (22244de5, shallow, the commit Android pins); the vendored `opus-1.6.1.tar.gz` and its sha256 probe are gone. `third-party/opus/build-opus-bazel.sh` runs CMake (`-G Ninja`, CMake 4.1.2 and Ninja 1.12.1 from the `cmake_tar_gz` and `ninja-mac_zip` http_files, like dav1d and libwebp) instead of configure/make. Option mapping: `--disable-shared --enable-static` = `OPUS_BUILD_SHARED_LIBRARY=OFF`; `--disable-extra-programs` = `OPUS_BUILD_PROGRAMS=OFF` (tests off too); `--disable-doc` = no CMake target; `--enable-intrinsics` = `OPUS_DISABLE_INTRINSICS=OFF` (arm64 NEON intrinsics; CMake has no switch for the hand-written asm that `--disable-asm` turned off, and it is not built); floating point = `OPUS_FIXED_POINT=OFF`; DRED/OSCE/deep PLC stay off (`OPUS_DRED=OFF`, `OPUS_OSCE=OFF`); `--with-pic` = `CMAKE_POSITION_INDEPENDENT_CODE=ON`; `-O2 -g` kept through `CMAKE_C_FLAGS_RELEASE`. The copied source gets the tarball's `package_version` so the library still reports 1.6.1. Same headers and `libopus.a`, dependents unchanged. libwebp (already CMake) now uses Ninja instead of Unix Makefiles. Not built yet (no builds allowed); armv7 is no longer accepted by the opus script. Mac: `core-xprojects/libopus` builds from this submodule and takes the same options, so its opus is now floating point (was fixed-point). Commit: see git log.

- **BoringSSL is the klaipher/boringssl fork, one source with Android** (W2). `third-party/boringssl/src` (1135 vendored files at 2ea3200c, API version 43) is now a submodule of klaipher/boringssl `nanogram` 1c764cec: upstream 0.20261105.0 (3dc4b638, API version 45, 134 upstream commits later) minus the 24 upstream Bazel files that would make `src/` a Bazel package (root BUILD.bazel, MODULE.bazel, .bazelrc, .bazelignore, the ones under third_party/benchmark, third_party/googletest and util/bazel-example). `BUILD.generated.bzl` keeps loading `src/gen/sources.bzl` (same 12 list names, so no regeneration); `BUILD` flags are unchanged because upstream's util.bzl and CMake flags did not change between the two versions (C11/C++17, same warnings). API check of the consumers against the new headers: PKCS and OpenSSLEncryptionProvider use only symbols that still exist (the BoringSSL branches), td's tdutils/tdnet/tde2e use the same OpenSSL-compatible symbol set as before (SHA1/SHA256/SHA512 are now `static inline` in sha.h/sha2.h but are only called, never address-taken), tgcalls and webrtc already compile against this commit on Android. Added `boringssl` to `moved_libraries` of the upstream-sync config. Not built: needs the combined build (watch for -Werror on the new sources and for `ssl_deprecated.h`, which `ssl.h` now includes).

- **twidget tests: 11 byte-identical duplicates removed** (review fix): `tests/LiteHTMLTests/{BackgroundImageExclusion,SVGLayerExport,PixelSnap,ColorMatch,FormRendering,SourceFilterCache,PointerEventsHit,FontCache,CSSMath}Tests.mm` and `tests/DocumentTests/{DamageBand,CachePurge}Tests.swift` are compiled from the submodule's `twidget/apple/Tests` through `FORK_LITEHTML_TESTS` / `FORK_RENDERER_TESTS` in `third-party/twidget/BUILD` (checked byte-identical at twidget a67d018f). The 19 same-named tests that differ from the fork's (iOS expectations) stay and are listed in VENDOR.md as not yet upstreamed. Not built; run `//third-party/twidget:TwidgetRenderingTests`.

- **mac-core-paths.txt: QuickJS nested path** (review fix): Mac and Android read the QuickJS nested in widget-sandbox at `packages/ios/Sources/CQuickJS/quickjs` (the fork's real gitlink path), not `third_party/quickjs`; the stale "local pin b526a817" note is gone with the manifest change. The Mac copy follows with its core bump.

- **Pins follow the manifest** (review fix; `shared/` = klaipher/nanogram-deps f185ddca, adds tgcalls 73cc1f8d). The manifest named newer fork tips than the app pinned, and the tgcalls pin (73cc1f8d, bbf155fe) was ahead of the manifest (2a5daa0f), so `check_shared` failed on both. Moved: openh264 037fff69, rnnoise 05b8dae0, libprisma 581c910a, sqlcipher-amalgamation 010c1b11, quickjs 7613b6b7 (all records-only commits: CHANGELOG/NANOGRAM-CHANGES), widget-sandbox 80818b2c (nested QuickJS re-pinned to quickjs 7613b6b7, the same commit as our own QuickJS pin), twidget a67d018f (litehtml: host bidi levels, calc ceiling 16,384, plus records). `check_shared --side ios` reports no pin/manifest difference after this commit; its only remaining errors are "not on origin/nanogram" until the forks are pushed (checked).

- **.bazelignore covers the nested checkouts the recursive submodule init adds** (review fix): crc32c's `third_party/{benchmark,glog,googletest}` inside the webrtc fork and the QuickJS nested in the widget-sandbox fork (same commit as our `third-party/quickjs-ng/quickjs` pin, which is the one Bazel builds; bump both together, see `shared/shared-libraries.json`). SwiftPM consumers are unaffected.

- **upstream-sync reports changes to moved libraries** (review fix): `upstream-sync.json` gets `moved_libraries` (15 libraries moved to forks or official pins, with fork URL and branch or official tag); `sync.py` lists every file the release changed under those paths and names the fork to patch, so upstream fixes to FFmpeg, openh264, rnnoise, libprisma, sqlcipher, libyuv, quickjs, widget-sandbox, twidget, ogg, opusfile, webp, boost_regex, wamr and the webrtc third_party dirs are not lost silently. Tests in `tools/dev/tests/test_upstream_sync.py`; procedure in `docs/upstream-sync.md`.

- **Watch app: libogg and libwebp are no longer third copies** (review fix). `OpusKit` links `framing.c`, `bitwise.c`, `crctable.h` and `ogg.h` of `third-party/ogg/ogg` (same commit be05b13e, byte-identical files), `WebPKit` links its 133 libwebp files into `third-party/webp/libwebp` (v1.6.0, all byte-identical; only `src/webp/config.h` stays hand-written), through relative symlinks as `OpusBinding/SwiftPMLinks` does. libopus 1.6.1 was left vendored in `OpusKit/Sources/COpus` at that time (superseded by the next entry). Not built (no builds allowed); the watch target needs the `third-party/ogg/ogg` and `third-party/webp/libwebp` submodules checked out, as the app already requires.

- **native-libs.json takes the Mac copy** (review fix): the Mac copy describes the current layout (libwebp version from the iOS submodule's configure.ac, openh264 from `codec_ver.h`, flatc from `prepare-flat-serialization.py`, no Mac tarball probes); the iOS copy still probed Mac files that no longer exist. `check_native_libs.py --sibling` reports no difference now; the Mac core bump to this commit makes both repos hold the same blob.

- **Review fix: QuickJSHostTests compile against the fork's `JSInterruptHandler(JSContext*, void*)`.** `stopping_interrupt`, `always_interrupt` and `disposer_interrupt` still took `JSRuntime*`; the first now stops the runtime through `JS_GetRuntime(ctx)`. Checked with `clang -fsyntax-only` (no build run).
- **Review fix: rnnoise SwiftPM package excludes `src/x86`, `src/compile.sh` and `src/rnn_train.py`.** `sources: ["src"]` is recursive and compiled the x86 AVX2/SSE4.1 files on arm64 (the Mac links rnnoise only through this package). Bazel was not affected (its BUILD globs only `src/x86/*.h`).
- **Review fix: libprisma BUILD loads again.** `objc_library` has no `include_prefix` or `strip_include_prefix` (rules_cc 0.2.27), so the whole `//third-party/libprisma` package failed to load. Replaced by `includes = ["libprisma/apple"]`; the only consumer is Swift `import libprisma`, which needs no prefix.

- **tgcalls pin moved to 73cc1f8d** (fork `nanogram-shared`, fast-forward of 9bfb76f8): adds the Package.swift exclude of `ios/` (82cd77e) so the Mac SwiftPM target no longer compiles the iOS bridge, plus host-test and doc path fixes. No iOS source change; the Mac pins move to the same commit.
- **Manifest final; parity cleanup** (W6). `shared/` = klaipher/nanogram-deps 0e3f132f: every library is an official pin or one fork branch with a commit (quickjs f6fc68b9, twidget a4f5391c, widget-sandbox 1b21a48e, tgcalls 2a5daa0f, webrtc fa0b3c5f); no pending or transitional pin; Mac and Android consumer paths follow their stages (`check_shared.py --side android`: no error; `--side mac`: the Mac's own pins and `submodules/webrtc`; `--side ios --strict`: only BoringSSL, still vendored). `docs/mac-core-paths.txt`: the VP9Hardware `mirror` lines are gone (the Mac builds the iOS sources), `lib` paths follow the manifest, `share` lines cover every iOS path the Mac now compiles. `native-libs.json`: `known_differences.libyuv.version` removed, libyuv version `shared` 1977 read through the core. `tools/dev/tests/test_parity.py` asserts there is no mirror line. The W7 decisions are in `shared/ledger/ios-w6-w7.md`.

- **Dead names removed from live docs and comments** (W6): the old WebRTC fork and its branches, the fast-forward alias branch of klaipher/webrtc, the per-platform tgcalls branches and the tgcalls port script, and the old Lottie renderer (live docs, comments in AnimatedStickerNode, AnimationCompression, the debug controller, the watch app tests, `docs/fork-records.md` rows for the archived forks, the stale 'trial merge' note in `docs/subsystems/tgcalls.md`). Left on purpose: this changelog, the dated research and spec documents under `docs/research` and `docs/superpowers`, the identity-proof notes' own history, the key `forceRLottieBackend` of an old debug-settings payload (`DebugControllerEntriesTests`), and the Mac paths in `docs/mac-core-paths.txt` before they are replaced by the Mac stage.

- **Stale paths of the swapped copies fixed** (W6). `CoreTextRenderHostTests.mm` and `LayerTreePainterTests.mm` still included `third-party/twidget/LiteHTML/LHHostSupport.h` (they would not have compiled after the twidget swap); they now include `third-party/twidget/twidget/apple/Sources/LiteHTML/LHHostSupport.h` like the three sources. Comments, docs and the stress BUILD note that named `third-party/widget-sandbox/{stress,corpus,fixtures,profile,gesture}` follow the submodule layout (`third-party/widget-sandbox/widget-sandbox/...`). Not built.

- **SceneEffects: shared logic is already the iOS files; the Mac's one extra helper moves over** (W7). Compared with comments and whitespace stripped, the Mac's `DiamondAnimation`, `DiamondGeometry`, `GramDiamond.metal`, `LiquidGlassShapes.metal` and `LiquidGlassSurfaceReleasePolicy` equal the iOS files exactly (the Mac only dropped doc comments), so the Mac references the iOS ones. `LiquidGlassShapeSet` gains `init(parameters:center:)` (the Mac's recording blob and call status bar use it). Left per app by design: the UIKit/AppKit views, `DiamondRenderer` and `LiquidGlassShapesPipelines` bundle glue (`Bundle.module` vs the Bazel resource bundle; the iOS pipelines go through MetalEngine's pipeline cache and prewarm, the Mac's older copy does not), and the SceneKit star/coin/confetti views that exist only on the Mac. Mac call-site follow-up: `LiquidGlassShapesAppearance` is `LiquidGlassAppearance` in the iOS file. Not built.

- **QR Studio: the platform-neutral logic is one source set for iOS and the Mac** (W7). New `submodules/TelegramUI/Components/QrStudioLogic` (Bazel filegroup `:Sources`, compiled into QrStudioScreen's `srcs`; the Mac package compiles the same files through SwiftPM symlinks) holds Lens, CodePillLogic, ExportMotion, SheetMotion, CodePaths, ExportLayout, Motion, ServerLook, Color, LookSettings and the CoreGraphics ExportRenderer (git mv, history kept). Mac-only improvements merged in: `QrStudioCodeAction.constrained(toTemporary:)`; `QrStudioLens.closesAfterHold(holding:isPersistent:)` (default false, the iOS call site is unchanged); `qrStudioPhaseGroupCount` (PhasedAnimation.groupCount follows it); the export renderer's `Plane` (a sticker drawn on the card, `plane:` defaults to nil, so iOS output is unchanged). The renderer gets `QrStudioPlatformColor` (UIColor/NSColor), a per-platform colour space (iOS keeps device RGB, the Mac sRGB), `qrStudioExportCGImage` and a UIImage wrapper `qrStudioExportImage`; `QrStudioColor.cgColor` is added to the iOS look extension; CodePaths imports QrCode only when the module exists (the Mac compiles `submodules/QrCode/Sources/QrCodeMatrix.swift`, which equals its old QrStudioCodeMatrix copy). Kept per app: QrStudioLook (iOS theme reference, UIColor vs NSColor), views, sticker decoders, tests of views. New iOS tests: persistent toast never closes after the hold, temporary constraint. Not built (risk: a moved file may use an internal helper of a view file; -warnings-as-errors).

- **VP9Hardware tests find their fixtures under SwiftPM too** (W7). `fixture(_:)` in `VP9HardwareTests` reads `Bundle.module` when built as a Swift package (`SWIFT_PACKAGE`) and keeps the Bazel `TEST_SRCDIR` lookup otherwise, so the Mac builds this test file and the two decoder sources instead of keeping copies (the Mac's `VP9HardwareStatsTests` equals the iOS one, and its stats-delta asserts now run on both). Not built.

- **SwiftMath: the Mac's hardening and authoring API land in iOS** (W7, so the Mac can build the iOS sources). From the Mac copy: `MTMathListBuilder` stops at nesting depth 64 (`nestingTooDeep`), caps tables at 100000 rows/columns/cells (`tableTooLarge`, `invalidTableState`), rejects `\\` outside an environment, builds `\substack` through `buildTable` and restores `currentEnv` on every exit, `cr` split from `\\`; `buildForAuthoring` and `MTMathRenderer.renderForAuthoring` (nil on unsupported line breaks, non-finite size, over 8192 px per side or 4096x1024 area); `MTTypesetter` clamps constructed glyph height to 64 em, guards non-finite heights and loses a force unwrap in the inner display; `MTAtomTokenizer` turns an empty relation atom into an invisible separator; `MTLineFitter` drops the unused `exceedsMaxWidth`. Platform glue stays one tree: `#if os(macOS) import Cocoa` in seven files and `swiftMathFontsBundle()` (`Bundle.module` under `SWIFT_PACKAGE`, the app's mathFonts.bundle otherwise). The iOS app now rejects malformed or oversized formulas the Mac already rejected. Not built (risk: the new `-warnings-as-errors` build of the changed files).

- **WebRTC is klaipher/webrtc `nanogram`, one pin for all three apps; the vendored third-party copies are gone** (W5.3). `third-party/webrtc/webrtc` -> fa0b3c5f (17 commits on 779c4aa7: the Android and Mac platform code, abseil/libsrtp/crc32c as the fork's pinned submodules, pffft, rnnoise-vad, libevent, jni_zero carried in the fork; local fork commit until pushed), `.gitmodules` branch `nanogram`. `third-party/webrtc/BUILD` now builds libsrtp (cisco v2.5.0, was the Chromium v2.2.0 snapshot: a behaviour jump), crc32c (21fc8ef3, byte-identical sources), pffft, rnnoise-vad and the ooura fft4g straight from the fork's `third_party/` dirs, and takes abseil from the official LTS 20260817.0 as the Bazel module `abseil-cpp` (`MODULE.bazel` local_path_override onto the fork's `third_party/abseil-cpp`, `.bazelignore`); the old absl copy with its lifetimebound edit is gone.
