Deleted: `third-party/webrtc/{absl,crc32c,libsrtp,libsrtp_config,pffft,rnnoise,fft4g,dependencies}` (usrsctp and libevent were not compiled). `crc32c_config.h` stays here as consumer glue (`third-party/webrtc/config/`). Parity: the ~56 `allow webrtc` lines, `webrtc-root` and the `webrtc_differences` gate are removed (shared/ parity.py and tests), `pin webrtc` now names the Mac `submodules/webrtc` and the Android path like any pin; `upstream-sync.json` follows branch `nanogram`. Needs the first Bazel run to refresh `MODULE.bazel.lock` (abseil-cpp pulls googletest and gloop from the registry) and a build to confirm abseil 20260817.0 compiles with Apple Clang and libsrtp 2.5.0 with BoringSSL. Not built.

- **tgcalls: one `nanogram-shared` commit for all three apps; the iOS bridge lives in the fork** (W4.2). `submodules/TgVoipWebrtc/tgcalls` is pinned to klaipher/tgcalls `nanogram-shared` 2a5daa0f (the merge of the Android line, 9bfb76f + eb270676 + the Android guards, `tools/` scripts and the `ios/` move; local fork commit until the fork is pushed). The Objective-C++ bridge (`Sources`, `PublicHeaders`, `tests/TestNegotiation.mm`, byte-identical `cmp` proof in `docs/shared/w4-2-w5-3-identity-proofs.md`) is the fork's `ios/` directory; `submodules/TgVoipWebrtc/BUILD` globs and includes point there, module name still `TgVoipWebrtc`. The wasm-to-C++ genrule runs the fork's `tgcalls/tools/wasm_to_source.py` (the iOS copy is deleted, identical blob). `tools/dev/tgcalls-port` is deleted with its mentions: there is no per-platform port step any more. Behaviour changes that arrive with the merge and need a runtime check on iOS: a late-added group-call video sink no longer gets the last frame replayed (Android-only guard keeps it on Apple, see the fork's NANOGRAM-CHANGES.md), `InstanceV2Impl` resets its channels only under `WEBRTC_ANDROID`. Not built.

- **twidget (with gumbo) is the klaipher/twidget submodule** (W3.4, step 3). `third-party/twidget/twidget` (`nanogram`, a4f5391c, shallow; Android's twidget line + the Mac branch + this app's 91 litehtml/Apple-layer edits as carried by the fork) replaces the vendored LiteHTML, TwidgetAssets, Examples, licences and the separate `third-party/gumbo` sources (byte-identical to the fork's `litehtml/src/gumbo`). `//third-party/twidget:gumbo` compiles it; `//third-party/gumbo` is an alias that keeps the html5lib tests. BUILD globs, include paths, the three WidgetRuntimeIOS includes of `LHHostSupport.h`, the iOS test shim's corpus path and the no-I/O scan thresholds (litehtml 140, quickjs 20) follow. The old 2,500-line VENDOR.md is `docs/shared/twidget-ios-vendor-history.md`. The fork's Apple headers still carry a stray `#import <Cocoa/Cocoa.h>` (fork follow-up; `shim/Cocoa/Cocoa.h` answers it meanwhile). The fork drops the iOS behaviour of edits 47-49, 56-58, so those blocks of `tests/fork_test.cpp` may fail on the first run. Not built.

- **widget-sandbox is the klaipher/widget-sandbox submodule** (W3.4, step 2). `third-party/widget-sandbox/widget-sandbox` (`nanogram`, 1b21a48e, shallow; Telegram's v0.6.3 line + the Mac remote-engine commits) replaces the vendored v0.5.0 corpus, conformance set, stress kit, profile, fixtures and the C++ gesture/SDK/CPU policies; `IntlBridge.swift` is cut out of the submodule's `SandboxEngine.swift` by a genrule. BUILD globs, the resource bundles, `generate-profile-tables.py` (output byte-identical but its header) and `conformance_report.sh` follow the new layout. Counts re-baselined without a run: conformance 68 auto / 47 visual tests (121 theme runs, was 113), channel 122 posts (was 114), corpus 279 listed files (was 278) and 127 attack pages (was 113). `CpuBudget` is v0.6.x's (no floor, a debt past the quota): `CpuBudgetTests` and the `CpuQuota`/`CpuSlice` expectations changed to the whole 500 ms slice. WidgetRuntime does not yet follow the v0.6 paint, node and query limits (VENDOR.md lists them). Not built. To do after a run: conformance baseline, corpus goldens.

- **QuickJS is the klaipher/quickjs submodule** (W3.4, step 1). `third-party/quickjs-ng/quickjs` (`nanogram`, f6fc68b9, shallow) replaces the vendored quickjs-ng v0.17.0 sources and `include/quickjs.h`; the shim, the three C test suites and the BUILD targets stay. Proof: 787 of 793 files are blob-identical to tdlib/td@0e88653d (the submodule pin); the other 6 are generated headers (`generate/auto/td/...`) that `TdBinding.mm` (`td/e2e/e2e_api.h` only) never reads. Proof: 333 of 334 old blobs are identical to the tag; the only difference is `gradlew.bat` (old copy stored with CRLF line endings, not used by the build). `BUILD` change (review fix): the `libwebp_sources` glob excludes `libwebp/**/.*` and `libwebp/webp_js/**`, because `libwebp/.git` holds a per-worktree gitdir path that would change the genrule input digest in every worktree and defeat the shared disk cache.

Proof: blobs of bitwise.c, crctable.h, framing.c, ogg.h and os_types.h are identical to the tag; config_types.h is the generated header (upstream only has `config_types.h.in`) and is carried unchanged.

Proof: all 144 old `core/` blobs and `LICENSE` are identical in the tag; every compiled glob matches the same file set before and after.

Proof: tree hash 831656cc of the old `third-party/boost_regex/include` equals `HEAD:include` of the submodule (byte-identical headers).

- **FFmpeg is the klaipher/FFmpeg submodule** (shared-libraries plan W1.1). `submodules/ffmpeg/Sources/FFMpeg/ffmpeg-9.0.2` is now a shallow submodule of `klaipher/FFmpeg` branch `nanogram` pinned at d84c50bc (same path, so the BUILD glob, `FFMPEG_VERSION` and the Mac `build.sh` autodetect are unchanged); the 10399 vendored files are gone. Android already builds the same commit. Proof: the old tree and d84c50bc differ in exactly two sources, `libavcodec/hevc/hevcdec.c` and `libavutil/encryption_info.c` (the Android fixes iOS now gains), plus dotfiles (`.gitignore`, `.gitattributes`) the BUILD glob excludes. `tg doctor` warns when the sources directory is empty.
- **Watch app: tlottie replaces rlottie** (shared-libraries plan, lane Watch; owner decision Option B, 2026-11-14; commits 8e30d1a0, 9a5424ac, 43996cbb, docs commit after them). `Telegram/WatchApp/Packages/RLottieKit` (Samsung rlottie v0.2 + 16 cherry-picks + `kMaxModelTreeDepth` 65 + hand-written `config.h`, 115 files under `rlottie/`; removed tree a215c911) is deleted, so the watch no longer carries a second Lottie engine. `Packages/TLottieKit` takes over: `TLottieAnimation` (Swift) calls tlottie's C API exactly as the phone's `TLottieAnimation.mm` does (`tlottie_new_with_options` with `TLOTTIE_CHANNEL_BGRA`, then `tlottie_render` straight into the CFData that backs the CGImage: no stride copy and no per-frame buffer copy), keeps the old wrapper's caps (over 1536 px, over 360 fps, over 9 s are refused) and the TGS inflate step. `LottieAnimationView`, `StickerCellView` and `StickerPicker` use it; `project.yml` and `tgwatch.xcodeproj` (xcodegen) reference TLottieKit. Tests moved with `git mv`; the sticker regression grid is still rlottie's (tolerance 6) and must be re-recorded on first run if it fails.
  - Behaviour change: rlottie's process-wide model cache (keyed by `url.lastPathComponent`) is gone, so every TGS is inflated and parsed again on each load (`LottieAnimationView` drops `animation = nil` and reloads when a bubble returns, picker tiles decode frame 0); memory is no longer retained for every sticker ever seen. Check `MediaRenderBenchmarkTests` load time on device and add a small cache only if it needs one. `renderFrame` also refuses sizes over 4096 px.
  - Script fixes after review: the export check no longer uses `grep -q` under pipefail (nm died of SIGPIPE, 141, so every cache miss failed); only `rustup` is required and is found on PATH, Homebrew or `~/.cargo/bin` (this machine has no `cargo`); the cache entry is staged and renamed in; the Xcode version is part of the key.
  - Build: `tools/build-tlottie-xcframework.sh` compiles `submodules/tlottie/tlottie` (same source and pin as the phone) with `--no-default-features --features cpu,no-std,c-api`, profile `release-nostd` (panic=abort), on the pinned nightly in `tools/tlottie-toolchain.txt` with `-Zbuild-std=core,alloc`, for `arm64_32-apple-watchos`, `aarch64-apple-watchos` (lipo'd into one device slice) and `aarch64-apple-watchos-sim`, and wraps them as `TLottieCore.xcframework` (a SwiftPM binaryTarget; generated, git-ignored). The crate's `no_std_runtime` supplies the allocator over system `malloc` and the aborting panic handler. Cached under `~/.cache/telegram-ios/watch-tlottie` by a hash of the crate sources, the script, the pin and the deployment target.
  - Wiring: `prebuilt_watchos_compile.sh` runs it before the SwiftPM resolve; `//submodules/tlottie:tlottie_sources` are inputs of `PrebuiltWatchosCompile` (a tlottie pin bump reruns it; version/build number/identity still do not); `tg watch build|test|project` run it first, `tg watch tlottie [--macos] [--force]` alone.
  - `Telegram/WatchApp` is the source of the watch app now (the `tgwatch` repo is not on any machine); `Telegram/CLAUDE.md` says so.
  - Not built or run (no-build round). Open for the main session: run `tools/dev/tg watch tlottie`, then `tg watch test`, then one device build with `--embedWatchApp`; confirm the nightly pin installs; compare frames before and after (`docs/visual-verification.md`).

Modernization stream ios-core (branch `mod/ios-core`, merge 0b8580ed; shared with the Mac app; not built or run). Dark by default unless noted.

- **Audio session no longer sleeps 2 s on the session queue** (iOS-CONC-4, audio half). A failed `setActive(false)` in `applyNone` used to `Thread.sleep(2.0)` on the serial audio-session queue, stalling the next activation (call, voice message, playback handoff). The retry is now `queue.after(2.0)`, guarded by `ManagedAudioSessionDeactivationRetryGate`: every `applyNone`, `setup` and `activate` advances a generation, and the retry runs only if its token is still current and no type is held, so it cannot deactivate a session a later request activated. Tests: `ManagedAudioSessionDeactivationRetryGateTests`. Not changed (verdict: tail-only or pre-UI paths): `MediaDataReader` 1/60 s polling, `StorageBox` and `AccountManagerImpl` open retries. Measure: System Trace around call/voice-message transitions with a forced `setActive(false)` failure.
- **Statistics charts on a CADisplayLink** (iOS-CONC-1). On iOS `GraphCore` and `GraphCore2` `DisplayLinkService` drop the 60 Hz global-queue `DispatchSource` timer with `DispatchQueue.main.sync` and use a `CADisplayLink` on the main run loop (common modes, `preferredFrameRateRange` 30-120 preferred 60); the link is created on the first listener and invalidated when the last one leaves or a tick finds none; ticks feed `update(delta:)` from `targetTimestamp` (never negative). Public add/remove API unchanged; macOS keeps its own path (GraphCore2 CADisplayLink/CVDisplayLink, GraphCore v1 timer). The existing macOS host test `testDisplayLinkDeltasAreNeverNegative` covers the shared `fire(frameTime:)` contract; the iOS branch has no host test (needs a device or simulator). Measure: Time Profiler and Animation Hitches on the channel statistics screen, thread wakeups.
- **First-frame requests always finish** (iOS-MEDIA-THREADS-1, completion half). `CachedVideoFirstFrameRepresentation` and `CachedVideoPrefixFirstFrameRepresentation` emitted nothing for a nil decoded frame or a failed JPEG encode and never completed, so `subscriber.keepAlive(videoSource)` held the source, its thread and decoder until the consumer disposed. `UniversalSoftwareVideoSourceTakeFrameResult.firstFrame(compressionQuality:)` now classifies the result (pending / jpeg / failed) and both representations complete on failure. Tests: `UniversalSoftwareVideoSourceFirstFrameTests`. Deferred: the bounded worker pool for `FFMpegMediaFrameSource` and `UniversalSoftwareVideoSource` threads (blocking FFmpeg reads and the thread-local interrupt context need a design first). Measure: NSThread count and live stacks after scrolling a video-heavy channel and leaving the chat.
- **Hardware decode for gallery scrub previews, behind an experiment** (IOS-VIDEO-1). With `nanogram.experiment.previewHardwareDecode` on (default off), `MediaPlayerFramePreview` opens its `UniversalSoftwareVideoSource` with `hardwareDecode: true`: H.264/HEVC of at least 1920 on the long side use a VideoToolbox hwaccel context (falls back to the software context when open fails), and `render(frame:)` converts the VideoToolbox pixel buffer with `VTCreateCGImageFromCVPixelBuffer`. The first-frame cache representations stay software (a session costs as much as one keyframe decode) and so do small clips. Tests: `UniversalSoftwareVideoSourceHardwareDecodeTests` (selection rule). Measure: scrub a 4K HEVC clip for 20 s, software vs hardware, CPU and Power Profiler plus time to first preview frame; check a preview while a main player runs (VT session limit).
- **Multi-threaded software AV1 on iOS, behind an experiment** (IOS-SIMD-1). `FFMpegSoftwareDecodeThreads.apply` now also runs on iOS when `nanogram.experiment.softwareDecodeThreads` is on (default off): AV1 at 720p or more gets min(active cores, 4) decode threads, and 1 thread while Low Power Mode is on or the thermal state is serious or worse. Covers every call site (gallery previews, first-frame cache, `FFMpegFileReader` chunk player, `preferSoftwareDecoding`). Mac unchanged (cap 8). Tests: `iOSThreadCount` cases in `FFMpegSoftwareDecodeThreadsTests`. Measure: `takeFrame(at: 0)` and 10 scrub steps on a 1080p AV1 clip, 1 vs 4 threads, on an iPhone 14/15 non-Pro with Power Profiler.
- **iOS thread pools on QoS, behind an experiment** (iOS-QOS-1). New `ExperimentalPerformanceFlags` (SwiftSignalKit): each device-dependent performance item reads a UserDefaults bool `nanogram.experiment.<name>`, default off. With `threadPoolQualityOfService` on, iOS `ThreadPool(threadCount:threadPriority:)` sets `qualityOfService` through the existing 0.2/0.5 mapping instead of `threadPriority`; the default stays the legacy priority, the Mac is unchanged (already QoS). Tests: `ThreadPoolConfigurationTests`, `ExperimentalPerformanceFlagsTests`. Measure: System Trace efficiency-core share while scrolling the photo picker and sticker panel, and video-sticker first-frame latency. Debug Settings toggle not wired yet (set the key with `defaults write` or `ExperimentalPerformanceFlags.shared.setOverride`).
- **AES-IGE decrypt in one fused ARMv8 loop** (IOS-CRYPTO-1). `MyAesIgeDecrypt` on arm64 expands the key once and runs AESD/AESIMC (NEON intrinsics, no new dependency) over the whole buffer instead of one `CCCryptorUpdate` per 16-byte block; IV in/out chaining, failure zeroing and the block-multiple check are unchanged. The old code stays as `MyAesIgeDecryptReference` (non-arm64 path and test oracle). `MTAesIgeDecryptEquivalenceTests` compares both on random vectors (AES-128/192/256, unaligned buffers, split-buffer IV chaining, round trip, failure zeroing). Shared with the Mac app (arm64 only there). Measure: micro-benchmark on 128 KB/512 KB/1 MB, then MTProto-queue CPU in a 200 MB download.
- **Later changes in ios-core** (after the entries above were written): the AES key schedule is cleared after use (IOS-CRYPTO-1); the GraphCore2 iOS branch of the CADisplayLink change was removed, so only GraphCore uses it on iOS (iOS-CONC-1); iOS AV1 decode threads are capped by performance cores (IOS-SIMD-1); the experiment flags are mirrored into SwiftSignalKit2; first-frame JPEG classification is iOS-only and kept off the Mac build (iOS-MEDIA-THREADS-1); the experiments are toggled by launch arguments plus a relaunch.

Modernization stream ios-glance (branch `mod/ios-glance`, merge 08cba231; not built or run, host tests listed below).

- **IOS-WIDGET-1: chats widget with small and Lock Screen families** (new modules `NanogramGlance`, `NanogramAppIntents`; `Telegram/WidgetKitWidget/GlanceWidgets.swift`; Debug switch `nanogramGlanceables`, default off). New widget `Nanogram_GlanceChats` on iOS 17+: `AppIntentConfiguration` with `SelectChatsIntent` (`[PeerEntity]`), families `systemSmall`, `accessoryCircular`, `accessoryRectangular`, `accessoryInline`. It reads the chosen chats through the same Postbox path as the SiriKit widgets (`getCommonTimeline` was split: `loadWidgetPeers(identifiers:environment:completion:)` is shared, the "<accountId>:<peerId>" identifier is the same). It shows counts and the generic title while the app is passcode-locked; names only when unlocked and, on Lock Screen families, only when `displayNameOnLockscreen` allows (`GlanceVisibility`, tested); message text is never shown; the timeline emits a second, locked entry when the auto-lock runs out. Candidates for the picker come from `nanogram-glance.json` in the app group (written by the app, no chats while locked or off). The two existing IntentConfiguration widgets are left untouched on purpose: replacing them on iOS 17 needs `CustomIntentMigratedAppIntent` and a device check that placed widgets keep their chats, which cannot be verified without a build. With the switch off the new widget shows its empty state (a widget cannot be hidden from the gallery at runtime). Tests: `//submodules/NanogramGlance:NanogramGlanceTests` (host).
- **IOS-APPINTENTS-1: App Intents, App Shortcuts and a Control Center button** (`submodules/NanogramAppIntents`, `Telegram/WidgetKitWidget/GlanceControls.swift`). `OpenChatIntent` (with `PeerEntity`), `OpenSavedMessagesIntent`, `SendToSavedMessagesIntent` (runs in the app, refused while the app is locked or the switch is off) and `NanogramShortcuts` (App Shortcuts). iOS 18 `SavedMessagesControl` (ControlWidgetButton). The intents call the running app through `GlanceIntentBridge` (registered by `GlanceCoordinator`); an intent that arrives before the app registered waits up to 8 s. INExtension handlers and the contacts-only Spotlight index are unchanged. `Telegram/BUILD`: `nanogram_app_intents_metadata` (default True) passes `app_intents` to the app and the widget extension; set it False if rules_apple 5.4.0 rejects the attribute (then Shortcuts and the control are not discovered, the rest builds).
- **IOS-LIVEACT-1: Live Activities for live location and calls without CallKit** (`NanogramGlanceActivityAttributes`, `GlanceActivityWidget.swift`, `submodules/TelegramUI/Sources/GlanceCoordinator.swift`; `NSSupportsLiveActivities` in the app plist). `GlanceCoordinator` (created next to `WidgetDataContext`, not touching `DeviceLocationManager`, which IOS-LOC-1 owns) observes `LiveLocationManager.summaryManager.broadcastingToMessages()` and the call manager, starts and updates one activity per kind, ends it when sharing or the call stops, ends leftovers of a killed process at start, and sets a `staleDate` (the sharing end, at most 8 h; 4 h for calls). Title is generic while the app is locked or `displayNameOnLockscreen` is off (`GlanceActivityPolicy`, tested). Stop and End buttons are `LiveActivityIntent`s (iOS 17) that run in the app. The coordinator also holds the app-side wiring of IOS-WIDGET-1 (snapshot) and IOS-APPINTENTS-1 (intent handler).
- **Review fixes applied after this text was written (ios-glance)**: passcode lock instead of 'not active' as the lock input (names and the chat list now work in the background), alwayslink for the intents, stored chat ids always resolve, intents require authentication, the new widget's chats feed the throttled reloads, StandBy small widget is a lock-screen surface, activity content equality ignores the stale date, snapshot written off the main thread with file protection, call timer and Stop-all fixes, App Shortcut parameters refreshed.

## 13.2 (202611132038) — 2026-11-13 — 3c4e6399

TestFlight build (processed VALID, What to Test set). Previous TestFlight build: 202611121002 (25edb39c); changes since are `git log 800c5c6a..3c4e6399` (32 commits). Source: the owner's MetricKit export (memory-limit kill opening a 1-page PDF, the language prompt on the next launch) and a tester's report that a call to a locked iPhone showed nothing; plan with 83 finding ids, statuses below.
What to Test: call this phone with it locked, in the background and with the app closed; Answer and Decline from the notification; answer on the Mac and check the phone stops ringing; open a few PDFs; relaunch and check the language prompt stays away.

- **PDF memory kill** (PDF-1/CR-3, PDF-2; ef1b9fa3, e975140f). MetricKit `memoryExceptionDiagnostic` at 14:53Z on 202611121002: PDFKit's own text recognition (Vision → TextRecognition/Espresso on the ANE) ran inside the app on a 1-page ~200 KB receipt while the app was near its limit. There is no public switch for PDFKit's OCR, so `PdfInlineViewingPolicy` (BackgroundDiagnostics, pure, tested) decides per open from `os_proc_available_memory()`: headroom under 1.5 GB (scaled to the device's budget) opens the system preview (`presentDocumentPreviewController`) instead of the in-app PDFView (`PdfOpenRouting.swift`). The in-app document is released while the browser is minimized and comes back when shown; caches are purged before opening only when memory is already short. Not done: text-layer sampling, and the lazy bitmap fallback (PDF-2 option B) pending a device check that iOS 27 QuickLook renders out of process.
- **Memory-limit kills in Crash Logs** (LOGV-2; 521d7927). `CrashReportCollector` maps MetricKit memory exceptions into `CrashReportStore` (decoded-fixture test).
- **Language prompt** (LANG-2; c90c42b0). A configuration refresh without a suggested code no longer deletes the stored entry (`reconcileSuggestedLocalization`), seen codes live under a fork-owned preferences key so A→B→A flips stay seen, and every transition is logged. Cause still unproven; the log line will show it. Shared core.
- **Incoming calls without CallKit (no Apple approval needed)** (CI-1/AR-3/CB-7, CI-2/CB-5/AR-4, CI-5/CB-2, CI-6/CB-6, CI-8, CB-1, CB-4, CV-1; a7aec272, baf5945c, 4c745b32, 268cac89, 53fa32dc, 217c7af8, 7fcd1ccc, a0ad09ab).
  - The NSE ring is `.timeSensitive` with `relevanceScore 1.0` (takes effect once the entitlement is added after the portal tick), carries an `incomingCall` category with Answer (foreground, auth) and Decline, and call ids/access hash in userInfo. Answer or tap injects the call update and opens the call screen; Decline hangs up on the server directly, so CallKit never flashes.
  - The NSE acknowledges the call (`phone.receivedCall`, shared core `_internal_acknowledgeIncomingCall`) so the caller sees Ringing; it keeps the connection open up to ~1 s for that.
  - Rings are taken down when the call ends, is declined, busy, or answered elsewhere (phone-call service message, READ_HISTORY for that peer, app-side ring replacement), and stale rings (>90 s) are swept.
  - Ringtone and anonymous INPerson for every ring; the caller's name is hidden when the app is passcode-locked or names are hidden on the lock screen (CI-7 owner default).
  - The microphone gate follows the permission reactively; `willPresent` shows banner and sound for a non-CallKit incoming call.
- **Call-stack fixes** (CB-3, CB-8, CB-9, CI-9, AR-8; 47ba6539, 4ff25ba7, 9f3ae723, 287f386d, 72abffec). CallKit answer/decline that arrives before the call exists is kept and applied (10 s); a ring past the server timeout + 10 s with no update is dropped as missed locally (shared core); CallKit end reasons follow the termination, banner decline hangs up instead of busy; a CallKit refusal for unknown/unentitled reasons rings without CallKit instead of hanging up (block-list filtering still declines); the iOS 26.4 VoIP metadata delegate reads `mustReport` (3c4e6399 fixed its selector to PushKit's `withCompletionHandler:` label; with the wrong label it was never called and the release build refused it).
- **NSE no longer traps when the database is busy at open** (CR-2/CV-1, owner default quiet failure; 8a6a8feb). A failed `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` at open on a non-owner connection retries once with a short busy timeout, then returns nil to the existing plain-push path.
- **Premium coin hang** (HANG-2; 196fb175, 93b14451). The 3D scene setup is deferred out of `View.init`; the logo is chosen as soon as it loads.
- **Disk writes** (DW-3/LOGV-4, DW-7, DW-5 measure half; 8140c24e, f9202608, 9360a1e9). Hardware AV1 reuses a segment's temp file on replay (LRU keyed by resource and ranges); StorageBox size updates are batched (10 s / 256 items, flush on background); the ledger records the top Postbox WAL call sites per day. Not done: removing the top WAL writers (needs a day of data), the mediaBox.video split, and the write-budget governor (DW-6, owner: no).
- **Diagnostics** (MET-1, MET-3; 81a4b260, 32b94772, eb4cf766). Frame-hitch sampling turns itself off after 30 min or in the background and samples at 10 ms (shared, Mac too); the ledger keeps launch/resume histograms.
- **Docs** (CV-2, AR-10; 32e524b8). `docs/release/apple-capabilities.md`: symbol names instead of line numbers, Time Sensitive (code ready, entitlement after the portal tick), Increased Memory Limit (owner declined), the extension-profile rule.
- **Build hygiene** (3be4d49a, 5f4052ed, f806e500): Postbox importer allow-list, Swift 6 isolation fixes, lint (no force unwraps).
- **Not done / waiting**: Time Sensitive entitlement key (owner ticks the portal on the app and `.NotificationService`; patch ready); Notification Filtering switch-on (Apple; patch ready); Increased Memory Limit (LOGV-1, owner declined); MTE A/B (LOGV-3, owner); re-ring (CI-4), AlarmKit (CI-11/AR-5), self-hosted relay (AR-2): owner defaults no; device logs for the failed call (CI-10/AR-11, CV-3); memory baseline growth (PDF-3/MET-2: hooks only, needs a day of logs).

## 13.2 (202611121002) — 2026-11-12 — 25edb39c

TestFlight build (processed VALID, What to Test set). Previous TestFlight build: 202611112014 (c2a45687); changes since are `git log c2a45687..25edb39c`: the native HLS player port (crash fix on malformed playlists, no re-writing of cached segments, 15 s buffer for muted inline videos, hardware AV1), state sync (read marks sent once, fewer probes and transactions), attach menu performance, chat list and navigation performance, smaller logs (network dumps behind a Debug switch), chat themes requested once per 10 minutes, rlottie removed.
What to Test: open and scroll several chats and swipe back; send messages quickly; play videos in channels (muted and full screen); open the attach menu, edit a photo, tap Done.

- **13.2 lost-fix audit** (read-only, both repos): no unrecorded loss. Open as recorded: F4 (history-fill request size), F7 (reads for strangers, upstream removed the hold; `docs/subsystems/suspicious-peer.md` corrected), the HLS part store (fixed in this round by perf/hls-native-port). Not fixed, minor: `MediaGridView`'s own `CADisplayLink` for its short scroll animations, and `PresentChatMediaEditor`'s lazy `UIImage(contentsOfFile:)` on the main queue.

- **rlottie removed** (`submodules/rlottie`, its `.gitmodules` entry, the `pin rlottie` / `share submodules/rlottie` lines of `docs/mac-core-paths.txt`, the upstream-sync pin). Upstream 13.2 deleted it and our iOS has rendered with tlottie only since the 13.2 merge; we kept it for the Mac, whose 13.2 merge drops it too. Nothing built it any more, so no behaviour change. The Watch app keeps its own `RLottieKit`.

- **Native HLS player: crash fix, no re-writing of cached segments, smaller buffer for muted inline videos, hardware AV1 on capable iPhones** (owner-approved port of our old-player work to 13.2's native player; five commits on `perf/hls-native-port`, built and run by the owner, not by this change).
  1. Playlist hardening (`674ad908`; port of the Mac's 27ea951d). 13.2's playlist parser added server-supplied byte ranges with trapping `start + length`, so a malformed playlist crashed the app (on scroll preload too). `HLSMediaPlaylist` now uses `addingReportingOverflow` and throws `malformedTag("EXT-X-BYTERANGE" / "EXT-X-MAP")`; `rangesFit(fileSize:)` checks the init and every segment (the loader checked only the last segment), in `HLSNativePlaylistLoader` and in `minimizedHLSQualityPreloadData`; `HLSLevels.estimatedBandwidth(size:duration:)` and `clampedDimension` replace `Int(Double(size)/duration)*8` and the dimension casts; the fallback target duration is clamped. Retries are unchanged. Tests: `HLSMediaPlaylistTests` and `HLSLevelTests` (the Mac's).
  2. + 3. Zero-copy parts and write accounting (`HLSNativePartStore`). The store read init and segment from MediaBox and wrote them again, combined, to a `.atomic` temp mp4 (the "use our parts for cached segments" open item; the write-once change 8d5d69bf covered only the removed JS player). It now probes the bytes in memory (`HLSFragmentTiming`, else `extractFFMpegMediaInfo(data:)`) and returns `.mediaBoxRanges` over `[initRange, dataRange]`: no second copy, no temp file, `keepResource` held while the part lives (`MediaBoxRanges`), and a `.mediaBoxRanges` part has no file to dispose (the store's disposal already handles both kinds). Only hardware AV1, which AVFoundation reads from a path, still writes a temp file, now counted under `DiskWriteCategory.tempBoxHLS`. The decision is `HLSStreamingStorage.nativePartContent` (MediaPlayer, so it is testable with the existing harness); the store got a `MediaBox` from `HLSVideoContent.makeContentNode`'s `postbox`, and is a new entry in `AllowedImporters.txt`. Tests (`HLSStreamingStorageTests`): a cached segment gives `.mediaBoxRanges` and 0 written bytes and decodes the same frames as the file; no init range gives one range; software AV1 stays a MediaBox part; hardware AV1 gives a temp file whose bytes are init + segment and is counted.
  4. Forward buffer (`HLSPlaybackController.setMaxBufferAhead`, `hlsForwardBufferSeconds`). A muted inline autoplay buffers 15 s ahead, not 60 s, so a video scrolled past fetches and holds less; once the node has had sound (gallery, fullscreen) it keeps 60 s even if muted again. The node has no inline/gallery flag, so "inline" is "never had sound". Tests: `HLSForwardBufferTests`; `HLSPlaybackControllerPipelineTests.testLoadsStopAtTheForwardLimitAndResumeWhenItRises`.
  5. Hardware AV1 (`VideoRenditionCodecPreference.isHardwareAv1Enabled`). `HLSCodecConfiguration` took it only from `ios_enable_hardware_av1` (default off), so A17 Pro and newer picked the larger H.264/HEVC renditions. It now follows `internal_isHardwareAv1Supported` (VideoToolbox), as the Mac does; the key present and false is a kill switch, absent (or not a number) means the device decides, and true never enables what the device cannot decode. Tests: `VideoRenditionCodecPreferenceTests` (device and flag disagreeing both ways).
  Nothing changes what is sent to Telegram's servers: the same ranges are fetched. Shared core: `submodules/MediaPlayer` is in `docs/mac-core-paths.txt`; `HLSPlayback` and `TelegramUniversalVideoContent` are not (the Mac has its own copies in `packages/`; the Mac already has item 1).

- **State sync: fewer duplicate requests, transactions and disk writes** (owner-approved perf round on `perf/state-sync`; shared core, so the Mac gets it through a core bump; tests written first, run with the round's build). Nothing a server would see from upstream changes except that byte-identical repeats are gone.
  - **Read-state pushes are sent once** (741b8ead). Why: in 54 minutes 125 operations for 79 distinct (peer, max read id) pairs, 47 of 92 `messages.readHistory` repeated an id already sent, 98 of 111 answers had `ptsCount: 0`. How: `ManagedSynchronizePeerReadStates` compared whole operations, and a Push carries the full read state, so a new top known id, outgoing read id or unread count was a "new" operation and re-ran. It now compares `syncPayload` (max read id, marked-unread flag, `thenSync`). A per-account `PeerReadStatePushLedger` remembers the last push whose requests all succeeded; an identical push skips `readHistory`/`markDialogUnread` and is confirmed by the existing verification. The record is dropped when the server reports a disagreeing read state or unread mark (`applyResetReadState`, `applyUpdatePeerChatUnreadMark`, validation), and a push in flight while the server reported is not recorded. Deviation from the brief: validation after a skipped push still runs when `thenSync` is set (holes may have appeared since the last validation, which the operation does not say). Tests: `PeerReadStatePushDedupTests`.
  - **Read-state validation skips its emptiness probe** (a59dd0e6). `getHistory(limit: 1)` only checked non-emptiness before `getPeerDialogs`; with a cloud message stored it is skipped, and a `getPeerDialogs` answer with no dialog falls back to the probe once (empty confirms, non-empty retries as before). Tests: `ReadStateValidationProbeTests`.
  - **Slow-view diagnostics** (5140a390). `combinedView(keys:)` passes the caller's file and line and logs the view-key kinds; a transaction over 50 ms logs the live view counts per kind (`ViewTracker.liveViewCountsDescription`). No behaviour change.
  - **The event hub opens no transaction for nothing** (bfb257d1). `AccountStateEventHub.dispatch` skips `postbox.transaction` when `addedIncomingMessageIds` and `wasScheduledMessageIds` are both empty; the empty batch and the completion still arrive in a queue block of their own after the same fan-out. Test: `AccountStateEventHubTests`.
  - **Not done: holding presence-only batches in the background.** It would defer updates inside the pts/seq sequencing and the gap handling, which this round must not touch.

Attach menu (photo grid, chat media editor, camera tile) performance and battery, from a review that symbolicated the owner's logs (branch perf/attach-grid-editor). Not run on a device; not built or tested by the author (owner runs the tests).

- **Camera tile creates its camera on first use (fe66ae93)**. `MediaPickerScreen` made the tile's `Camera` (`CameraSession.init`, a blocking ~7 ms IPC on main) at every attach open; in 3 of 5 sessions it never ran. Now `ensureChatCamera` creates it on the tile's first `.start` (`applyChatCameraCaptureUpdate`) or on a tap (`cameraTapped`), never while the live preview is off (Power Saving) except for a tap, and never after the picker was dismissed. The hand-off contract of ff5ed8f6 is unchanged: a tap creates the camera if needed, and `mediaPickerChatCameraHandOff(tileCaptureState:)` starts it (the tile's state is `nil` then). Rule: `mediaPickerChatCameraShouldCreateNow` (ChatMediaSend `MediaPickerCameraCell.swift`). Test: ChatMediaSendTests `testChatCameraIsCreatedOnlyWhenTheTileRunsItOrATapNeedsIt`.
- **Thumbnail filler pauses while the picker is covered (a54515e5)**. `ThumbnailFillPacing` only counted open pickers and the data source holds its lease for its lifetime, so the filler kept walking the library behind the full-screen editor, the camera and a minimized sheet. New condition `isPickerCovered` (the pacer counts covering owners; covered when they cover every open picker). `MediaPickerScreen` covers while `hasGallery`, while the camera is handed off and while `isMinimized`, and uncovers on deinit. Tests: ThumbnailFillPacingTests `testPausedWhileThePickerIsCovered`, `testPacerPausesWhileCoveredAndResumesWhenUncovered`, `testCoveredPickerDoesNotPauseAnotherOpenPicker`.
- **Editor "Done" no longer hangs ~288 ms on the caption commit (1bc6243e)**. The commit read the caption with keyboard autocorrection (a synchronous round trip to the keyboard process), then `autoSelect` relaid out the hidden picker and the attachment panel (`AttachmentPanel.loadTextNodeIfNeeded`) inside the same press. A caption saved on focus loss now uses `captionWithoutAutocorrection` (optional method on `TGCaptionPanelView`, implemented by `LegacyMessageInputPanel`; the send path still applies autocorrection); rule `chatMediaEditorCaptionReadAppliesAutocorrection(focusLost:)`. While `hasGallery` the picker holds back its selection relayout (`MediaPickerSelectionRelayoutGate`) and the selection-count push to the attachment controller, and runs them once, without animation, when it is visible again. Tests: ChatMediaEditorCaptionTests `testCaptionSavedOnFocusLossSkipsAutocorrection`, ChatMediaSendTests `MediaPickerSelectionRelayoutTests`. The shared autocorrect helper for the send path is another change; not duplicated here.
- **Shared MediaGrid memory is released after the last picker (c2476472)**. The singletons kept ~80-110 MB (up to 28 decoded 768x768 pages and writer contexts, launch-cache images, the filler's whole-library index and fetch result) until a memory warning or the background. When the last picker lease is released, the filler drops its catalog and fetch result (`ThumbnailFiller.releaseCatalog`), the launch images are released, the store flushes and then `trimToVisible(ids: [])` (skipped if a picker opened meanwhile). The next picker decodes what it shows again (launch images reload from the launch cache files). Test: ThumbnailFillPacingTests `testLastLeaseReleaseDropsDecodedPagesAndTheFillerIndex`.
- **Whole-library pass at utility QoS (3f6e193a)**. Every open read every asset's id and date (`makeCatalog`) on a serial queue inheriting user-initiated QoS. The first 4,096 items now come from a short user-initiated pass (`makePrefixCatalog`, with the launch snapshot) and the full pass runs on `changeQueue` at utility QoS; the full install replaces the prefix as before (generation 0 guard). Not done: reusing the result when the PhotoKit persistent change token is unchanged (optional, iOS 16+; needs a device to validate the token semantics). Test: MediaGridPhotoLibraryDataSourceTests `testWholeLibraryPassRunsAtUtilityAfterTheFirstScreens`.
- **Album list built when the albums menu opens (0503668c)**. `MediaPickerScreen` built it 0.4 s after every open and on every library change, but only `presentGroupsMenu` reads it. The signal is kept and `groupsPromise` subscribes to it on the first `presentGroupsMenu` (and stays subscribed after that, as before).
- **Editing context opens its disk cache and creates directories lazily (3976dce7)**. `TGMediaEditingContext.init` (main, every open) opened an LMDB database and created 5 directories. Now `diskCache` and the directory accessors create them on first use; `cleanup` skips what was never created (so an unused context no longer schedules the 10 s disk-cache cleanup).
- **Frame-hitch windows (b1fcae66)**: `attachGridScroll` (touch until 0.25 s without offset change after release), `attachGridZoom` (pinch), `attachEditor` (editor first appearance to disappearance), `camera` (full-screen camera `animateIn` to `dismiss`, stories camera too). V2 grid only; the legacy grid host has none. NavigationContainer untouched.

Performance, battery and disk-write fixes (branch perf/chat-list-nav, owner-approved 2026-11-11; tests written first, not yet built or run).

- **Back-swipe frame-hitch windows closed only at the next push** (6ecbaea1). `endHitchWindow()` ran only from `completeTopTransition`, so a `backSwipe:` window stayed open (display link at top rate) until the next push. Now `NavigationHitchWindow` (pure) holds the lifecycle; `endCommittedSwipe`, `landOnScreenBeneath` and the three cancel paths close it. Test: `NavigationHitchWindowTests`.
- **Back-swipe start stalled ~74 ms drawing rows that changed under a covered screen** (e84061bc). `isStillPrepared` checked only the layout. A list out of every window (`CoreVirtualListView.applyChanges`, `ListView.replayOperations`) posts `NanogramCoveredContentDidChange`; the container marks the prepared screen unprepared (`NavigationPopPreparation.noteContentChange`) and its idle hook lays out and runs `recursivelyEnsureDisplaySynchronously(false)` again. Test: `NavigationPopPreparationTests`.
- **Every history update laid out every loaded row on main (R20)** (fffd9824). `allUpdated` re-submitted all entries with a new `stableVersion`, so `isEqual` failed and each row was laid out synchronously. `preparedChatHistoryViewTransition` marks an update `keepsStableVersion` when the entry equals the previous one and the associated data and `rowPrepEpoch` are equal (`shouldKeepStableVersion`, in ChatRowPrep; presentation data is compared by identity); the CoreList backend then keeps the old entry. Neighbour changes and inset changes still produce a new entry. Spec R20's note (narrowing `allUpdated` is unsafe) is respected: `allUpdated` itself is unchanged. Test: `ChatRowStableVersionTests`.
- **Chat open decoded photos synchronously for off-screen preload rows** (1e3d6b31). Rows of the synchronous pass are counted from the anchor outward; only those starting inside the viewport height load synchronously, the 160 pt preload band loads asynchronously (`ChatViewportLoadBudget`). Test: `ChatViewportLoadBudgetTests`.
- **Delete "dust" snapshot could hang main for ~400 ms** (e3c00465). `makeContentSnapshot(scale:)` draws at scale 1.0; bubbles above 900 pt (was 1800) take the normal delete animation (`ChatDustSnapshotPolicy`). The context-menu snapshot is unchanged. Test: `ChatDustSnapshotPolicyTests`.
- **Fling flights forced 120 Hz main-thread sampling** (0c8a0aa5, 379e8cc3). `pinsSamplingLinkRate` now false (its own comment: no smoothness gained, double the main-thread work); no rate pinning of the animation in Low Power Mode (`PhysicsScrollEngine.pinnedFrameRate`). Test: `PhysicsScrollEngineRefreshRateTests` (the engine had no tests on these flags).
- **Chat-list media preview redrew on every row layout** (1acbbf69). `ChatListMediaPreviewNode.updateLayout` re-ran `asyncLayout` and apply each time (201 samples of thumbnail decoding in back-swipe stacks); `ChatListMediaPreviewLayoutGate` skips an unchanged size and media id. Test: `ChatListMediaPreviewLayoutGateTests`.
- **Send tap hitch up to ~210 ms** (f8a8d95b). `Keyboard.applyAutocorrection` (used by `sendCurrentMessage` and `getAttributedText`) commits only when `needsAutocorrectionCommit(text:selection:hasMarkedText:)` is true. The input panel's empty-text read on layout and the story caption length check pass `applyAutocorrection: false`. Test: `KeyboardAutocorrectionTests`.
- **Log volume ~0.5 GB/day (hit iOS's 1 GB/day write limit 4 times)** (49878305, 57d213eb). (a) "Log to file" no longer turns on MtProtoKit's request/response dumps: new Debug Settings switch "Network protocol dumps" (`LoggingSettings.logNetworkDumps`, off by default); `MTLog` and `MTLogEnabled()` follow it, `MTShortLog` lines stay. (b) Frame-hitch stacks: one per window (was 4), none unless the Frame hitches switch is on. (c) The MTContext keychain, datacenter and transportSchemeStats dumps are skipped in app extensions (the notification service printed them on every push). Tests: `MTLogChannelTests` (`MTShouldLogMT`), `LoggerNetworkLoggingTests`, `FrameHitchMonitorTests`.
- **`account.getChatThemes` once per chat open with the same hash** (86a8ec84). `ChatThemesRequestGate` skips a request for a hash that is in flight or was made less than 10 minutes ago; the request is unchanged, `forceUpdate` bypasses. Test: `ChatThemesRequestGateTests`.

## 13.2 (202611112014) — 2026-11-11 — c2a45687

TestFlight build (processed VALID, What to Test set). Previous TestFlight build: 202611092335 (7e19e458); changes since are `git log 7e19e458..c2a45687`: the attachment camera opening as a still frame when tapped right after the menu appeared (ff5ed8f6), message widgets off by default with the QuickJS-ng security fixes (5004b51d), Telegram for macOS 13.2's core patch and tgcalls 9bfb76f for the Mac merge (4c07ad72, 2370d9b0; no iOS behaviour change), and the broadcast player's bounded endpoint-id read (c2a45687, Mac-only code). Entries below.
What to Test: the attach menu's camera tile tapped at once, several times (live picture each time); a chat with a widget message shows the unsupported-content pill and nothing runs.

- **tgcalls 9bfb76f: Telegram for macOS 13.2's broadcast playback** (Telegram's `38dcea1`): the Mac's livestream part reader and video decoder, and in shared code an optional playout callback on `GroupInstanceDescriptor`, empty by default, so iOS is unchanged. TgVoipWebrtc compiles for the simulator.

- **Shared core: Telegram for macOS 13.2's core patch** (for the Mac 13.2 merge). Telegram's Mac 13.2 builds its core from Telegram-iOS release-13.2 plus `scripts/telegram-ios-macos.patch` (telegram-macos ec221d85, 18 files): MediaBox incremental data reads, a message's failure code (`OutgoingMessageInfoAttribute`, `notifyMessageFailure`, `failMessages`), audio file transcription (`AudioFileTranscription`), the group-call broadcast player (`GroupCallBroadcastFetcher`, `…Levels`, `…Platform`, `…VideoOutput`, `BroadcastPartSlice`) and two lines in `WalletWidgetCalls`. Applied with git's three-way merge; one conflict in `PendingMessageManager`'s upload failure, resolved to keep our failure log line and take upstream's helpers. Upstream's own code: what it sends to the server is upstream's. iOS: TelegramVoip, TelegramCore, Postbox and WalletContext compile for the simulator; the new `MediaBoxIncrementalDataTests` and `AudioFileTranscriptionTests` run with the round's build.

- **Security: message widgets off by default, and the widget engine's memory-safety fixes** (owner, 2026-11-11, after the public tg-widget-rce report: a bot's widget message runs its script when the chat shows it, with no tap, and the report shows native code execution in Telegram 13.2's QuickJS-ng on iOS 27). (1) `MessageWidgetLocalSettings.widgetsEnabled` is off unless turned on in Debug Settings ▸ "Message widgets"; the key is new (`nanogram.messageWidgets.enabled.2`) so an "on" stored under the old default does not carry over. Off, a widget message shows the unsupported-content pill and no script runs; a running widget stops when the switch flips (`MessageWidgetAvailability`). The widget harness turns widgets on at launch. (2) `third-party/quickjs-ng`: 21 security and crash fixes from TelegramMessenger/quickjs master (VENDOR.md change 14). The report's parser trigger still crashes the engine after them, which is why widgets stay off. Tests: `:QuickJSSecurityTests` (new; RED on the old engine under ASan, a heap-buffer-overflow), `:QuickJSHostTests`, `:QuickJSSmokeTests` pass with and without ASan; `LocalSettingsTests`, `CrashSentinelTests`, `DebugControllerEntriesTests` updated for the off default (run with the round's build).

- **Attach-menu camera opened as a still picture when tapped right after the menu appeared**. Tester screen recording 2026-11-11 on 202611092335 (which already had the Power Saving fix a1a85a32): paperclip, camera tile tapped ~0.5 s after the menu appeared, the full-screen camera showed a still frame for good (green camera dot on); reopening later worked. The app log of the same morning shows the tile's camera lifecycle on every open: `Init`, then `stopCapture` 1.1-1.3 s later (the tile is not on screen yet, `applyChatCameraCaptureUpdate` → `.stop`), then `startCapture` 0.2-1.7 s after that. `cameraTapped` only started the camera when the tile has no live preview (Power Saving), trusting that a live tile is already running; a tap inside that window handed a stopped camera to `CameraScreen`, which never starts a handed-over camera. Upstream 13.2 has the same gap; our picker may open it wider since its PhotoKit state moved off the main thread (a guess, not measured). Fix: the hand-off starts the camera unless the tile's last request was a start (`mediaPickerChatCameraHandOff(tileCaptureState:)`, ChatMediaSend `MediaPickerCameraCell.swift`; `MediaPickerScreen.cameraTapped` passes `chatCameraCaptureState`); starting a running camera is a no-op (`CameraContext.startCapture` returns when the session runs). Tests: ChatMediaSendTests `testChatCameraHandOffStartsTheCameraUnlessTheTileStartedIt`, `testChatCameraHandOffAlwaysEnablesThePreview` pass; MediaPickerUI compiles for the simulator. Not run on a device.

## 13.2 (202611092335) — 2026-11-09 — 7e19e458

TestFlight build (processed VALID, What to Test set). Previous TestFlight build: 202611082219 (489139c3); changes since are `git log 489139c3..7e19e458`, the entries below: the attachment camera's frozen preview in Power Saving (a1a85a32), the iPad display-link cap without its Debug switch (ae5063b1), and tooling (parity hook, tg testflight staging cleanup, app dSYM path). dSYMs: `~/Library/Application Support/Nanogram/dsyms/202611092335` (the app's as `Nanogram.app.dSYM.zip`).
What to Test: the attachment camera opened several times in a row with Low Power Mode on and off (live picture every time, also when closed and reopened quickly); on iPad, scrolling and animations still smooth.

- **tg testflight / tg device find the app's dSYM of a fresh build (tooling)**. Since the bundle became `Nanogram.app` (4f72a2e3, 2026-11-08), rules_apple writes the app's dSYM to `bazel-bin/Telegram/Telegram_dsyms/Nanogram.app.dSYM`; `archive_dsyms` only looked for `Telegram.app.dSYM` next to the ipa. The uploads of 2026-11-08 passed only because that worktree still had a `Telegram.app.dSYM` from 11-07 whose UUID matched; the first build in a fresh worktree (2026-11-09) stopped with "no Telegram.app.dSYM next to Telegram.ipa" before uploading. `archive_dsyms` now takes `Telegram_dsyms/Nanogram.app.dSYM` first and falls back to `Telegram.app.dSYM`, still refusing a dSYM whose UUID is not the app's. Tests: `tools/dev/tests/test_tg.sh` (fresh layout, and a stale `Telegram.app.dSYM` beside it). docs/profiling.md updated.

- **tg testflight removes its staging folder after an upload (tooling)**. `device_signing.py testflight` staged each build (archive, unpacked .ipa, signed package, ~1.9 GB) in `TMPDIR/testflight-*` and never removed it; 13 uploads on 2026-11-08 left 24 GB there and the next build stopped at the 25 GB free-space check (removed with the owner's OK on 2026-11-09). After a successful upload the folder is now removed (`finish_testflight_work`); `--export-only` keeps it (the signed package is the result) and a failed export or upload still keeps it for the logs. The dSYMs are unaffected: `tg testflight` archives them from bazel-bin before the upload. Tests: `test_device_signing` `TestflightWorkFilesTests`.

- **Attach-menu camera froze in Power Saving (upstream 13.2 bug our Power Saving rules expose)**. Tester screen recording 2026-11-09: chat → attach → camera tile → full-screen camera showed a frozen picture (green camera dot on, then off after closing and reopening) until it recovered seconds later; official Telegram on the same phone did not. Root cause: with Power Saving on, `energyUsageSettings.fullTranslucency` is false, so the chat camera tile has no live preview and `applyChatCameraCaptureUpdate` stops the camera and sets the tile preview's `isEnabled = false`. `cameraTapped` hands that same preview view to `CameraScreen` (`CameraHolder`) and only starts capture; `CameraScreen.setupCamera` never enables a handed-over preview (only `resumeCameraCapture` does), so the session ran behind a disabled preview connection. Official Telegram enters Power Saving only below its battery threshold; ours also follows Low Power Mode, a serious thermal state and iOS 27 reduced resource usage (`SystemEnergyConditionsMonitor`), so the path is common here. Fix: the hand-off always enables the preview (`mediaPickerChatCameraHandOff`, ChatMediaSend `MediaPickerCameraCell.swift`; used by `MediaPickerScreen.cameraTapped`). Test: ChatMediaSendTests `testChatCameraHandOffAlwaysEnablesThePreview` (passes; MediaPickerUI compiles for the simulator). Not run on a device. The story camera's two shutter sounds are unchanged upstream behaviour (dual mode captures on both cameras, each plays the system sound).

- **Pre-push parity hook (tooling)**. Same file as the Mac's `parity.py`: the hook now tells the Mac from iOS by the core gitlink in HEAD instead of `submodules/telegram-ios/.git`, so a Mac worktree without the core checked out no longer runs the iOS history-root check. No change for iOS pushes. Test: `test_parity`.

- **iPad display-link cap baked in; its Debug switch and the pacing log removed**. Owner, 2026-11-08. An iPad's shared display link always follows the rate its animations ask for (60 or 30 Hz when they need no more), as on the iPhone; upstream forced up to 120 Hz on every 120 Hz iPad. It had been on by default since 2026-11-07. Removed: `ExperimentalUISettings.tabletDisplayLinkFrameRateCap` (old blobs with its key decode), the Debug row (id 81 retired), `SharedDisplayLinkDriver.capsTabletFrameRate`, `startPacingLog`/`pacingLog` and `DisplayLinkPacingStats` (the pacing log only started when that switch was toggled), and `sharedDisplayLinkFrameRateRange`'s `capsTabletRate:` parameter and its 120 Hz tablet override. NANOGRAM-CHANGES.md updated. Tests: TabletSecondBatchTests (rewritten for the rule without the switch), DisplayTests, TelegramUIPreferencesTests, DebugSettingsUITests pass; app compiled.

## 13.2 (202611082219) — 2026-11-08 — 489139c3

TestFlight build. Previous TestFlight build: 202611082109 (6c74cf54); changes since are `git log 6c74cf54..489139c3`: the "send as" avatar button loads with the chat again (not a second later); composer: the emoji/sticker data build waits for a pause in typing, no redundant typing-attribute or caret writes per keystroke, and one log line per run of deletes (held-delete diagnostics). What to Test: holding delete, typing right after focus, the send-as button timing. Entries below.

- **Composer: held delete and typing stalls — likely causes removed, diagnostics added**. Owner's tester, 2026-11-08 20:46 (13.2, iOS 27.2): holding delete removed one character per hold (no key repeat), until starting a voice recording; the owner also saw freezes while typing. Not reproduced and not confirmed; research (code read of the whole per-keystroke path; the delete key, `attributedText` setter, typing-attributes refresh and input-state echo are upstream's) found no code that swallows a delete, and these candidates, all fixed without behaviour change: (1) the emoji/sticker panel data build (ours, ~250 ms on the main thread) could start 0.4 s after focus, in the middle of typing; on iOS 27 the system keyboard and its key-repeat timer run on our main thread, so it now also waits for 0.5 s without a text change (`InputDataStartGate.typingQuietPeriod`, `idleDecision(secondsSinceLastScroll:secondsSinceLastTextChange:)`; `ChatControllerNode.lastInputTextChangeTimestamp`, set when the input text object changes). (2) `refreshChatTextInputTypingAttributes` (both variants) wrote `typingAttributes` on every keystroke, twice; an unchanged set is no longer written (`setTypingAttributesIfChanged`). (3) `ChatInputTextView.attributedText`'s setter restored the caret after every write even when it had not moved; it now writes it only if it moved (a programmatic selection change cancels key repeat). Diagnostics: one log line per run of deletes, `Composer delete burst: count=N spanMs=M writes=K` (`DeleteBurstTracker`, `ChatInputTextView.deleteBurstLog` → Logger "Composer"): a held key with `count=1` got no repeats from the keyboard, `writes>0` means the composer rewrote text or caret during the run. Tests: InputDataStartGateTests (2 new), TypingAttributesRefreshTests (new, TextFormatTests), DeleteBurstTrackerTests (new, ChatInputTextNodeTests); ChatInputTextNodeTests, ChatEntityKeyboardInputNodeTests pass; app compiled.

- **"Send as" avatar button shows while the chat opens again, as in Telegram**. Owner, 2026-11-08: the button appeared about a second after a group or channel opened. Its list subscription was inside the deferred chat-open setup (`openSetup.run`, flushed the main-queue turn after `viewDidAppear`, so after the push transition), and the list is read from the database first, so deferring it bought little. The subscription in `ChatControllerLoadDisplayNode.swift` starts at load again (upstream's placement); the rest of the deferred setup (network-speed notices, global media/live-location panels, name colour, bot callback alerts, recorders, typing activity) stays deferred. No unit test (chat controller glue); compiled.

## 13.2 (202611081636) — 2026-11-08 — 976148f0

TestFlight build. Previous TestFlight build: 202611081505 (6a8d17ba); changes since are `git log 6a8d17ba..976148f0`: the back-swipe preparation no longer clears the contents of the screen beneath (the chat list came back without avatars and previews after chat → another screen → back) and the swipe always lays that screen out again; Sendable-clean `TempBox` slot and `RequestCoalescer` (Mac warnings gate). What to Test: the back-swipe switch on, navigation two levels deep and back, avatars/previews/highlights intact. Entries below.

- **Back swipe preparation no longer blanks the screens beneath (fix of 202611081505)**. Owner, 2026-11-08 16:14 (screenshot): after opening a channel, then another screen, then going back, the chat list showed no avatars and no media previews, and the tapped row kept its highlight. `releasePreparedPopController` (run when the stack changes) called `recursivelyClearContents()` on the prepared screen while it was off the hierarchy; contents set directly on layers (avatars, image nodes) are not redrawn when the screen comes back. The clear is removed (ASDK's own off-hierarchy handling stays as upstream), and `interactivePopBegan` always runs the bottom controller's `containerLayoutUpdated` again (the skip for a prepared screen saved little and could keep stale state). The preparation still finishes the screen's drawing ahead of the swipe. Switch unchanged (off by default). Tests: DisplayTests pass.

- **Shared-core warnings for the Mac package build (Postbox, TelegramCore)**. The Mac's warnings gate (counts may only go down) found one new Swift 6 concurrency warning each from this round: `TempBox`'s shared slot (`Atomic<TempBox?>` is not `Sendable` in the Mac's package build) and the global `peerPhotosRequestCoalescer`. `TempBox` uses a small `@unchecked Sendable` slot class with an `NSLock` (the pattern `StaleChannelDiagnostics` uses, warning-free on both builds); `RequestCoalescer` is `@unchecked Sendable` (all state is behind its `Atomic`). No behaviour change. Tests: PostboxTests, TelegramCoreTests pass.

## 13.2 (202611081505) — 2026-11-08 — 6a8d17ba

TestFlight build. Previous TestFlight build: 202611081305 (3b8bd9e5); changes since are `git log 3b8bd9e5..6a8d17ba`: chat close frees the emoji panel data off the main thread; the overscroll "next chat" avatar loads asynchronously; chat-list rows paging in under the finger do not decode media previews synchronously (switch, on); wallpaper pattern prewarmed for the first chat open (switch, on); back-swipe preparation of the screen beneath (switch, off); profile photo lists shared and cached for 60 s (dropped on any change or deletion), polls not refetched within a minute when unchanged, wallet TON Connect refresh throttled and WALLET_UNAVAILABLE remembered for 5 minutes; iPad floating keyboard in a floating window reserves no height; forced unread reindex at most every 6 hours; TempBox lock; memory and database-write diagnostics; keyboard A/B switch and keyboard probe removed. What to Test: smoothness of chat close/overscroll/chat-list scroll/first chat open, photo galleries/polls/wallet still current, iPad floating keyboard, the back-swipe switch, a 15-minute log with Frame hitches off. Entries below.

- **Review fixes for round 14 (Opus review of 96a19bfb..6b72b5aa)**. (1) Deleting a group or channel photo (its service message, `AvatarGalleryController` delete path) left the remembered photo list (60 s) showing it: `_internal_deleteMessagesInteractively` now drops the remembered lists before and after any interactive deletion (`peerPhotosCacheInvalidating`). (2) Back swipe preparation: the "prepared" marker now holds only while it names the same controller object (`preparedPopController ===`) and that controller stayed out of the hierarchy (`isStillPrepared`); `update(layout:…controllers:)` releases the preparation when the prepared screen is no longer the one beneath the top or is shown — a screen popped and pushed again, or a new controller at a reused address, no longer skips its layout. (3) iPad windowed-keyboard rule: the full-screen check ignores orientation (UIKit posts the new-orientation keyboard frame before the window layout rotates), test `test_windowedKeyboard_fullScreenMidRotation_isTheOldPath`. (4) CHANGELOG: back-swipe switch id 148, prewarm commit hashes after the replay. Tests: TabletLayoutTests, DisplayTests, TelegramCoreTests pass.


- **Chat open: the wallpaper pattern is rendered ahead of the first chat (Debug Settings "Chat open: prewarm wallpaper pattern", default on; branch perf/chatopen; not built or run, host tests written)**. Evidence: owner main-thread samples of 2026-11-08 (iPhone 17 Pro Max, iOS 27.2; `navigateToChatControllerImpl` stalls of 60.7 and 39.7 ms) and the earlier log of the first chat after launch: `WallpaperBackgroundNodeImpl.loadPatternForSizeIfNeeded` -> `patternWallpaperImageInternal` drew the pattern synchronously (~36 ms, "pattern cache miss (empty)", synchronous because `useSharedAnimationPhase || contents == nil`). What: `WallpaperPatternPrewarmer` (WallpaperBackgroundNode; 3c327d4f) renders the chat pattern on a utility queue into the same static `cachedPatternImages` shared slot, with the exact key the chat looks up (wallpaper, portrait screen size, `.aspectFill`, pattern colour from the wallpaper colours, no star gift) and the chat's own draw arguments (scale `min(2, screen scale)`), so the chat takes the existing "pattern cache hit" path and the pixels are the ones it would draw. `TelegramRootController` starts it 3 s after the root controllers exist and again 3 s after each chat wallpaper change (main app, iPhone only; pattern wallpapers only). A memory warning, a cancel or a newer request drops a render in flight, and the existing memory-warning observer empties the cache; a render never overwrites an entry the chat stored first. The decisions (portrait phone size, render or not, epoch) are in the Foundation-only `WallpaperPatternPrewarmPlan.swift`. Switch: `ExperimentalUISettings.chatOpenPrewarmWallpaperPattern` (a48e8f33), stable id 147 in the Chat open section. Per-chat wallpapers, plain/gradient wallpapers, iPad and landscape are not prewarmed (the chat draws them as before). Tests (not run): `//submodules/WallpaperBackgroundNode:WallpaperPatternPrewarmPlanTests`, `//submodules/DebugSettingsUI:DebugSettingsUITests` (`ChatOpenSwitchTests`, `DebugControllerEntriesTests`), `//submodules/TelegramUIPreferences:TelegramUIPreferencesTests` (`testChatOpenWallpaperPrewarmIsOnByDefaultAndTheChoiceIsKept`). Analysis of the other two stall paths (nothing more deferred, with reasons): `ChatControllerNode.init` (~20 ms) is a long tail of UIKit view creation for first-frame nodes (input panel buttons, glass views, navigation buttons, history list) with no sample above 1 per window; `reloadChatLocation` -> `NavigationContainer.update` -> `topTransition` is the push itself.

- **Back swipe: the screen beneath is prepared while the top one is idle (branch perf/backswipe; Debug Settings > List scroll > "Back swipe: prepare the previous screen", id 148, default OFF; tests written, not run by the agent)**. Evidence (r13an/log2.txt, 3 ms main-thread samples in frame stalls): `NavigationContainer.panGesture` -> `interactivePopBegan` took ~70 ms per swipe start (611 samples in 30 blocks): `ASDisplayNode _insertSubnode:belowSubnode:` ~158 samples (coordinator init inserting the previous node), `maybeCreateNavigationBarTransition` 59-97, and `recursivelyTriggerDisplayForLayer` -> `ASAsyncTransaction waitUntilComplete` ~112 (up to ~55 ms in one block). Finding: ASDK clears an off-screen node's contents one runloop after it leaves the hierarchy (`applyPendingInterfaceState`, non-range-managed branch), so every pop redraws the whole previous screen and `recursivelyEnsureDisplaySynchronously(true)` blocks on it; the bottom controller's `containerLayoutUpdated` also runs at that moment. Now, with the switch on, `NavigationContainer.schedulePopPreparation` (after an update, a transition's end, a focus change; 0.6 s idle delay, `NavigationPopPreparation.idleDelay`) lays the controller beneath the top one out for the layout the swipe would use (`bottomControllerLayout`, extracted from `interactivePopBegan`), sets its node's size and starts its display without waiting (`recursivelyEnsureDisplaySynchronously(false)`), all off the hierarchy. `interactivePopBegan` then skips the repeated `containerLayoutUpdated` when the same controller and layout were prepared, and its synchronous display call finds no pending transactions, so `waitUntilComplete` returns at once (the wait is kept as is for anything really pending). Only one level; the drawn contents are freed on a memory warning (`recursivelyClearContents`), when the stack changes, or when the switch goes off. Not done: the lazy navigation bar transition (`takeView` moves the live bars, so building it on the first `.changed` could show a different first frame) and pre-inserting the node (entering the hierarchy starts visibility work for a screen under the top one). Setting `NavigationPopPreparationSettings.isEnabled` is written from `SharedAccountContext`; key `backSwipePreparePreviousScreen` (default false). Tests: `//submodules/Display:DisplayTests` (`NavigationPopPreparationTests`), `//submodules/TelegramUIPreferences:TelegramUIPreferencesTests`, `//submodules/DebugSettingsUI:DebugSettingsUITests` (strict ordering).

- **Foreground memory growth of ~1 MB/s: instrumentation, plus a stale-window guard (branch perf/memidle; tests written, not run by the agent; cause NOT proven)**. Evidence (owner's iPhone 17 Pro Max log of 2026-11-08): footprint rises ~1 MB/s in the foreground (11:33:40-11:34:30 +70 MB; 11:43:02-11:43:25 746 to 768 MB), flat in the background; malloc in use 337 to 788 to 1293 MB while graphics/media stay under 4 MB, so it is live heap that goes cold. Code audit result: nothing in the always-on diagnostics is unbounded. `MainThreadSampler` allocates its 4096-sample ring once (calloc, 4 MB); `FrameHitchMonitor` keeps at most 4 captures per window (`keptStallLimit`) and windows are keyed by name; `Logger` flushes its buffers at 64 KB or 1 s; `DiskWriteAccounting` keys are bounded categories. The "idle" windows are not idle: the log shows an open chat with a peer typing about once a second and a new message every few seconds, each one fetching `photos.getUserPhotos(limit 100)`, `getHistory`, `getPeerDialogs`. Added (1) `FrameHitchStats.isExpired`: a window still open after 60 s is closed, so a lost `end` cannot keep the top-rate display link ticking all session; (2) counters in the 30 s `Memory detail` line: `frame hitch captures` (count, bytes), `frame hitch open windows` (bytes 1 = link running), `frame hitch reports pending`, `frame hitch windows expired unended`, `log queued entries` (log calls queued on the logger queue and not run, with their message bytes), `log write buffers`, `animation cache open items` (opened `AnimationCacheItemAccessor`s with the bytes of the compressed files each one holds), `animation renderer item contexts`, and a live-instance count `CameraContext`. Tests (not run): `//submodules/Display:DisplayTests` (`test_aWindowThatNeverEndsExpires`, `test_diagnosticsFollowOpenWindows`), `//submodules/TelegramCore:TelegramCoreTests` (`testBacklogCountsBufferedBytesUntilTheyAreWritten`). Next step: read these counters in the next log to see which one grows.

- **Removed Debug Settings "Keyboard probe (plain text view)"**. It answered its question (a stock `UITextView` gets the same in-process keyboard and 24-41 ms focus as the chat editor; see the A/B removal entry). The `kb=` state on keyboard hitch windows stays. Owner's request.

- **iPad: floating keyboard no longer lifts the chat input panel in a floating window / Slide Over (branch fix/ipad-floating-keyboard; tests written, not run by the agent)**. Evidence: owner's screenshot (iPad Pro 13" M4, iPadOS 27.2, landscape 2000x1500): app in a small floating window at the right (about x 1410-1985, y 50-1320), the system keyboard floating at the bottom left (about x 35-515, y 810-1295), outside the window; the input panel sat at about y 770 with empty wallpaper below. Cause: `WindowContent.swift` keyboardWillChangeFrame handler. The floating frame is not screen-wide, so it takes the `else` of the `width == UIScreen.main.bounds.width` test; `minKeyboardY` is the frame's minY converted to the window (760) and `screenHeight` becomes the window height (1270). The frame's maxY (1295) is not below that, so the final `else` of the `keyboardFrame.maxY < screenHeight` test runs: `keyboardHeight = screenHeight - minKeyboardY` = 510 pt, although the keyboard is not docked and covers nothing of the window. Nothing checked that the keyboard is docked or overlaps the window. Now `tabletWindowedKeyboardHeight` (TabletLayoutRules.swift) decides on a regular-width tablet whose window does not cover the screen: the height is the overlap of the window-space keyboard frame with the window, only if the keyboard's bottom is at the screen bottom (5 pt) and it spans the window's width; otherwise 0. It returns nil (old path, unchanged) for phones, compact width and full-screen windows. The hide, empty-frame and hardware-bar overrides still run after it. Tests: `//submodules/Display:TabletLayoutTests` (`test_floatingKeyboard_*`, `test_dockedKeyboard_*`, `test_phoneAndCompactWidth_areNotHandled`, `test_keyboardHide_emptyFrame_reservesNothing`). Not verified on device.

- **Wallet: TON Connect sessions refreshed at most every 5 minutes on foreground; WALLET_UNAVAILABLE remembered across launches for 5 minutes (Nanogram wallet; shared core: WalletContext; branch perf/request-dedup; tests written, not run by the agent)**. Evidence: log of 2026-11-07 (93 min, 64 foreground flips): `wallet.tonConnectGetSessions` sent 65 times; `wallet.getState` answered `WALLET_UNAVAILABLE` 4 times (`wallet_state_failed`, WalletContextImpl.swift), at 12:02:10, 13:04:20, 13:04:44 and 13:06:28; the MT service instance differs between them, i.e. each was a new launch (the in-process `WalletStateRetryPolicy` already waits an hour after that answer). Fix: (1) `WalletTonConnectRefreshThrottle` (WalletRequestMemory.swift): `updateTonConnectEnvironment(refresh:automatic:)` skips a refresh that is only caused by foreground / network resume / the feature becoming available when the last successful `tonConnectGetSessions` is under 300 s old and no wallet screen is open (`walletScreenCount == 0`); explicit refreshes (Apps screen `refreshTonConnectSessions`, wallet identity change, pending disconnects) and the first one after launch are unchanged; `resetTonConnect` forgets the last success. (2) `WalletUnavailableMemory`: the time of a `WALLET_UNAVAILABLE` answer is stored in UserDefaults (`nanogram.wallet.stateUnavailableAt.<namespace>`); a context created within 300 s seeds `serverStateRetryPolicy` with an authoritative failure at that time, so no automatic `getState` goes out; opening a wallet screen still calls `resetForUserDemand()` and asks at once; a success deletes the key. Tests (not run): `//submodules/WalletContext:WalletRequestMemoryTests`. Risk: a TON Connect request that arrives while the app is backgrounded and is not delivered by an update waits for a wallet screen or up to 5 minutes before the foreground refresh finds it.

- **Polls: a poll that comes back on screen unchanged within a minute is not fetched again (shared core: TelegramCore; branch perf/request-dedup; tests written, not run by the agent)**. Evidence: log of 2026-11-07 (93 min): `messages.getPollResults` sent 359 times for 45 messages, up to 19 for one poll. Cause: `AccountViewTracker.updatePolls` starts a `pollRefreshSignal` for every open (not closed) poll each time it enters a history view (chat open, scroll back, view rebuild) and that signal fetches immediately. Fix: `PollFetchThrottle` (State/PollFetchThrottle.swift, 60 s window). `fetchPoll` records (message id, stored poll, time) when it sends; the FIRST fetch after a poll enters a view is skipped when a record younger than 60 s exists and the stored `TelegramMediaPoll` equals the recorded one. The 30 s refresh of a poll that stays on screen, the 10-fetch burst after a deadline, and everything else run as before. A changed stored poll (vote or retract applied locally, update arrived, poll closed) fetches at once; `forgetPollFetch` (vote, add/delete option, opening the voters list) clears the record. Closed polls were never fetched (`pollMessages` filters `isClosed`), so nothing changes for them. Tests (not run): `//submodules/TelegramCore:TelegramCoreTests` (`PollFetchThrottleTests`).

- **Profile photo list: one request instead of hundreds (shared core: TelegramCore + PeerAvatarGalleryUI; branch perf/request-dedup; tests written, not run by the agent)**. Evidence: log of 2026-11-07 (93 min, 64 foreground flips): `photos.getUserPhotos(limit: 100)` sent 282 times, 236 for one user, every 3-4 s in places, three identical within 2 ms (11:35:39); FLOOD_WAIT_3/5/10/14/35 answered five times. Root cause: `peerInfoProfilePhotos` (AvatarGalleryController.swift) re-ran `fetchedAvatarGalleryEntries` on EVERY `postbox.peerView` emission, and a peer view also changes with presence, typing and unrelated cached data. Fix: (1) the view is mapped to `PeerInfoProfilePhotosInputs` (peer, first entry, public/fallback photo) and `distinctUntilChanged` drops emissions that cannot change the list; (2) `_internal_requestPeerPhotos` (RequestUserPhotos.swift) goes through `RequestCoalescer` (Utils/RequestCoalescer.swift): concurrent callers for the same (account, peer, current-photo fingerprint) share one request, a successful list is reused for 60 s; a failure is never remembered and a `FLOOD_WAIT_n` / `*_WAIT_n` answer blocks that key for n s. The key contains the peer record's current photo resource ids, so a changed photo never meets an older list; `updatePeerPhotoInternal`, `updatePeerPhotoExisting` and `removeAccountPhoto` (set, replace, delete, make-main, contact photo) drop the cache before they start, on every progress event and when they end. The request itself is unchanged. Tests (not run): `//submodules/TelegramCore:TelegramCoreTests` (`RequestCoalescerStateTests`, `RequestCoalescerSignalTests`), `//submodules/PeerAvatarGalleryUI:PeerAvatarGalleryUITests` (`PeerInfoProfilePhotosInputsTests`). Residual: a photo deleted by someone else (not the current one) can show up to 60 s late on a gallery opened again.

- **Review fixes for this round (Opus review of cc9c2ea2..5ffba8b4)**. (1) WAL write logging no longer blames the wrong code: frames found at a connection's first commit (left by the previous process or an extension) are accounted but not attributed to the first transaction, and the per-commit lines say "the WAL grew by N frames … (another process's writes in between count too)" instead of "appended" (`SqliteWriteAccounting.swift`, `TransactionWriteAttribution.swift`; tests `testInheritedFramesAreNotAttributedToTheFirstTransaction`, updated wording asserts). (2) The `Memory purge (background)` line is flushed right after it is written (it used to reach disk only on resume, so a memory kill in the background lost it). (3) The unread-reindex marker is written only after the reindex ran (a launch killed before it keeps the next one due). (4) `TempBox.shared` uses a guard with a message instead of a force unwrap. (5) Doc comment of `chatListAsyncMediaWhileScrolling`. Tests: `//submodules/Postbox:PostboxTests`, `//submodules/TelegramUIPreferences:TelegramUIPreferencesTests` pass.

- **Chat list: media previews of rows that page in while scrolling decode in the background (branch perf/main-thread-round4; Debug Settings > List scroll > "Chat list: async media previews while scrolling", id 146, default ON; tests written, not run by the agent)**. Evidence: main-thread stack during a frame stall (202611081037, r13an/log2.txt, window keyboardHide, 69.8 ms gap): `ChatListNode.dequeueTransition` -> `ListViewImpl.replayOperations` -> `insertNodeAtIndex` -> `ChatListItem.configureNewNode` -> `ChatListItemNode.asyncLayout` closure (ChatListItem.swift) -> `ChatListMediaPreviewNode.updateLayout(size:synchronousLoads:)` -> `TransformImageNode.setSignal` -> `mediaGridMessagePhoto` -> `CGImageSourceCreateThumbnailAtIndex`/`CGContextDrawImage`, ~40 ms for one row. Finding: rows that scroll into view without a transaction are already built with `synchronousLoads: false` (ListView.swift fillMissingNodes from the scroll path); the synchronous flag reaches a row only through a transaction's `.PreferSynchronousResourceLoading`, which `ChatListSynchronousLoadingPolicy` sets for the first content, a folder switch, a scroll-to-item and any insert of more than 3 rows. The last one also fires when more chats page in under the user's finger. Now, with the switch on, `prefersSynchronousResourceLoading` returns false for that bulk-insert case while `ChatListNode.isTracking || isDeceleratingAfterTracking` (new parameters `userIsScrolling`, `asyncWhileScrolling`, defaults keep the old result); the first transition, a filter change and a scroll-to-item stay synchronous, so the first screen is not blank. New `ChatListRowExperiments.asyncMediaWhileScrolling` is written from `SharedAccountContext` (launch and on change), key `chatListAsyncMediaWhileScrolling` in `ExperimentalUISettings` (default true, decoded with `?? true`). Cost: such a row's preview shows its placeholder/blur for a frame. Not verified: that this bulk-insert rule is the one that fired in the sampled stall (the log does not carry the transaction's options). Tests: `//submodules/ChatListUI:ChatListUITests` (`ChatListSynchronousLoadingPolicyTests`), `//submodules/DebugSettingsUI:DebugSettingsUITests` (`DebugControllerEntriesTests`, strict ordering with the new row), `//submodules/TelegramUIPreferences:TelegramUIPreferencesTests` (`ExperimentalUISettingsDefaultsTests`).

- **Overscroll "next chat" control no longer draws its avatar synchronously on a scroll tick (branch perf/main-thread-round4; not tested by a unit test: UI-only; not run by the agent)**. Evidence: main-thread stack during a frame stall (202611081037, r13an/log2.txt): `PhysicsScrollEngine.sampleTick` -> `CoreVirtualListView.handleUserScroll` -> `ChatHistoryListNodeImpl.maybeUpdateOverscrollAction` -> `ChatOverscrollControl` body -> `AvatarComponent` -> `AvatarNode.ContentNode.setPeer` -> `ImageNode.setSignal` -> `peerAvatarImage` -> `generateImage`, ~28 ms. The control is created at the first pixel of overscroll (`offset < -0.1`), so that render sat on the first tick of the pull. `AvatarComponent` now calls `setPeer(..., synchronousLoad: false)`: the image (and the letter placeholder) is produced off the main thread and lands a frame later; the control starts collapsed and grows over 94 pt of pull, so it is still small then. Same pixels once shown. No switch (a one-frame-late avatar on an almost collapsed control); say so if you want one.

- **Closing a chat frees the emoji/sticker panel data off the main thread (branch perf/main-thread-round4; tests written, not run by the agent)**. Evidence: main-thread stack sampled during a frame stall (build 202611081037, r13an/log2.txt): `ChatControllerImpl.__deallocating_deinit` -> `ChatControllerNode.deinit` -> ivar destroyer -> `ChatEntityKeyboardInputNode.InputData` -> `EmojiPagerContentComponent` -> `ItemGroup` (thousands of items), ~75 ms.
