Now `ChatControllerNode.deinit` hands the item groups of the emoji and sticker pagers (`panelItemGroups`, `contentItemGroups`: strings, files, colors, no views or disposables; checked) to `OffMainRelease.release` (new, `ChatEntityKeyboardInputNode/Sources/OffMainRelease.swift`), which keeps them alive on a utility queue for 0.5 s and then drops them there. The delay makes sure the node's own references (promise value, `inputMediaNodeData`, destroyed right after `deinit`) are gone first, so the background queue frees the last one. The components themselves (context, input interaction) still free on main; they are light. Behaviour unchanged. Tests: `//submodules/TelegramUI/Components/ChatEntityKeyboardInputNode:ChatEntityKeyboardInputNodeTests` (`OffMainReleaseTests`: last reference freed off main, caller dropping its reference does not free).

- **Memory growth diagnostics and a purge at background / memory warning (iOS; branch fix/memory-growth; tests written, not run by the agent; the leak itself is NOT found)**. Evidence: owner's iPhone 17 Pro Max, builds 202611081037/202611081219, log of 2026-11-08. `Memory consumption` rose 365 -> 942 MB in 13 min and 279 -> 1406 MB in 62 min (peak 1545 MB); `Background diagnostic` showed malloc in use 337 -> 788 -> 1293 MB with 1.0-1.1 GB compressed, so the growth is live heap that goes cold, not graphics (0.8 MB) or media (4 MB). Two relaunches without a terminate line follow 942 MB and 1406 MB (jetsam while suspended); the log has no `Memory warning`. Findings: (1) the heap climbs about 1 MB/s while the app is in the foreground with nothing else logged (11:33:40-11:34:30, 11:43:02-11:43:25, 11:44:20-11:44:55: +5 MB per 5 s), and does not climb in the background; (2) the camera opens (`CameraContext Init`; 9 in the log, the 5 checked came within 2 s of a foreground) line up with a +150-250 MB spike (451 -> 700, 677 -> 815, 831 -> 1000 MB), of which part stays (the floor after each is higher); (3) the floor rises after every foreground whether or not the camera opened. Static review: ChatRowPrepCache (8 entries, 5 s TTL), ChatRowViewPool (4 per kind, 8 total), ListRowPool (8), MediaGridThumbnailStore (32 decoded pages, trimmed on warning and background), PeerAvatarImageCache (purged on warning and background), the FrameHitch sampler (4096-sample ring), Logger buffers and the animation renderer's pixel buffer pools are bounded and the first four drain on a memory warning; none can hold hundreds of MB. Not changed: no clearly unbounded cache of ours was found, so no cap was invented. Added: `MemoryProbeRegistry` / `LiveInstanceToken` / `MemoryDetailReport` / `MemoryPurgeReport` / `MallocZoneUsage` / `MemoryRelief` (submodules/BackgroundDiagnostics); `AppDelegate` logs `Memory detail:` every 30 s with Log to File on (process memory, thread count, the four largest malloc zones, live-object counts for ChatControllerNode and ChatHistoryListNode, chat controller count, URLCache memory, registered cache sizes; computed off the main thread), and at each enter-background and memory warning runs every registered purge plus `malloc_zone_pressure_relief` and logs `Memory purge (...)` with the footprint before and after. The purge only returns free heap pages and drops caches that opt in; memory in use is not touched, so it will not fix a retention by itself. Nothing sent to Telegram changes.

- **Unread-count reindex at most every 6 hours instead of every launch (shared core: Postbox)**. Upstream forces `reindexUnreadCounts` at every Postbox open (`//#if DEBUG` around `setShouldReindexUnreadCounts(value: true)` is commented out): the owner's log shows `reindexUnreadCounts took 0.03-0.05 s` on the Postbox queue at each launch and the init transaction appending 2000-2900 WAL frames (~10 MB), and iOS relaunches the app many times a day (jetsam). The forced reindex now runs when the reindex version changes (as before) or when the last forced one is ≥ 6 h old (`PostboxUnreadReindexSchedule`, marker file `unread-reindex-timestamp` in the Postbox base path; a clock that went backwards does not block), else logs `reindexUnreadCounts skipped`. Unread counters still get the periodic repair. Tests: `UnreadReindexScheduleTests` (RED: type missing, GREEN; `//submodules/Postbox:PostboxTests` all pass).

- **Log-only: every large database write is named in the log at once, and every VACUUM is bracketed (shared core: Postbox; branch fix/db-rewrite; tests written, not run by the agent)**. Why: the owner's 202611081037 log shows process 4292279788 writing 353.5 MB in its first report (`sqlite.wal.postbox/db 177.3 MB (10 writes)`, `sqlite.checkpoint.postbox/db 176.1 MB (1 writes)`) for a 178 MB account database: about every page of it went through the WAL once and was checkpointed back. Distinct pages copied by the one checkpoint equal the whole file, so one commit (or one commit plus the init reindex) touched every page; VACUUM, SQLCipher rekey and a table rebuild are the operations that do that. The log has no VACUUM, upgrade or cleanup line, and no Postbox open lines at all for that process (the 7 minutes between 11:46:46.994 and 11:54:22 are missing), so the cause could not be proven from it. Candidates ruled out in code: the nightly VACUUM only runs from the BGProcessingTask and never while the app is active; `openPostbox` has no migration at userVersion 25; the per-launch init transaction (Postbox.swift:2201, `reindexUnreadCounts`, forced on every launch) appends only 2000-2900 frames (about 10 MB) in the other launches' logs. Not ruled out: a first commit that finds a large WAL left by the killed previous process (the accounting counts every frame in the WAL at a connection's first commit as written now). What now gets logged and flushed (`postboxLogSync`) immediately: (1) any single commit that appends 16 MB or more of WAL, from the WAL hook (`SQLite <db>: one commit appended N frames ...`) and, with the call site, from `internalTransaction` (`Postbox large transaction: File.swift:line appended N frames ...`); (2) a first commit that finds 16 MB or more already in the WAL (`... held N frames at this connection's first commit; they were written before it opened`); (3) a checkpoint that copies 16 MB or more (`a checkpoint copied N pages`); (4) VACUUM begin and end with caller, path, page counts and duration (`SqliteValueBox: VACUUM begin/end ... from <caller>`), flushed before the statement runs. Files: SqliteWriteAccounting.swift (`committed`/`checkpointed` return a notice), SqliteValueBox.swift (hook, idle checkpoint, `vacuum(caller:)`), TransactionWriteAttribution.swift (`largeCommitLine`), Postbox.swift (`internalTransaction`). Nothing changes in what is written or sent.

- **TempBox shared instance behind a lock (shared core: Postbox; hardening)**. iPad MetricKit (iPadOS 27.2, builds 202611021642/202611031231): two Intents-extension SIGSEGVs in `TempBox.__deallocating_deinit`, called from `WidgetIntentHandler.init` → `TempBox.initializeShared` while other handlers did the same on other threads; the unguarded global `sharedValue` was assigned concurrently and the old value released twice. The trigger was already removed by 268c99ed (one extension environment per process); `sharedValue` is now an `Atomic<TempBox?>` so a second caller can no longer crash any process. Upstream code. Test: `TempBoxConcurrentInitTests` (200 concurrent initializations). The Mac gets it with the next core bump.

- **Removed Debug Settings "Keyboard: leave the keyboard window alone (A/B)" (branch fix/remove-keyboard-window-ab)**. Result of the owner's run on 202611081219 (2026-11-08 13:05-13:07): with the switch on the keyboard was still drawn in our process (`kb=inProcessWindow:UIRemoteKeyboardWindow keyViews=40 layoutStar=yes`, unchanged), so reading `keyboardSceneDelegate.keyboardWindow` is not what keeps it there; and the switch broke the chat layout (screen recording 13:07: the keyboard came up but the input panel stayed under it, because `Window1` takes the keyboard's position from that window). The "Keyboard probe (plain text view)" showed the same for a stock `UITextView` (focus 24-41 ms, 40 key views in process), and a minimal standalone UIKit app on the iOS 27.2 simulator (scene life cycle, one `UITextView`) also had `UIKeyboardLayoutStar` and 32 `UIKBKeyView`s in its own process: on iOS 27 the key views live in the app process for any app, so the keyboard's own cost (layout creation on show, key drawing in commits) is not specific to us. The stored `keyboardWindowAccessOff` key is ignored from now on (the window is always read again). The probe and the `kb=` diagnostics stay.

## 13.2 (202611081305) — 2026-11-08 — 3b8bd9e5

TestFlight build. Previous TestFlight build: 202611081219 (75a23b99); changes since are `git log 75a23b99..3b8bd9e5`: notification tone "None" keeps the vibration (a near-silent sound is substituted in the Notification Service Extension for unmuted, non-silent message pushes whose resolved tone is None), and the app bundle/executable are named Nanogram (`Nanogram.app/Nanogram`; the dSYM stays `Telegram.app.dSYM`), with tg/signing/icon tooling following. What to Test: tone None vibration (ring switch on and off; muted chats and silent messages unchanged), the app updating in place under the new name. Entries below.

- **Notification tone "None" keeps the vibration (iOS; branch fix/notification-none-haptic; tests written, not run by the agent)**. Bug: with the tone set to None the push has no `sound` key, `content.sound` stays nil in the Notification Service Extension, and iOS fires the Taptic Engine only for a notification that has a sound, so the vibration vanished with the tone. Now `NoneToneHaptic` (new, `Telegram/NotificationService/Sources/NoneToneHaptic.swift`) decides in the extension: a message push (`.poll` action, category not `t`/`st`/`str`) with no or an empty sound, not silent, with title/subtitle/body, gets the bundled near-silent `nanogram-none-haptic.caf` when the account's own settings resolve to tone None and notifications on. Resolution mirrors `resolveNotificationSettings` (topic settings, then chat settings, then the private/group/channel default; channel vs supergroup from the stored peer; expired mute counts as unmuted). It reads those settings in one postbox transaction, in `NotificationService.swift` just before the action switch (the action block became the body of `processAction`; not re-indented to keep the upstream diff small). Anything else is unchanged: muted chats, "Send without sound", a sound already in the payload (default, `0.m4a` ringtone, cloud tone), unknown peer or empty settings (falls back to no sound), reactions, stories, calls, the quiet placeholder, in-app handling (its vibration is a separate setting). Nothing server-visible changed. The sound is `Telegram/Telegram-iOS/Resources/notifications/nanogram-none-haptic.caf`: 0.25 s, mono, 44.1 kHz, 16-bit PCM, noise of +-2 LSB (about -84 dBFS), made with Python `wave` and `afconvert -f caff -d LEI16`; bundled by the existing `AppResources` glob. Review fixes: secret chats map to the private-chat defaults and read the settings of their associated peer; the substituted content is also saved as the best-attempt content (timeout fallback vibrates); the lookup is held in a disposable with a weak self; a log line records each substitution. Needs a device check: that iOS vibrates for it (vs skipping a near-silent file), with the ring switch on and off. Tests: `//Telegram/NotificationService:NotificationServiceTests` (`NoneToneHapticTests`).

- **The app bundle and its executable are now Nanogram (`Nanogram.app/Nanogram`; the app dSYM keeps the Bazel target's name, `Telegram.app.dSYM` with `DWARF/Telegram`, and the Nanogram executable's UUID; branch chore/executable-nanogram)**. Why: the owner's whole-phone Activity Monitor snapshot listed the app as "Telegram" (954 MB), indistinguishable from the official client; MetricKit, crash reports, CPU profiles and spindumps name the executable too. `Telegram/BUILD` `ios_application` gets `bundle_name = "Nanogram"` (rules_apple 5.4 derives the executable name from it; Info.plist `CFBundleExecutable` is `$(EXECUTABLE_NAME)`). The Bazel target stays `//Telegram:Telegram` and the archive stays `bazel-bin/Telegram/Telegram.ipa`, so upstream's CI and Make.py are untouched; bundle id, app group and data are unchanged, so an installed build updates in place. Tooling: `tools/dev/tg` uses `APP_NAME="Nanogram"` and `SIM_APP` (simulator builds land in `bazel-bin/Telegram/Telegram_archive-root/Payload/Nanogram.app`: rules_apple puts a tree-artifact bundle whose name differs from the target under `<target>_archive-root`; a leftover `bazel-bin/Telegram/Telegram.app` from older builds is stale), simulator app path, `tg run`, `tg device` install, `archive_dsyms` UUID check), `device_signing.py` finds the single `.app` in `Payload/` (`payload_app_name`, so an older Telegram.app .ipa still signs), `AddAlternateIcons.sh` (run by the build on the archive) finds the bundle instead of assuming `Telegram.app`, docs updated. Symbolication: the app image is now named `Nanogram` in reports and matches `Telegram.app.dSYM/Contents/Resources/DWARF/Telegram` by UUID (first release build 2026-11-08 stopped at `archive_dsyms`, which looked for Nanogram.app.dSYM; fixed); the frameworks keep their names (TelegramUIFramework etc.). Tests: `tools/dev/tests/test_device_signing.py` (18, incl. `PayloadAppNameTests`), `tools/dev/tests/test_tg.sh` (all pass), both run.

## 13.2 (202611081219) — 2026-11-08 — 75a23b99

TestFlight build. Previous TestFlight build: 202611081037 (edb083a4); changes since are `git log edb083a4..75a23b99`: launch order files regenerated for the 13.2 launch path, the frame hitch log builds its stack reports off the main thread, keyboard investigation tools (in-process keyboard state per keyboard animation, "Keyboard probe (plain text view)", "Keyboard: leave the keyboard window alone (A/B)", a typing window), database size and nightly optimisation decisions in the log. What to Test: the keyboard probe and the keyboard-window A/B (for logs), typing, cold launch. Entries below.

- **Database size and nightly optimisation decisions in the log (shared core: Postbox; logging only)**. The owner asked how big the database is and whether the nightly optimisation runs; the logs could not say. The nightly `BGProcessingTask` (`<bundle>.cleanup`, on external power, at a time iOS picks) ran 2026-11-08 01:03-01:04:37 (cache reindex, then `optimizeStorageIfNeeded`), but a database that was checked recently or is not fragmented logged nothing. Now: `DB size at <file>: X MB, WAL Y MB` once per database open (file attributes only, no SQL), `SqliteValueBox: not fragmented, X MB, P% free pages (VACUUM from 25%)`, and `Postbox: storage optimization not due, last check N h ago (every 168 h)`. Rule unchanged: VACUUM at most weekly, when ≥ 25% of pages are free, live data ≤ 256 MB, never while the app is active, interrupted when the background time ends. The Mac gets the lines with its next core bump. Tests: `//submodules/Postbox:PostboxTests`.

- **Keyboard investigation tools (branch perf/hitch-round3; all Debug Settings ▸ List scroll, off by default)**. Why: WWDC23 "Keep up with the keyboard" says the iPhone keyboard runs in its own process since iOS 17, yet our main thread builds the keyboard layout and draws key caps (202611081037 log); a tester's video comparing App Store, Telegram and Nanogram describes "micro-stutters" while typing in Telegram (the screen recording runs at a fixed ~55 fps in all three apps, so it cannot show 120 Hz stutter). (1) Keyboard windows' summaries end with `kb=inProcessWindow:<class> keyViews=N layoutStar=yes|no remoteHosts=N` (`describeKeyboardHosting`, SharedAccountContext; walks the keyboard window only) or `kb=noKeyboardWindow`. (2) "Keyboard probe (plain text view)" (id 144): a stock `UITextView` focused/dismissed under `FrameHitchMonitor.measure(alwaysLog:)` with the same `kb=` line, to tell an app-wide cause from the chat editor's configuration. (3) "Keyboard: leave the keyboard window alone (A/B)" (id 145, `ExperimentalUISettings.keyboardWindowAccessOff`, `KeyboardWindowAccess.isDisabled`): `AppDelegate.keyboardWindow` returns nil, so nothing asks `keyboardSceneDelegate.keyboardWindow` (read on every keyboard notification and navigation layout); tests whether that read keeps the keyboard in our process. Cost while on: the keyboard does not follow a swipe-down and overlays are not lifted above it. (4) A `typing` window (0.5 s after each text change in `ChatTextInputPanelNode.chatInputTextNodeDidUpdateText`). Tests: `DebugControllerEntriesTests` (ids 144-145), existing Display/TextInputPanel/Preferences suites.

- **Frame hitch log: stack reports are built off the main thread (branch perf/hitch-round3)**. The 202611081037 keyboard log showed the instrument perturbing its own measurement: 132 of the 415 samples in `keyboardShow` stalls (and 21 at chat scroll ends) were `FrameHitchMonitor.finish` → `MainThreadSampler.reportForCapture` → `appendTree` (128-frame trees with `dladdr`) for the window that had just ended — a keyboard hide followed by a show recorded the hide's report building as the show's hitch, which inflated the hide→show pairs to 70–83 ms. The summary line is still written on the main thread; captures are still taken there (a memcpy); the text trees are built and logged on a utility queue. Test: `FrameHitchMonitorTests.test_measureLogsASlowCallWithItsStack` (RED: report built on main, GREEN). Analysis of the same log, with system frames symbolicated against the device's iOS 27.2 symbols: of the remaining keyboard stall time, ~30–40% is the system keyboard itself (`UIKeyboardLayoutStar createLayoutFromName`/`setToInitialKeyplane` on every show, `UIKBKeyView displayLayer` in commits), 6–12% first-responder/input-view machinery, 3–6% our layout passes; the focus tap (25–53 ms) is entirely `becomeFirstResponder` → keyboard layout creation.

- **Launch order files regenerated for the 13.2 code (Telegram/OrderFiles, release link only)**. The 2026-10-31 lists predate the 13.1.2 and 13.2 merges: against the release binary of 202611080941, the share of their names still present had dropped (TelegramUI 954 → 882, TelegramCore 1036 → 992 since build 202611021745), and functions 13.2 added to the launch path were not listed at all. `tools/dev/orderfile.py --runs 3` on the logged-in "iPhone 17" simulator (iOS 27.2) with a fresh debug build of 569510a4 (owner's OK to launch, 2026-11-08). Names present in the 202611080941 release binary, old → new list: TelegramUI 882 → 1,224, TelegramCore 992 → 1,122, Postbox 772 → 821, SwiftSignalKit 138 → 174, MtProtoKit 115 → 221. Names the linker cannot find are ignored, so the link cannot break; the effect is fewer page faults on a device cold launch, not measured yet (MetricKit `timeToFirstDraw` of the next builds).

## 13.2 (202611081037) — 2026-11-08 — edb083a4

TestFlight build. Previous TestFlight build: 202611080941 (5030b678); changes since are `git log 5030b678..edb083a4`: no GameController hardware-keyboard query on iPhone chat open and history drag, and the frame hitch log's second round (keyboard windows in the right handler, sampler always on, 128-frame stacks, timed focus tap). What to Test: chat open from the list, the keyboard measurement run. Entries below.

- **iPhone: no GameController hardware-keyboard query on chat open and history drag (branch perf/hitch-round2, commit "iPhone: do not query GameController…")**. Evidence: the first frame hitch log (202611080941, 2026-11-08 10:16:01) — a 98.7 ms stall when a chat was opened from the list, 8 of its 29 main-thread samples in `hardwareKeyboardIsConnected()` (`GCKeyboard.coalesced`, TabletSystemRules.swift) called from `ChatListControllerImpl` for `shouldActivateInputOnChatOpen`; `shouldDismissKeyboardOnHistoryDrag` made the same query on every chat history drag. The answer only matters on a tablet, so both rules now take `hasHardwareKeyboard` as an `@autoclosure` evaluated only for a tablet with the input requested/focused; phones are unchanged in behaviour. Test: `TabletSystemRulesTests.test_theHardwareKeyboardIsOnlyQueriedWhereItMatters` (RED 4 queries on phone paths, GREEN 0; `//submodules/Display:TabletSystemTests`).
- **Frame hitch log, second round (branch perf/hitch-round2)**. The `keyboardShow`/`keyboardHide` windows were hooked into the rotation observer by mistake (the same `updateLayout(inputHeight:)` line), so the first log had no keyboard windows; they now open in the `keyboardWillChangeFrame` handler. The main-thread sampler now runs for as long as the switch is on (a stall that begins before a window opens, such as the tap that raises the keyboard, has samples), keeps up to 128 frames per sample (UIKit's event and keyboard paths passed 48 and lost their roots, splitting the tree; ring now ~4 MB) and prints the whole depth. New `FrameHitchMonitor.measure(_:_:)` times one synchronous call and logs `window=<name> syncMs=…` with the stack when it took ≥ 25 ms; used for `keyboardFocusTap` around `makeInputFirstResponder()` in `ChatTextInputPanelNode.ensureFocusedOnTap()` (the first log showed an 84 ms stall there, inside UIKit's keyboard bring-up). Test: `FrameHitchMonitorTests.test_measureLogsASlowCallWithItsStack`.

## 13.2 (202611080941) — 2026-11-08 — 5030b678

TestFlight build. Previous TestFlight build: 202611080042 (1488aa3f); changes since are `git log 1488aa3f..5030b678`: the chat message list does less main-thread work per scroll frame, the keyboard path drops a whole-window responder search and moves the emoji data prewarm past the keyboard animation, the new "Frame hitches" debug log (frame timing with main-thread stacks for chat scroll, lists and the keyboard), and the list scroll pacing log split into drag and fling with the A/B switch state. What to Test: chat scroll, keyboard open/close, the frame hitch log, the chat list row-reuse A/B. Entries below.

- **Chat message list: less main-thread work per scroll frame (branch perf/chat-scroll-tick; tests were not run by the agent)**. Behaviour is unchanged; only the cost per `handleUserScroll`/fling frame drops. (1) `planRowPrefetch` (prepare-layout-ahead, `CoreListChatHistoryBackend`) no longer rebuilds keys, `ListViewItemLayoutParams` and the `cache.retain` list on every frame: `ChatRowPrefetchPlanGate` (ChatRowPrep) compares the loaded range, direction, content width, `prefersSynchronousResourceLoading` and an `entries` revision (bumped by `didSet` on every write) with the last run and skips when equal; it still re-plans at least every 0.25 s so state the signature cannot see (interaction epoch, time to live, a failed job) is never lost. (2) `CoreVirtualListView.withPresentedFrames` computes `modelToPresentedViewportDelta` (`animationController.now()`, `CALayer.convertTime`, dictionary lookups) once per pass; `immediateDisplayedItemRange` and `forEachVisibleItemNode` use it (same `.presented` frames, same visible set; `forEachVisibleItemNode` now reads all frames first, then calls the closure). `loadedFrame(of node:)` (used by `itemNodeFrame` for every visible row) takes an O(1) path: the node's superview is its `CoreListNodeHostView` and `CoreVirtualListView.loadedIndex(ofItemView:)` confirms it is a loaded row via a view-to-index map rebuilt once per window revision; anything else falls back to the old scan. `item.content.map` in `ChatHistoryListNode` and the per-call delta in `itemNodeFrame` are not changed. (3) `installAttachmentFlightTracks` memoizes per attachment layer (`AttachmentFlightComputationMemo`): the key is the full `AttachmentOffsetMap` (`isIdentical(to:)`: band, anchor, content base, edge, floating, height, yield partners and gap), the trajectory's sample buffer identity (the memo retains it) and the coordinate shift; with the stored track's duration and begin time and a still-present animation, an unchanged track skips `composedKeyframe` and the array compare; a rigid result is memoized too. Any miss takes the old path. Tests: `ChatRowPrefetchPlanGateTests` (ChatRowPrepTests), `ChatScrollTickGeometryTests` and `AttachmentFlightMemoTests` (CoreListChatScrollTickTests, new target in CoreList/BUILD). iOS only.

- **Keyboard open/close path: three main-thread savings (branch perf/keyboard-path; tests written, not run by the agent)**. (1) `Window1` no longer prints `hide with …` on every animated interactive keyboard dismiss (WindowContent). (2) The first-responder search over the whole window view tree, run on every `keyboardWillChangeFrame`, every touch-down while the keyboard is up (`panGestureBegan`), the keyboard-type observer and `KeyboardManager.updateSurfaces`, now goes through `FirstResponderLookup` (Display): it keeps the last responder found weakly and returns it while it is still `isFirstResponder` and a descendant of the searched root; otherwise the full depth-first search runs and refreshes the cache. The input accessory height is recomputed from the responder's ancestors on every hit (deepest `WindowInputAccessoryHeightProvider` from the root down, else the passed height), so it follows the depth-first result exactly. `getFirstResponderAndAccessoryHeight`, `UIView.findFirstResponder()` and `KeyboardManager`'s search all use it. (3) With "Emoji panel: defer first-responder handoff / heavy layers" on (default), the emoji and sticker data build (its combine runs on the main queue) no longer starts at text-input focus, when the keyboard animation starts: `ChatTextInputPanelNode.inputPanelsFocusPrewarmRequested` (new, focus only) makes `ChatControllerNode` schedule the start `InputDataStartGate.focusPrewarmWait` (0.4 s) later through the idle path (which also waits for a quiet history list). A touch-down on the panel button and an explicit demand still start at once, and the gate keeps the start single. Switch off: unchanged. Tests: `FirstResponderLookupTests`, `InputDataStartGateTests` (focus delay, demand during the delay). iOS only.

- **Debug Settings ▸ List scroll ▸ "Frame hitches: log with main-thread stacks" (new, off by default; branch perf/hitch-catcher)**. Why: the chat message list in 13.x is `CoreVirtualListView` (not `ListView`), so the scroll pacing log never saw it; the owner reports lag in chat scrolling and when the keyboard opens and closes, and 10 s CPU profiles average away single slow frames. While a window is open, a `CADisplayLink` at the screen's top rate times every frame (`FrameHitchMonitor`, Display): a hitch is a callback more than 1.5 planned frames late, where the planned frame is the previous callback's `targetTimestamp - timestamp`, so ProMotion rate changes are not hitches; gaps over 1 s are a paused link. Windows: `chatScroll` (CoreListChatHistoryBackend drag begin to scroll end), `list:<type>` (ListView gestures), `keyboardShow`/`keyboardHide` (from `keyboardWillChangeFrame` in WindowContent for the animation duration + 150 ms, so the layout it triggers is included). At the window end: one `[FrameHitch] Frame hitches: window=… frames hitches hitchMs msPerS longestMs ab=…` line, then for the 4 longest stalls (≥ 25 ms and ≥ 3 frames) a `Frame hitch stack` with the main thread's merged call tree during the stall. The stacks come from `MainThreadSampler` (CpuProfiler): while a window is open it suspends the main thread every 3 ms to walk its frame-pointer chain into a 4096-sample ring (~12 s, 1.6 MB allocated on first use; nothing is allocated while the thread is suspended); a stall copies its samples (`captureFrom:to:`) and the text tree with a Binary Images footer is built when the window ends. The `ab=` context now also carries `chatRows=<chatScrollExperiments>`. `ExperimentalUISettings.frameHitchLog` (new key, default false), Debug Settings id 143. Tests: `FrameHitchMonitorTests` (steady frames, planned rate change, stall with its extra time, one-frame slip without stack, longest stalls kept, paused link, summary line), `DebugControllerEntriesTests`. iOS only.

- **Debug Settings ▸ "List scroll: log frame rate" now counts the drag and the fling apart and names the A/B switches (branch perf/scroll-pacing-phases)**. The owner's logs of 202611072118 and 202611080042 showed the chat list at ~93 Hz average with ~23% "late" frames and a longest gap of exactly 50 ms in most flings, in both builds. 50 ms is 20 Hz, the bottom of ProMotion's slowdown at the end of a fling: after the finger lifts, UIKit lowers the scroll's rate on purpose (120, 80, 60 … 20 Hz), and the old line counted those planned slow frames as late, so it could not show a hitch nor tell the row A/B apart. `ListViewScrollPacing` now keeps two parts: `drag(...)` (finger down, ProMotion at full rate; `late` = interval over 1.5 frames, as before) and `decel(...)` (after `scrollViewDidEndDragging(willDecelerate: true)`; `jumps` = interval over two frames and 2.5× the previous interval, a stall rather than the planned step-down, which is at most ~1.5× per step). Each line ends with ` ab=lazyRows=… rowReuse=… upstreamRate=… a11yNow=…` (`ListViewScrollPacing.context`, set in `SharedAccountContext` from the current settings), so runs with the switches on and off are told apart from the log alone. Log-only; off by default. Tests: `ListViewScrollPacingTests` (slowdown is neither late nor a jump, a stall in the fling is a jump, a stall while dragging is late, switch state appended). Also removed a duplicated 202611080042 entry left by the replay with a placeholder commit. iOS only.
## 13.2 (202611080042) — 2026-11-08 — 1488aa3f

TestFlight build. Previous TestFlight build: 202611072118 (6ac2025b); changes since are `git log 6ac2025b..1488aa3f`: chat list rows
(lazy rare subnodes on by default; row reuse behind a switch, off), the MtProtoKit first-response timeout, the round-video
transcription collapse fix, and the switches now on by default (chat open, emoji panel, chat scroll rows, iPad display link cap),
all below. What to Test: chat list smoothness and the row-reuse A/B, "Updating…" after switching apps, round video transcription.

- **Chat list rows: optional row reuse (Debug Settings ▸ List scroll ▸ "Chat list rows: reuse rows", off by default; branch perf/chatlist-row-cost, commits 382e9e45 and 518b65ac)**. Creating a `ChatListItemNode` costs more than the 8.3 ms frame at 120 Hz, so nearly every row entering the list costs a frame. With the switch on, a row that leaves a main chat list, a folder or the archive list is kept in a pool of 8 per list (`ListRowPool` in Display) and reused for the next rows of that list. `ListViewImpl.removeItemNodeAtIndex` (non-animated branch, row still had an index) calls `itemNodeRemovedObserver`; the row is not usable at once, because `.Remove` is also emitted for rows that come back in the same update (a chat moved to the top, a deleted row's disappearing placeholder, an animated scroll's temporary nodes). The pool therefore admits a row only in a batch deferred to the next main-queue turn (`DispatchQueue.main.async`; `Queue.mainQueue().async` would run inline on main and reset a row that the same replay inserts again), after `supernode == nil && !containsItemNode(node)` and `prepareForRowReuse()`. `ChatListItemNode.rowReuseRefusal()` (pure) refuses rows that are not plain and at rest: created with the switch off, tainted (context menu, story tap, flash highlight), not an ordinary chat item (`ChatListRowReuseFacts`: topics, communities, monoforum, side lists, search, editing, selected, promo, archive row, loading), video avatar, topics, edit/reorder/placeholder children, removal animation, highlighted, swiped or revealing, typing, non-default zPosition/sublayer transform/clipping, or any CA animation in the layer subtree (a removed animation would still run its completion). `prepareForRowReuse()` resets the list-engine state (`ListViewItemNode.prepareForPoolReuse`), disposables and the presence timer, removes the optional views the apply adds, resets `AvatarNode` (story ring, image, letters, loads) and `ImageNode` (no cross-fade from the previous avatar), resets both `ChatListBadgeNode`s (`resetForRowReuse`: stored content, background image, text, animations, frames, visibility, so the next apply does not return early on equal content and leave a stale pill), restores the 21 fixed subnodes to the alpha, hidden, transform and frame captured at `init` (captured only when the switch is on at creation), and clears caches and accessibility data. The reused row goes through the new-node path of `ChatListItem.nodeConfiguredForParams` (`layoutParams == nil`, not animated), so it renders as a new row. The pool is drained on theme or strings change, memory warning, entering the background and when the switch is turned off. Rows created with the switch off or in another lazy-subnode mode are never reused. `ExperimentalUISettings.chatListRowReuse` (new key, default false), Debug Settings id 142, applied in `SharedAccountContext`. Tests (not run by the agent): `ListRowPoolTests`, `ListViewItemNodeRemovalPolicyTests`, `ListViewItemNodePoolResetTests`, `ImageNodeReuseTests`, `AvatarNodeReuseTests`, `PeerPresenceStatusManagerTests.testCancelStopsAPendingRefresh`, `ChatListRowReuseEligibilityTests`, `ChatListRowReuseLedgerTests` (Mirror ledgers for `ChatListItemNode`, `ItemListRevealOptionsItemNode`, `ListViewItemNode`, `AvatarNode`, `ContentNode`, `ImageNode`), `ChatListRowReuseResetTests`, `ChatListBadgeNodeReuseTests`. iOS only.
- **Chat list rows: rare parts are built on first use (Debug Settings ▸ List scroll ▸ "Chat list rows: lazy rare subnodes", on by default; branch perf/chatlist-row-cost, commit 1ab7a640)**. Measured reason (10 s CPU profile of the main thread while scrolling the chat list on an iPhone at 120 Hz): 71 samples in `ListViewImpl.updateVisibleItemsTransaction` inserting rows, 20 of them subtree materialization at the first `.layer` touch in the row apply (`ChatListItem.swift` `transition.updateAlpha(node: strongSelf)`), plus more Core Animation commit time that grows with layers per row. `ChatListItemNode.init` built ~22 subnodes, most hidden in an ordinary row. Now eight of them (community shadow, message status, pinned / hidden-peer / muted icons, unread and mention badges, online dot) are not added to the row's hierarchy at creation; `showLazySubnode(_:)` inserts each at the exact z-position it has when added in `init` (pure placement in `ChatListLazySubnodes.swift`: nearest lower attached child of `mainContentContainerNode`, `textNode` never an anchor) the first time the apply needs it visible. Plain writes (`image`, `isHidden`, `frame`, `alpha`) still happen on a detached node (Texture keeps them as pending state), so the badge width animation start values are unchanged; only layer touches (`transition.update*(node:)`, `.layer.shouldRasterize`, `onlineApply` of an offline dot) are skipped while detached, through `updateLazyFrame` / `updateLazyAlpha` / `isLazySubnodeAttached`. The online dot is attached before anything is added to `avatarNode` above it (live badge, avatar badge, timer badge). A shown part is never detached again. `ChatListRowExperiments.lazyRareSubnodes` is read when a row node is created; off builds today's tree. `ExperimentalUISettings.chatListLazyRareSubnodes` (new key, default true), Debug Settings id 141, applied in `SharedAccountContext`. Tests (not run by the agent): `ChatListLazyPlacementTests`, `ChatListLazySubnodeHierarchyTests` (ledger of every node property, tree identical to flag-off for any show order, loaded layer order, online dot below overlays, pending alpha/frame survive attach), `ExperimentalUISettingsDefaultsTests`, `DebugControllerEntriesTests`. iOS only.
- **"Updating…" after coming back from another app: a fresh connection that stays silent is dropped after ~3 s instead of 12 s** (owner's log 2026-11-08 00:07:17: TCP to the DC connected in 50 ms, the first requests got no byte back, `response timeout` fired after 12.25 s, the reconnect answered in 50 ms; getDifference itself answers in 60-250 ms in every case of the week's log; branch fix/mtproto-first-response-timeout). MtProtoKit `MTTcpConnection` measures the TCP setup time (`connectToHost` → `connectionInterfaceDidConnect`) and, while a direct connection (no SOCKS / MTProto proxy) has not received a byte yet, arms the response timer with `MTTcpResponseTimeoutForSend` (new `MTTcpResponseTimeoutPolicy`): max(3 s, 10 × setup time) plus the usual 1 s per 12 KB, never more than the usual 12 s rule; after the first byte the usual rule applies unchanged. The log line says "response timeout (first response, connected in N ms)". On a slow but live link this can cost one extra reconnect that repeats the same requests; nothing new is sent. Shared core: reaches the Mac with the next core pin. Tests: `MTTcpResponseTimeoutPolicyTests`.

- **"iPad: Display Link 120 Hz Cap" is on by default** (owner decision 2026-11-07: checked on an iPad; branch perf/ipad-cap-default). An iPad's shared display link now asks only for the rate its animations need (60 or 30 Hz), as on the iPhone, instead of always 120 Hz; no change on iPhones. `ExperimentalUISettings.tabletDisplayLinkFrameRateCap` defaults true under the new key `tabletDisplayLinkFrameRateCapDefaultOn`. The pacing log no longer starts from the property's `didSet` (the launch value would have logged every 10 s on every iPad): `SharedDisplayLinkDriver.startPacingLog()` is called by the Debug Settings switches of the two rate rules. Test: `ExperimentalUISettingsDefaultsTests.testTheIPadDisplayLinkCapIsOnByDefaultAndTheChoiceIsKept`.

- **Round video in a channel no longer stays an empty circle after its transcription collapses** (upstream bug, owner's recording 2026-11-07; branch fix/instant-video-transcription-collapse). `ChatMessageInteractiveInstantVideoNode.animateTo` (expand to the voice-style layout) hides the node and sets `canAttachContent = true` (lets go of the player) in the completion of a 0.2 s animation; a collapse inside that time (`animateFrom`, which shows the node and sets `canAttachContent = false`) ran first, and the late completion then hid it again. New `InstantVideoFileTransitionGate`: each transition takes a token and the expand completion applies only if no newer transition started. Tests: new target `ChatMessageInteractiveInstantVideoNodeTests` (`InstantVideoFileTransitionGateTests`).

- **On by default: "Chat rows: prepare layout ahead", "Chat rows: reuse row views", "Chat open: defer non-visible setup", "Emoji panel: defer first-responder handoff / heavy layers"** (owner decision 2026-11-07 after running them on the iPhone; branch perf/defaults-on). `ExperimentalUISettings` defaults true / mask 3, stored under new keys (`emojiPanelDefersHeavyWorkDefaultOn`, `chatOpenDeferSetupDefaultOn`, `chatScrollExperimentsDefaultOn`) because the whole struct is saved on every change and the old keys hold an off nobody chose; the old keys are no longer read. Debug Settings texts say "Default: on". Kept off, as rulings: "Advanced ducking (iOS 17)" (8f72d1c2 turned it on, reverted in the next commit: with `.mixWithOthers` another app's audio may reach the peer, review 2026-11-04, not yet checked on a call; the owner has it on to check), "Message widgets: wallet" (the pay/sign path is untested with a funded wallet), the three "List scroll (A/B vs Telegram)" switches (they exist to compare against our defaults; the log writes a line per scroll) and "iPad: Display Link 120 Hz Cap" (no effect on iPhone, untested on iPad). Tests: `ExperimentalUISettingsDefaultsTests`, `EmojiPanelSwitchTests`, `ChatOpenSwitchTests`, `ChatScrollSwitchesTests`.

## 13.2 (202611072118) — 2026-11-07 — 6ac2025b

TestFlight build. Previous TestFlight build: 202611071437 (f059025a); changes since are `git log f059025a..6ac2025b`: the
native-glass switch removed (d2d465e2), the list-scroll A/B switches and scroll pacing log (950a43e0), and the Display
accessibility test host fix (6ac2025b), all below. What to Test: the chat-list scroll A/B (three runs, send logs) and the
single glass look.

- **Test infrastructure: the Display accessibility test host is uninstalled from every `BAZEL_TEST_` simulator before a run** (branch perf/list-scroll-ab). `uninstall_test_host.sh` still matched "BAZEL_TEST_iPhone 17_26.5" after the runners moved to iOS 27.2, so it uninstalled nothing and `DisplayAccessibilityTests` ran the new test bundle against the previous host build; with `ListViewImpl` gaining a stored property the stale host crashed ("Index out of range" in `ListViewImpl.neighbors(at:)`, 3 tests). Now any `BAZEL_TEST_` simulator matches, so a runtime change in `test_simulator.bzl` cannot silently disable it again.

- **List scroll A/B against Telegram: three Debug Settings switches, all off by default** (owner request 2026-11-07, chat-list scrolling feels short of 120 Hz; branch perf/list-scroll-ab). A read-only comparison of everything on the chat-list scroll path against 13.2 (5083532f) found ListView's scroll path identical (31-line diff: `transferVelocity` at display rate) and our data, row and glass changes lighter than upstream; the only two of ours that act during a scroll are the shared display link's 60/30 Hz rule (3cd4aadf) and deferred row accessibility strings. "Display link: Telegram's rate" switches the first to upstream's rule (`sharedDisplayLinkFrameRateRange(…, followsUpstreamRate:)` → `upstreamSharedDisplayLinkFrameRateRange`; also starts the existing pacing log, now with `upstreamRate=`). "List rows: accessibility text at once" sets `ListViewDeferredAccessibility.forcesImmediate`. "List scroll: log frame rate" adds `ListViewScrollPacing`: per ListView gesture (drag begin → drag end or deceleration end) it counts `scrollViewDidScroll` intervals as frames (a gap over 100 ms is a finger rest, not a late frame) and logs `ListView scroll pacing: list=<type> frames avgHz late longestMs`. Settings keys `displayLinkUpstreamRate`, `listAccessibilityImmediate`, `listScrollPacingLog`. Tests: `TabletSecondBatchRulesTests` (upstream rule, ours kept when off), `ListViewDeferredAccessibilityTests.test_forcesImmediateRunsAtOnce`, new `ListViewScrollPacingTests`, `ExperimentalUISettingsDefaultsTests` (off by default, round trip), `DebugControllerEntriesTests` (order with all on).

- **Glass: the "Glass: native (no Telegram clamp)" Debug Settings switch is removed; Telegram 13.2's look is the only one** (owner decision 2026-11-07, branch fix/remove-native-glass-switch). The switch only changed the look: both modes run the same blur and refraction, and the clamp is one clamp of a number in UIKit's luma callback, so the native mode saved no battery or GPU. `+load` now always installs the luma clamp (iOS 26 and 27, when UIKit's backdrop class is found); `EffectSettingsContainerView.nativeNoClampDefaultsKey` / `isNativeNoClampRequested(in:)` and `GlassFixedLook.isNativeRequested` / `setNativeRequested` are gone, as is DebugSettingsUI's dependency on GlassBackgroundComponent. A stale `nanogram.glass.nativeNoClamp = YES` in a tester's defaults is ignored. The "…glass off" measurement switches stay. Tests: `GlassFixedLookTests` (switch tests removed), `GlassGroupsSwitchesTests` (section without the switch); GlassCapture no longer passes the key.

## 13.2 (202611071437) — 2026-11-07 — f059025a

TestFlight build of the Telegram-iOS 13.2 merge. Previous TestFlight build: 202611061452 (33d7d094); changes since are
`git log 33d7d094..f059025a` (the 13.2 import 5083532f and merge 7b112389, plus everything below). Release-round
notes: the push check now runs on the installed iOS 27.2 simulator (31 runner pins), the composer's six known legacy
bugs are expected failures, xctestrunner is patched for expected failures, and the media editor's Metal suites skip
under the runner and pass through the export harness (35/35 on iOS 27.2). The release build also needed
f059025a (AppBundle imported outside DEBUG).

- **Disk writes: gift grids cache at the displayed pixels and convert only cells that stay; cancelled conversions leave no temp file; unchanged preferences and cache entries are not rewritten; the Postbox WAL checkpoints at 3000 frames** (branch perf/disk-writes-gifts-wal, on int6; not built or run, tests added; evidence: MetricKit disk-write exception and `disk-writes.json` of the owner's iPhone 15 Pro, 2026-11-06/07: 1.45 GB and 3.65 GB a day against iOS's 1 GB limit). Commits 0d3605af, 3ec84e1e, fcaee948, 944b73b0.
  - Gift animation cache (0d3605af; `AnimationCacheWritePolicy` new in `LottieAnimationCache`, `InlineStickerItemLayer`, `GiftItemComponent`): `animationCache` was 2708 MB in 3731 writes (726 KB each) on 2026-11-07 while browsing the resale store (`GiftItemComponent` mode `.generic`: 88 pt icon). The cell passed twice the size as the point size and the cache renders at 2x, so each gift was cached at 352 px (keyframe-only, every frame intra) while a 3x screen shows 264 px and a 2x screen 176 px. `InlineStickerItemLayer` takes `displayPointSize`; `AnimationCacheWritePolicy.pixelSize` caps the cached pixels at `ceil(displayed points * screen scale)` (264 px on the owner's phone: 56% of the pixels; 176 px on a 2x phone: 25%). Every cell that came into view also started a full conversion at once, including those a fling passed in a fraction of a second; grid cells (`.generic`, `.profile`, `.select`, `.thumbnail`, `.grid`, `.preview`) now pass `cacheStartDelay` 0.35 s, which wraps the cache's `fetch` in `AnimationCacheWritePolicy.deferred`: the cache calls `fetch` only on a miss, so a cached item never waits, the first frame is not delayed, and a cell gone before 0.35 s writes nothing. `.header`, `.upgradePreview`, `.buttonIcon`, `.tableIcon` and every other `InlineStickerItemLayer` user keep size and start (both parameters default to nil/0). Frame rate (every second source frame) and the settled look are unchanged; the cache key now carries the smaller size, so gifts cached before this build are converted once more at it. Tests: `AnimationCacheWritePolicyTests` (`//submodules/TelegramUI/Components/LottieAnimationCache:LottieAnimationCacheTests`).
  - Cancelled conversions (3ec84e1e; `DCTAnimationCacheImpl`): a conversion whose subscribers were all gone still finished into a temp file the cache then ignored, which stayed in the temp directory. `AnimationCacheItemWriterImpl.finishInternal` now fails a cancelled writer (file removed) and the `get` completion removes the file of an item nobody waits for. Test: `DCTAnimationCacheCancelledConversionTests` (`//submodules/TelegramUI/Components/DCTAnimationCacheImpl:DCTAnimationCacheImplTests`).
  - Postbox no-op stores (fcaee948; shared core): `PreferencesTable.set` and `ItemCacheTable.put` (via `PostboxImpl.putItemCacheEntry`) compare with the stored bytes and write nothing, report nothing to views and leave the transaction empty (no transaction-state-version bump) when they are identical. Peers, cached peer data and ordered lists already did this. Test: `PostboxIdenticalStoreWriteTests`.
  - Postbox WAL (944b73b0; shared core): `walAutocheckpointFrames` 1000 -> 3000 and `walJournalSizeLimit` 8 -> 24 MB. The checkpoint counter of the 202611060203/202611061452 builds counted WAL frames, not distinct pages (the page-counting fix is in this Unreleased section), so its 493 MB/day overstates what reached the database file; the next build reports the real number. `synchronous=NORMAL`, `checkpoint_fullfsync` and the hook unchanged. Tests: `SqliteValueBoxConnectionTests` (`testAutocheckpointRunsPastTheThreshold`, `testNoCheckpointBelowTheThreshold`, the journal limit test); run `//submodules/Postbox:PostboxTests`.

- **Crash in the resale gift store while scrolling fixed** (fdb5bdc2; `GiftStoreScreen.swift`, `GiftOptionsScreen.swift`; upstream bug, unchanged in 13.2). MetricKit from TestFlight 202611061452 and 202611060203: three identical SIGTRAPs at `ComponentHostView.swift:47` (`precondition(!isUpdating)`): the screen's update set the scroll view, UIKit sent `scrollViewDidScroll`, `updateScrolling` called `ResaleGiftsContext.loadMore()`, which pushed state synchronously into the content component, whose `state.updated` re-entered the root host's update. `loadMore()` from the scroll callback now runs on the next main-queue turn (`justDispatch`, as upstream does in `SelectCraftGiftScreen` and `PeerInfoGiftsPaneNode`); the gift transfer list had the same inline call and gets the same change.
- **Video sticker VP9 probe no longer traps a process without the shared logger** (`VideoStickerHardwareDecoding.swift`): the probe and its stats reporter log through `Logger.sharedIfInstalled?`; `Logger.shared` asserts when none is installed, which crashed the media editor export harness (debug build) on its first video sticker. The app always installs the logger, so it logs as before.
- **MediaEditor export no longer traps when no source produced a frame (not built; `MediaEditorExportTests` run on the simulator)**. `MediaEditorVideoExport.encodeVideo` force-unwrapped `mainInput` when it called the composer; an image export whose image could not become a texture (no Metal device) reached it with nil and aborted the process ("Unexpectedly found nil"). It now logs, marks the writer's video input finished and fails the export through the existing frame-failure path (`frameProductionTimedOut`, status `.failed(.writing)`). Commit: see `git log -- submodules/TelegramUI/Components/MediaEditor/Sources/MediaEditorVideoExport.swift`.

- **Call engine sync with Telegram 13.2: tgcalls and webrtc forks (branch calls/sync-13-2; not built or run, host tests named below)**. Pins: tgcalls 57b914d8 -> ad275cac (`nanogram-shared`, 5 commits + records), webrtc 86cd593c -> 779c4aa7 (`nanogram-security-fixes`, merge of Telegram's 590a8b23). Telegram's tgcalls c1de0882 is not public, so each change comes from the public `dev/android` cherry-picks of Telegram's own commits where they exist and is re-implemented where not. Fork records: tgcalls `CHANGELOG.md`/`NANOGRAM-CHANGES.md` ("Sync with Telegram 13.2"), webrtc `CHANGELOG.md`.
  - webrtc: 590a8b23 (NetEq `Normal` crossfade overrun, CVE-2026-9119) has the same `normal.cc`/`normal.h` bytes as our 0ee3826, checked with `git diff` of both blobs; merged (not rebased, no force-push) and `normal_unittest.cc` takes upstream's. The other 11 fork fixes are untouched (the merge differs from 86cd593 only in that test file). Mac: its `tg_owt` already carries the same fix (97d355ae, df7a4d32).
  - tgcalls af52b5d + 1d574df (dev/android 12ab3b3, d96660e = development 8d5e7e0, c5ef736): 11.0.0, 14.0.0 and 18/19 now read `network_sctp_t1_init_ms`/`_t1_cookie_ms`/`_max_backoff_ms` (only `InstanceV2Impl` did), values outside 1-60000 ms or beyond int keep the 400/400/750 ms defaults, every connection logs its values. 59a614e (dev/android 545f5ae = c1de0882): `network_reflector_resolve_remote_candidate_ip` defaults on in 7-9/12/13, 11.0.0, 14.0.0 and 18/19; only an explicit `false` from the server turns it off. 1e99bc9: `ContentNegotiationContext::removeOutgoingChannel` indexed the descriptions vector with the other vector's bound, so a camera turned off before the peer answered returned as a second video channel. 51dbd05: CompatImpl (14.0.0) uses `ExternalSignalingConnection` under `network_signaling_nosctp` (Android's 14.0.0 always does) and mute sets the sender's `encodings[0].active`, re-applied after every negotiation step.
  - `submodules/TgVoipWebrtc/BUILD`: targets `content_negotiation_test`, `signaling_sctp_options_test` (Telegram's 13.2 definitions). Tests to run: `//submodules/TgVoipWebrtc:signaling_sctp_options_test`, `//submodules/TgVoipWebrtc:content_negotiation_test`, `//third-party/webrtc:neteq_normal_test` (ASan flags in its header). `signaling_sctp_options_test`'s logic was run on the host here (`ok`); the sources of all changed engine files compile against this tree's headers.

- **Lint cleanup of the Telegram 13.2 code: no force unwraps/casts/tries or trapping Int32 timestamp arithmetic on the lines the push guardrail checks (branch fix/lint-13-2; not built or run)**. The pre-push guardrail (`tools/dev/lint/tglint.py`) blocked pushing int6 on 499 findings, all on lines that came verbatim from the 13.2 import (5083532f): 370 `int32_timestamp_arithmetic`, 110 `force_unwrapping`, 18 `force_cast`. Behaviour is unchanged for valid input; invalid input no longer traps.
  - Timestamps: `x + N` on Int32 became `Int32(clamping: Int64(x) + N)` (`AccountStateEventHub`, `StateReplay+Peers`, `ReplyThreadHistory`, `TruthWorld`, `Postbox` test) or `Int64(date) + 60 > Int64(serverTime)` (`UpdateInterpreter+Typing`). The StateSync tests use a new `StateSyncTestCase.baseDateOffset(by:)` instead of `self.baseDate + N`.
  - `dict[k] == nil ? dict[k] = [x] : dict[k]!.append(x)` became `dict[k, default: []].append(x)` (and `default: [:]` for nested dictionaries) in `UpdateInterpreter`, `StateReplay+*`, `SyncSnapshot`, `ChannelPolling`, `PeerInfoProfileItems`, `PeerInfoSettingsItems`.
  - `as!` became `as?` with a guard (`continue`, `return`, or an empty fallback cell); `try`-less `!` on Optionals got the same fallback the surrounding code uses. Impossible cases that cannot return: `SyncSnapshot.initialStateWithPeerIds` asserts and falls back to a zero state; `ChatMediaEditorScreen`/`WallpaperMediaEditorScreen.node` and `MediaGridLayoutLayer.init(layer:)` use `preconditionFailure` with a message instead of a bare cast; `MediaPickerScreen` ObjC `TGMediaSelectionContext` inits keep an explicit non-optional type.
  - `MediaEditorComposerEntity.prerenderTextTransformations` now returns an optional (callers skip the entity); `AnimatedStickerDirectFrameSource` is created before the `QueueLocalObject` (on the same queue).
  - Build-system `WidgetIDL` throws its own `IDLError` for unknown interfaces/dictionaries.
  - To build/test: TelegramCore (and its StateSync tests), Postbox tests, AvatarNode, BrowserUI, Display, InstantPageUI, MediaPickerUI, TelegramUI (ChatController, ChatControllerLoadDisplayNode, TelegramRootController), CameraScreen, ChatMessageBubbleItemNode, ChatMediaEditorScreen, ChatMediaSend, CoreListDemoTests, InteractiveTextComponent, MediaEditor, MediaGrid, MessageWidget, PeerInfoScreen, PeerInfoVisualMediaPaneNode, ShareScreen, StaticGlassBackground, StoryContainerScreen, Tests/WidgetHarness, build-system/WidgetIDL.

- **New photo picker grid (MediaGrid, Telegram 13.2): iCloud lane off, background filler paced, store capped and cleared, request lessons from the old picker applied** (branch perf/mediagrid-paced, on top of int6; owner decisions of 2026-11-07; not built or run on a device, tests added; MediaGrid suites run with xcodebuild, not Bazel)
  - Filler pacing (A): `ThumbnailFillPacing` (new, `MediaGridPhotoLibrary`) lets the thumbnail filler run only while a library picker is open (`MediaGridPhotoLibraryDataSource` takes a lease in `init`, gives it back in `deinit`; two pickers keep it going until both close), the app is in the foreground, Low Power Mode is off and `ProcessInfo.thermalState` is nominal or fair. Anything else sets `ThumbnailFiller.isHalted`: requests in flight finish, nothing new starts (local or network). Before this the filler, once planned, kept walking the library after the picker closed and in the background. `ThumbnailFillPacer` listens for background/foreground, `NSProcessInfoPowerStateDidChange` and `thermalStateDidChangeNotification`.
  - In flight (A): the app's filler runs 4 local requests (2 while the grid moves fast) instead of 16 (4), on the existing utility queue; the limits are instance settings (`idleInFlightLimit`, `fastInFlightLimit`), upstream's constants stay for the demo and the existing tests.
  - iCloud lane off (A): `MediaGridPhotoLibraryServices.shared` calls `applyAppPacing()` which sets `isNetworkLaneEnabled = false`. Photos that exist only in iCloud show PhotoKit's small local thumbnail or the grid's placeholder until the cell's own sharp request (visible cells only, network allowed as in the old picker) fetches them. The filler no longer downloads anything in the background.
  - Store size cap (A): `ThumbnailFiller.storedLimit` = 60,000 thumbnails (about 26 MB of pages plus 4 MB of index at 430 bytes and 70 bytes each); beyond it neither the filler nor the sharp-image harvest writes new thumbnails (the cells fall back to sharp requests). Fill order is viewport-outward, so the newest photos are the ones kept.
  - "Clear cache" (A): Storage Usage's "Clear cache" (all categories, not a single chat; same rule as the widget storage wipe) calls `MediaGridPhotoLibraryServices.shared.clearCaches()`: `MediaGridThumbnailStore.removeAll` (forgets every thumbnail, drops writer contexts and decoded pages, deletes the page files through the superseded-file path and writes the empty index), the retry registry, the launch snapshot and the launch cache files. With no picker open nothing is rebuilt until the next one opens. Storage Usage's totals still do not include this store (outside Postbox).
  - Requests off the main thread (A): `MediaGridPhotoLibraryDataSource.start(_:)` now issues `PHImageManager.requestImage` on a serial `userInitiated` queue; `ImageRequestHandle` (lock, token arrives later than a cancel may) replaces `Job.token`, so a cell reused before the request started never reaches PhotoKit. This is the old picker's IU7 lesson; the result handler is unchanged.
  - Fewer concurrent requests (A): PhotoKit lane 12 -> 8, prefetch 6 -> 4, iCloud lane 4 -> 2 (Telegram's own simulator notes: throughput does not depend on the concurrency; fewer PhotoKit/CMPhoto threads).
  - VISUAL (revert alone): cells whose target is under 512 px (levels 3 to 31) ask PhotoKit for `.highQualityFormat` instead of `.opportunistic`, so each image arrives once instead of a degraded and a final delivery, each through the main queue (old picker's "avoid opportunistic double delivery"). The 48 px thumbnail is on screen until then; level 1 keeps `.opportunistic`. Constant `opportunisticMinPixels`.
  - Memory (A): the data source's decoded-image `NSCache` (80 MB cap) is emptied on memory warning and on entering the background (the thumbnail store already did both).
  - Audited and left as is: sizes match the cell's pixel size (`requestImage(at:pixelSize:)`); cells leaving the window are reassigned in place and their requests cancelled; prefetch is directional (2 screens ahead, none behind) and resolves PHAssets lazily at dispatch; images of 512x512 px or more are redrawn off the main thread; thumbnail page decoding is on a concurrent queue (3 at most); the fling gate exists (thumbnails only while moving fast). Not changed: the catalog build enumerates the whole fetch result off the main thread at every picker open (it feeds the thumbnail lookup; a measurement item), and the first layout may wait up to 50 ms on the main thread for the launch cache (by design, avoids a white first frame).
  - Tests (not run; no Bazel label exists for these modules): `MediaGridPhotoLibraryTests` (`ThumbnailFillPacingTests`, new cases in `ThumbnailFillerTests`, `ImageLoadSchedulerTests`) and `MediaGridTests` (`MediaGridThumbnailStoreTests.testRemoveAll...`) through `xcodegen` + `xcodebuild` on the MediaGrid project (`submodules/TelegramUI/Components/MediaGrid/CLAUDE.md`).
- **Video stickers keep looping after Power Saving turns off (branch fix/video-sticker-loop; not built or run on a device, tests added)**. Owner report: video stickers in a chat played once and stopped. Cause: `ChatMessageAnimatedStickerItemNode` chose `.loop` or `.once` once, at the first setup, from `energyUsageSettings.loopStickers`; Power Saving (our thermal serious/critical rule, Low Power Mode, iOS 27 reduced resources, or the battery threshold) sets that to false, so a sticker set up meanwhile stayed `.once` (it stops at its last frame) until its row was rebuilt, even after Power Saving ended.
  - The decision is now the pure `stickerPlaybackDecision` and the change rule `stickerLoopChangeAction` (new module `StickerPlaybackPolicy`, no dependencies). A visible sticker observes the new `SharedAccountContext.energyUsageSettingsUpdates` (published by `SharedAccountContextImpl` whenever the effective settings change) and follows `loopStickers`: once to loop restarts a stopped sticker (or just switches the mode while it still plays); loop to once lets the current pass finish and stops. Custom emoji (always loop) and animated emoji (still at the end) are not affected; `ChatMessageStickerItemNode` has no such choice. The shared AnimatedStickerNode is unchanged (its public `playbackMode`/`isPlaying`/`play()` are used).
  - Upstream bug fixed on our side: automatic Power Saving by battery level stayed on after charging past the threshold, because `automaticEnergyUsageShouldBeOn` re-read only on the battery-level notification (not delivered while the app is suspended) and on system-condition changes. It now also re-reads on `UIApplication.didBecomeActiveNotification` and `willEnterForegroundNotification`, in both branches; the value is still `distinctUntilChanged`. `PresentationData.swift` is iOS-only (not in docs/mac-core-paths.txt).
  - Thermal rule checked, unchanged: the 120 s hold ends by itself (a scheduled re-read, covered by `test_phone_monitor_thermalHysteresis`) and the change reaches `SharedAccountContext`, which now publishes it to the stickers.
  - Tests (one label run at a time): `//submodules/TelegramUI/Components/Chat/StickerPlaybackPolicy:StickerPlaybackPolicyTests` (run, pass), `//submodules/TelegramPresentationData:TelegramPresentationDataTests` (`AutomaticEnergyUsageForegroundTests`).
- **Glass uses the system's own adaptive look on iOS 26 and 27 (owner decision; branch fix/native-adaptive-glass; not built or run, tests added)**. Telegram pins the backdrop luminance native glass sees (the luma clamp, `backdropLayer:didChangeLuma:` on UIKit's `UISDFBackdropView`) so its glass stays light or dark with the theme; the 13.2 merge kept that on iOS 27 (upstream's adaptive ranges) and on iOS 26 (13.1.2's fixed 0.8/0.801 and 0/0.15). Now neither OS installs the clamp: glass adapts to what is behind it, as Apple's does. `registerEffectViewOverrides` returns before any class lookup on iOS 27 (launch-time gain kept); on iOS 26 the single lookup stays only for the `didMoveToWindow` blur-radius hook (Reduce Transparency handling). Shapes, tint, our glass fixes, Reduce Transparency and Increase Contrast handling are untouched. One switch to compare frames: Debug Settings "Glass: Telegram fixed look" (default OFF = native; `NSUserDefaults` key `nanogram.glass.telegramFixedLook`, read once in `+load`, so it applies after a restart); ON restores the previous look on each OS. Code: `GlassFixedLook` (new, `GlassBackgroundComponent`), `EffectSettingsContainerView.isLumaClampInstalled` and `telegramFixedLookDefaultsKey` (`UIKitRuntimeUtils`), `DebugController`. Known gap: the bubble-free static glass assets were captured with the clamp on, so next to live native glass they can differ over dark or bright content (they stay theme-keyed). Tests (not run): `//submodules/TelegramUI/Components/GlassBackgroundComponent:GlassBackgroundComponentTests` (`GlassFixedLookTests`: clamp not installed by default, key contract, range with and without the clamp).
- **Glass defaults to Telegram 13.2's look; Apple's fully native glass only behind a Debug Settings switch (owner decision, revised; branch fix/native-adaptive-glass; not built or run, tests updated)**. Default again: the luma clamp (`backdropLayer:didChangeLuma:` on UIKit's `UISDFBackdropView`) is installed on iOS 26 and 27 with upstream 13.2's ranges (`GlassBackgroundView.adaptiveLumaRange`, from upstream 5083532f): dark 0-0.874, light 0.22-1.0 on iOS 26 and 0.125-1.0 on iOS 27. This replaces the 13.1.2 fixed iOS 26 constants (light 0.8/0.801, dark 0-0.15) our merge had kept. The switch is now Debug Settings "Glass: native (no Telegram clamp)" (default OFF; `NSUserDefaults` key `nanogram.glass.nativeNoClamp`, read once in `+load`, applies after a restart); ON installs no clamp (Apple's native glass). Launch-time gain kept: `registerEffectViewOverrides` still does exactly one class lookup, by the running OS's name (iOS 26 `_TtC5UIKitP33_...UISDFBackdropView`, iOS 27 `_TtC5UIKit17UISDFBackdropView`), no loop over both names; with the switch on, iOS 27 does no lookup at all. Our glass fixes, shapes, tint, Reduce Transparency and Increase Contrast handling are untouched. The GlassCapture harness launches with `-nanogram.glass.nativeNoClamp NO`, so it still produces the clamped look the static glass assets were captured with. Code: `GlassFixedLook` (`isNativeRequested`/`setNativeRequested`), `EffectSettingsContainerView.nativeNoClampDefaultsKey`/`isNativeNoClampRequested(in:)`/`isLumaClampInstalled` (`UIKitRuntimeUtils`), `DebugController` (`glassNativeNoClamp`, id 124 unchanged), `GlassGroupsSwitchesTests`. Tests (`GlassFixedLookTests`): default is Telegram's look, key contract, clamp installed by default on iOS 26+, upstream ranges, pass-through 0...1 without the clamp.
- **Cancelled video preloads no longer block their reading thread (media cancel race; branch fix/media-preload-cancel, owner-approved; not built or run, tests added)**. `FFMpegMediaFrameSource` runs its FFmpeg reads on a thread of its own that blocks on a `DispatchSemaphore` until MediaBox delivers the bytes. The dispose of `seek`/`ensureHasFrames` signalled the semaphore the context task had published in an `Atomic`; a dispose that arrived before the task ran (or before the read published its semaphore), and the source `deinit`, signalled nothing, so the task then blocked with nobody to wake it (a thread, FFmpeg context and buffers kept, and the abandoned fetch kept running). Now `MediaReadCancellation` (new, `MediaReadCancellation.swift`, no Postbox import) holds a `cancelled` flag set before the semaphores are signalled and checked under the same lock that registers a semaphore; every read wait (`readPacketCallback`, `seekCallback`, `initializeState` direct-file wait) goes through `MediaReadCancellationScope.wait`, which returns without waiting when the source lifetime or the current request is cancelled. Each `seek`/`ensureHasFrames` request has its own token (cancelled by its dispose; a task whose request is already disposed returns at once; the token is cleared when the request ends, so the dispose that follows completion changes nothing); the source `deinit` cancels the lifetime token. `initializeState` on a cancelled scope sets `readingError`, which drops the fetch disposables. Effect: cancelled preloads stop requesting data sooner (fewer requests, same as upstream's intent of dispose = cancel); the normal path is unchanged. Tests: `//submodules/MediaPlayer:UniversalMediaPlayerTests` (`MediaReadCancellationTests`: dispose before the task starts and source deinit before it starts never wait; dispose and deinit while waiting wake the reader; normal delivery unchanged; a late cancel does not touch later reads). Commit: see `git log fix/media-preload-cancel`.
- **Emoji/sticker/GIF panel open hang: signposts and a switch-gated mitigation** (branch perf/emoji-panel-hang, on top of int5; the 231 ms hang is 28 % on-CPU, so the cause is a block that a Time Profiler cannot show; no cause is proven yet)
  - A 1961ed74: os_signpost intervals (category `Performance`, `EmojiPanelOpenSignpost` in EntityKeyboard) around the whole open: `emojiPanelOpenToReady` (tap to the first run-loop turn after the panel's first layout), `emojiPanelInputNodeForState` > `emojiPanelInputNodeInit`, `chatInputNodeUpdateLayout` > `emojiPanelEntityKeyboardUpdate`, and the keyboard steps `emojiPanelReloadInputViews`, `emojiPanelEnsureFocused`, `emojiPanelKeyboardDismiss`. A System Trace / Performance Trace of one open now shows which step holds the main thread while it is blocked. Always on (an enabled check per call when nothing records).
  - VISUAL 1961ed74 (switch): with Debug Settings "Emoji panel: defer first-responder handoff / heavy layers" on, switching into the media input mode no longer swaps the keyboard's input view and changes the first responder before the panel exists. The layout that builds the panel is requested first; `performDeferredMediaKeyboardHandoff` runs after that layout completed (fallback 0.4 s), guarded by `DeferredKeyboardHandoffGate` (one run, stale after a newer schedule or a mode change). Not used for poll option fields (custom text view) or when the panel is already built. Off: the old code runs unchanged.
  - VISUAL 05933f47 (switch): GIF pane tiles (each an `AVSampleBufferDisplayLayer`) are created for visible tiles only once the pane has been in a window for one run-loop turn, at most `LazyGifTilePlan.tilesPerPass` (6) per pass; the rest follow on the next turn (`emojiPanelDeferredGifTiles`). Off: all visible tiles at once, as before.
  - 6b524b65: `ExperimentalUISettings.emojiPanelDefersHeavyWork` (default false, key absent decodes as false), Debug Settings section "Emoji panel (open hang)" in the main app, cached as `EmojiPanelOpenTuning.defersHeavyWork` by `SharedAccountContext`.
  - Tests (not run): `//submodules/TelegramUI/Components/EntityKeyboard:EntityKeyboardTests` (`EmojiPanelOpenTuningTests`), `//submodules/DebugSettingsUI:DebugSettingsUITests` (`EmojiPanelSwitchTests`), `//submodules/TelegramUIPreferences:TelegramUIPreferencesTests` (`testEmojiPanelDeferralIsOffByDefaultAndTheChoiceIsKept`).
  - Opus review fixes (r6): C1 the handoff used `Queue.mainQueue().async`, which runs inline on main, so it ran inside `containerLayoutUpdated`; now `NextFrame.run` (one-shot CADisplayLink + 50 ms fallback, runs once, never inline). I1 `endOpen()` also runs via `NextFrame` after the layout and commit. I2 the deferred GIF tile passes are one batch per frame tick (`NextFrame`) instead of back-to-back `main.async`. I3 the handoff always calls `ensureInputViewFocused()`, also when the rich text node is missing. M1 deferred tiles load synchronously (no extra shimmer). M3 an open interval that no layout ends is closed after 2 s. Tests (not run): `NextFrameTests` in EntityKeyboardTests (never inline, runs once, cancel, dangling interval closed). Call-site wiring in ChatControllerNode is not unit-testable (node needs a full context).

- **Chat open: signposts and a switch that defers non-visible setup** (branch perf/chat-controller-init, on top of int5; not built or run, tests added; evidence: owner traces 2026-11-06, `ChatControllerImpl.init` 111 ms hang and 177/152 ms busy streaks, closures #146/#157). Commits, A = no visible change with the switch off:
  - A 34d62709 + 1442e1ed: os_signpost intervals `chatInit` (whole `ChatControllerImpl.init`), `chatInitInteraction` (the ~150-closure `ChatControllerInteraction`), `chatInitDisplayNode` (`loadDisplayNode`), `chatInitReloadLocation`, `chatInitPresentationData` (first `themeAndStringsUpdated`), `chatInitDeferrable` (one per deferrable item), `chatInitDeferredSetup`, `chatFirstLayout` (first `containerLayoutUpdated`), in `NanogramSignposts` (both SwiftSignalKit copies; the Mac gets the names with the core).
  - VISUAL 2cca2b89 (switch) + 1442e1ed (wiring): Debug Settings ▸ "Chat open: defer non-visible setup" (`ExperimentalUISettings.chatOpenDeferSetup`, default off, main app, own section). On: a standard chat (not previewing, not embedded) starts these after `viewDidAppear` on the next main-queue turn instead of inside `init`/`loadDisplayNode`: account/chat name colour, bot callback alert, audio and video recorder listeners, typing/choosing-sticker/recording activity, send-as list, network-speed notices, and `GlobalControlPanelsContext` with its state subscription (music/call/live-location panels). Everything that feeds the first frame stays synchronous. Off: `DeferredSetup` (34d62709) executes in place, same order. Not deferred (first-frame inputs or re-run per reload): presentation data, content data/`reloadChatLocation`, history, account premium state, sticker and download settings, foreground/read state, network title, gallery-hidden media, volume buttons.
  - Opus review fixes (r6): I1 the iOS 18 on-device translation host is created synchronously again (not deferred; it would have sent the first auto-translation requests of the first chat after launch to the server). M2 the deferred setup also flushes 1.5 s after the first layout if `viewDidAppear` never comes (flush is idempotent), so the recorder listeners always hook up.
  Tests: `//submodules/SSignalKit/SwiftSignalKit:SwiftSignalKitTests`, `//submodules/DebugSettingsUI:DebugSettingsUITests`, `//submodules/TelegramUIPreferences:TelegramUIPreferencesTests`.
- **Chat-scroll experiments: "Chat rows: prepare layout ahead" and "Chat rows: reuse row views"** (branch perf/chat-row-prep-reuse, on top of int5; not built or run; both switches in Debug Settings ▸ Chat scroll, main app, default off, with both off the row paths are the former ones). Evidence: owner traces of 2026-11-06 (chat scroll main thread: `rebalanceActiveWindow` 39 %, `CoreListNodeHostView.rebuild` 29 %, text layout in `ChatMessageBubbleItemNode` ~220 ms, row teardown 76 ms).
  - aaa74e75: `ExperimentalUISettings.chatScrollExperiments` (bits 1 and 2), the Debug Settings section, `ChatScrollExperiments` (two cached Bools set from `SharedAccountContext`), and the new module `ChatRowPrep` (key, cache, pool, idle queue, signposts) with host-less tests.
  - 7c7a5eec, prepare layout ahead: on each user-scroll frame the backend plans the 3 rows beyond the loaded edge in the direction of travel; `CoreListChatRowPrefetcher` builds them on a serial queue with `ChatMessageItemImpl.nodeConfiguredForParams` (inline off main, as `ListViewImpl` does) and `CoreListNodeHostView.rebuild` takes a result only for an equal `ChatRowPrepKey` (item instance, id and version, width, insets, neighbours, presentation data, font size, `ChatControllerInteraction.rowPrepEpoch`, rotation, sync-load flag), else builds synchronously. At most 8 rows, 5 s to live, evicted and cancelled when outside the plan. `rowPrepEpoch` has a `didSet` on 22 `ChatControllerInteraction` properties.
  - 7c7a5eec, reuse row views: `CoreVirtualListView.rowRetirementHandler` (new, nil by default) lets the backend leave unloaded rows hidden in the container and finish them at an idle run-loop turn (2 ms budget); certified types are reset (`ChatMessageItemView.prepareForRowReuse`) and pooled (4 per type, 8 total, drained on memory warning and when the list leaves the hierarchy). Certified: `ChatMessageStickerItemNode` only; every other type is removed and released later instead of in the scroll tick. `ChatMessageItemImpl.resolvedItemViewClass()` is the former inline class choice.
  - Review fixes (Opus review of this branch; not built, `ChatRowPrepTests` run and pass, other labels below pending for disk space): I1 `rowPrepEpoch` is a `ChatRowPrepEpoch` counter that moves only when a Bool or id property really changes (`recommendedChannelsOpenUp` is assigned on every scroll frame, `canReadHistory` and `focusedTextInputIsMedia` on every interface update, which evicted every prepared row each frame); collections and settings structs still count every assignment. I2 `ChatRowIdleWorkQueue` observer order is `Int32.max` (after the Core Animation commit) and the run-loop turn that queued work is skipped (a wake-up block guarantees a following turn); the 2 ms budget is checked between rows (a row is not split). I3 documented as debug only: the background build reads main-mutated `ChatControllerInteraction` state without a lock, so the switch must not default on before that state is snapshotted at plan time. I4 at most 16 rows wait in the idle queue; a batch that does not fit is answered `false` and the list removes it in the tick. Minors: sticker placeholder fade completions capture the specific node; `TransformImageNode.resetArguments()` is called on a pooled sticker (no one-frame stretch); the sticker's `dateAndStatusNode` is replaced by a new node on reset; a pooled host is taken out of the container when handed out, so `render()` re-adds it on top at its frame; a row built synchronously while its job was in flight is not claimed again; `ChatRowPrep` uses `strict_swift_library` (Swift 6 mode; `ChatScrollExperiments` statics are `nonisolated(unsafe)`); lookahead is 3 in code and docs.
  - Signposts (Performance): `chatRowPrefetchHit`, `chatRowPrefetchMiss`, `chatRowPoolReuse`, `chatRowPoolMiss`, interval `chatRowPrefetchCompute`.
  Tests: `//submodules/TelegramUI/Components/Chat/ChatRowPrep:ChatRowPrepTests`, `//submodules/TelegramUI/Components/Chat/ChatMessageStickerItemNode:ChatMessageStickerItemNodeTests`, `//submodules/TelegramUI/Components/CoreList:CoreListScrollPathTests` (adds RowRetirementTests), `//submodules/DebugSettingsUI:DebugSettingsUITests`, `//submodules/TelegramUIPreferences:TelegramUIPreferencesTests` (label of the ExperimentalUISettings tests as before). The CoreList xcodebuild suite should also run once.
- **Reaction preview in the reaction context menu plays at the source's frame rate and sharp** (branch perf/reaction-preview-fps, on top of int5; not built or run on a device; evidence from code, owner report of choppy playback on iPhone 17 Pro Max and 14 Pro). The large animated reaction above a message's reaction menu (`ReactionPreviewView`, 100 pt, `EmojiStatusComponent` -> `InlineStickerItemLayer` -> `DCTMultiAnimationRendererImpl`) was cached by `cacheLottieAnimation` with `frameSkip = 2` (a 60 fps TGS played at 30 fps, `frameDuration = 2/fr`; the renderer then ticks a 30 Hz link: `animationFrameDurationNeedsHighTickRate`) and at `min(2, screenScale)` pixels per point (200 px on a 3x screen for 300 px of display: 1.5x upscale, soft). Upstream does the same, so this is not a Nanogram regression; the cap was never lifted for the one large animation.
  - New `LottieCacheQuality` (`LottieAnimationCache`): `.standard` is exactly upstream (skip 2, <= 2x scale, id unchanged); `.full` keeps every frame (skip 1, 60 fps durations) at the screen's scale under its own cache id (`<resource id>#full`, so a 2x screen does not meet a standard entry of the same size). `cacheLottieAnimation(frameSkip:)` and both `animationCacheFetchFile` overloads take a defaulted `frameSkip`.
  - `InlineStickerItemLayer(fullQuality:)` / `EmojiStatusComponent(fullQuality:)`, defaults false; only `ReactionPreviewView` asks. Power rules unchanged in spirit: `.full` is only granted when Low Power Mode is off, the thermal state is nominal or fair and the device has more than two cores (the same condition under which `DCTAnimationTickPolicy.frameSkip` ticks at 60 Hz); otherwise the preview is cached and drawn exactly as before.
  - Not changed: the DCT renderer's tick (already 60 Hz for 1/60 s frames, `.fps(60)` on the shared link = 30...60 Hz range on ProMotion; 120 Hz would add no frames to a 60 fps source), `AnimatedStickerNode`, other emoji/sticker surfaces. Mac: none of the touched modules is in `docs/mac-core-paths.txt`; the defaults leave other callers as they were.
  - Tests: `//submodules/TelegramUI/Components/LottieAnimationCache:LottieAnimationCacheTests` (policy under Low Power / thermal / two cores, cache ids and scale, and frame counts and durations written for a 60 fps fixture: 90 x 1/30 s standard, 180 x 1/60 s full).
