- **WAL checkpoint byte counter counts pages, not frames** (branch fix/wal-checkpoint-accounting, shared core: Postbox is in docs/mac-core-paths.txt, so the Mac gets it with the next core bump; measurement only, the database, its pragmas and the checkpoint itself are unchanged). `SqliteWalWriteAccounting.checkpointed` added every backfilled frame, but a checkpoint writes the newest copy of each page once, so `sqlite.checkpoint.<db>` over-counted whenever the same pages were rewritten (R6 item 1). It now reads the 4-byte page number from the headers of the frames backfilled since the previous checkpoint (`<db>_sqlite-wal`, left in place by the PASSIVE checkpoint until the next write restarts it) and counts distinct pages x page size; if the file cannot be read it falls back to the frame count (the old upper bound). The "WAL checkpoint" signpost and its "N of M frames" payload are untouched. Cost: up to one 4-byte pread per newly backfilled frame (about 1000 per checkpoint) on the Postbox queue next to the two full syncs. Tests (`SqliteValueBoxWriteAccountingTests`): `testCheckpointCountsDistinctPagesNotFrames`, `testSecondCheckpointCountsOnlyNewFrames`, `testUnreadableWalFallsBackToTheFrameCount`, `testCheckpointOfARewrittenRowCountsFewPages` (1100 rewrites of one row: <= 20 pages); the four existing tests still pass. `//submodules/Postbox:PostboxTests` hit the 300 s bazel timeout in the unrelated slow `TimeBasedCleanupTests`; the 8 accounting tests passed in that run.

- **Post-merge fixes of the 13.2 import** (branch nanogram/int6-132; from up132-review.md; not built or run, tests added where noted). Note: the merge commit 7b112389 carries the trailer "Claude Sonnet 5.5" instead of "Claude Opus 5.5"; history is not rewritten.
  - C1: `CrashReportsController` calls `exportMetadata(bundleId:)` (upstream changed the signature) with the app bundle id; the app did not compile without it.
  - Not changed on purpose: reads are no longer held for strangers (upstream's `holdsReadsForSender` removal stays as merged; owner decision pending, `docs/subsystems/suspicious-peer.md` still describes the old hold), macOS telemetry guards (upstream's), retry-forever of the history-fill confirmation, MediaGrid picker defaults. `WindowTouchRelease` / `PhysicsScrollEngine` scene-change gesture cancel: `Display` and `CoreList` are not in `docs/mac-core-paths.txt`, so no `#if !os(macOS)` guard was added. The Mac gets C2, C3, I4, I6 (`MediaPlayer`), M1 and the `HardenedMedia` property with its next core bump.
  - C2 (`MultipartUpload`): upstream's early switch to big parts (growing file crossing 10 MB, nothing in flight) now fires only while `committedOffset <= heldHeaderLength`, and resets `heldHeaderLength`; after small parts were committed our restart under a new file id covers the crossing (before, the same id got big parts and a wrong part count). Test `MultipartUploadTests.testACrossingOf10MBWhileGrowing...`.
  - C3 (`StoredMessageConfirmation`): the request is unchanged (upstream's 200-id `messages.getMessages`). An answer with fewer entries than ids asked (a `messageEmpty` counts as an entry) no longer makes the left-out ids `missing`: only ids the server marks with a `messageEmpty` (its peer, when given, must match) are deleted; the rest go to the new `unconfirmed` set and stay stored (logged in State); `Holes.swift` reopens a hole over them as over a candidate written during the confirmation, so the next fill asks again. A complete answer keeps the owner's rule of 2026-10-25 (no entry = deleted). Tests: `StoredMessageConfirmationTests` (short answer, messageEmpty without peer, complete answer) and the fill tests whose fake server now answers `messageEmpty` for deleted ids (`StateSyncApi.deletedMessages`/`everythingGone`).
  - I1 (`TgVoipWebrtc/BUILD`): the `content_negotiation_test` and `signaling_sctp_options_test` targets are removed; their sources (`tgcalls/v2/ContentNegotiationTest.cpp`, `SignalingSctpOptionsTest.cpp`) are not in our tgcalls pin 57b914d8, so a wildcard build/test over the package failed. Re-add them with the tgcalls port.
  - I2 (`PresentationGroupCall.setIsMuted`): when the microphone gate (or `canUnmute == false`) refuses an unmute, the call re-reports its mute state to CallKit (`reportMuteToCallKit`), so the lock screen and Dynamic Island no longer stay on "unmuted" while the call is muted. Test `GroupCallMicrophoneAccessGateTests.testARefusedChangeIsOneThatCallKitMustBeToldAbout` (the report itself is not unit-testable: it needs a CallKit integration).
  - I6 (`ChunkMediaPlayerV2`): the custom (HLS) source is told `isPlaying && !isSuspendedInBackground`, so a muted inline HLS video in the background stops filling its forward buffer. Test `ChunkMediaPlayerV2CustomSourceTests.testCustomSourceHearsNotPlayingWhileSuspendedInTheBackground`.
  - M1 (`OggOpusWriterContinuityTests`): `testResumeFromStateWithoutPageNumberReparsesTheData` is split to match upstream 13.2's `TGOggOpusWriter.resume`: a state with `encoderData` but no `pageno` is refused (`...IsRefused`), and an older-kind state (no `encoderData`/`opusVersion`) is resumed by reparsing the data (`...WithoutEncoderDataReparsesTheData`).
  - I4/F1/F2 (TelegramCore `State/Sync`): our three write reductions are back inside upstream's replay: `applyUpdateChannelState` skips an unchanged pts (`channelStateUpdatedByPoll`), `applyUpdateMessageImpressionCount` skips a count that does not raise the stored view count (`messageAttributesWithIncreasedViewCount`); the equal participants-count skip was never lost (`AccountMutableState.mergeChats`). `StaleChannelDiagnostics` hooks re-added: `noteTooLong` x2 in `UpdateInterpreter+Channels.applyChannelTooLong`, `beginPoll`/`endPoll` in `pollChannelOnce`, `noteDifference` (tooLong, slice, empty, difference) in `DifferencePolicy.applyDifferenceAnswer`. Log-only; requests to the server are unchanged. Test `ReplayWriteReductionTests` (through `FinalStateReplayer.applyReplay`).
  - I3/W1/W2/W3 (message widgets, TestFlight testers should see no change unless Lockdown Mode is on): widgets no longer run in Lockdown Mode (`HardenedMediaPolicy.allowsMessageWidgets`; the bubble shows the unsupported-content pill) nor when the new Debug Settings switch "Message widgets" is off (default on = upstream); `chatMessageWidgetRenderingEnabled()` combines the server kill switch, the local switch and the policy. The widget wallet is off behind the Debug switch "Message widgets: wallet" (default off; `MessageWidgetWalletAvailability.isReady` also needs it) until a device test with a funded test wallet. Crash sentinel `MessageWidgetCrashSentinel` (Caches/message-widget-sentinel.json): `MessageWidgetRegistry` notes a source before it runs and when it ends; a launch that finds sources still noted (the app crashed while they ran; the list is emptied while the app is in the background so a swipe-away is not a crash) blocks them for 7 days (at most 64), and a blocked source's widget starts stopped, i.e. the static "Widget stopped" badge and no script; turning "Message widgets" on again forgets the blocked sources. Tests: `MessageWidgetTests` (`CrashSentinelTests`, `WalletAvailabilityTests`), `ChatMessageWidgetBubbleContentNodeTests` (`MessageWidgetRenderingGateTests`), `HardenedMediaTests`, `DebugSettingsUITests` (`DebugControllerEntriesTests`). Still to run under our `.bazelrc` hardening flags (libc++ fast mode, auto-var-init zero, stack protector) before the first build: `MessageWidgetCorpusTests`, `MessageWidgetTests` (attacks), `WidgetRuntimeTests`, `WidgetRuntimeCorpusIncrementalTests`, `WidgetRuntimeCorpusBootTests`, `WidgetRuntimeRenderCorpusTests`, `WidgetRuntimeCorpusGoldenTests`, `WidgetRuntimeWPTTests`, `TwidgetProfileHooksTests`, `TwidgetForkTests`, plus the Tests/WidgetHarness conformance run; any trap is an upstream bug to report.
- **Merge of upstream Telegram-iOS release-13.2** (branch nanogram/int6-132, on top of nanogram/int5; upstream snapshot 5083532f, `versions.json` app 13.2; nothing built or run, tests not executed). 81 files conflicted. Rules: keep our fixes and take upstream's new behaviour; where both changed the same logic, combine; upstream's server behaviour stays. Fork submodule pins are NOT moved: tgcalls stays at 57b914d, webrtc at 86cd593, rlottie at 083f19a. Resolutions, file -> what was kept from each side:
  - Build files. `.gitmodules`: ours (absolute klaipher URLs, `shallow = true`, rlottie entry kept; upstream's rules_* entries stay out, we use `local_path_override`/`archive_override`). `MODULE.bazel`: ours for rules_swift/apple_support, plus upstream's new `xctestrunner` `single_version_override` (patch `build-system/bazel-patches/xctestrunner-collect-test-diagnostics.patch`). `versions.json`: no conflict (ours Bazel 9.3.1, app 13.2). `.gitignore`: both. `CLAUDE.md`: ours (`@AGENTS.md`); upstream's long rewrite is not taken, nothing of it is lost that `docs/` does not hold. `docs/iphone-duo-layout.md`: ours (upstream's extra test-target names in the Duo test list are not copied; list them when the targets are run). `TelegramUI/Components/CoreList/CLAUDE.md`: both (upstream's scroll "hold" paragraph added). `TgVoipWebrtc/CLAUDE.md`: ours.
  - BUILD files. Camera: ours (`CameraTests` stays the simulator test with the glob), upstream's host `swift_test` kept as `CameraHostTests` (its 3 tests have no `import Camera`, so they are excluded from our glob). CameraNeo/ChatListUI/TelegramCallsUI/TelegramUniversalVideoContent/PhotoResources/MediaPlayer: dependencies of both. MediaPlayer test resources: `Tests/Resources/*` plus upstream's `Tests/Fixtures/*.mp4`. Display: our test targets plus upstream's accessibility test host, runner and `sh_binary` (`rules_shell`). ItemListUI/EntityKeyboard/VideoMessageCameraScreen: our simulator constants, upstream's extra deps and visibility. ReactionSelectionNode: our `ReactionSelectionNodeTests` (4 files) and upstream's `ReactionContextGeometry` + `ReactionContextGeometryTests` (3 files), each target with explicit sources. ChatControllerInteraction: our host `UnreadMessageRangeTests` plus upstream's `ChatControllerInteractionTests`. MediaEditor: our `MediaEditorTests` (2 colour tests) plus upstream's export support/harness/tests (our two files excluded from its glob). WalletContext: both test targets.
  - Lottie. Upstream removes `LottieSettings` and the rlottie backend: taken in `AnimatedStickerNode` (initializers without `lottieSettings`), `ReactionSelectionNode`, `AccountContext` (our `AnimatedStickerRendererMode.processWide` line kept, the cached-settings code gone), `FetchCachedRepresentations` (our `LottieRenderingSettingsCache` deleted), `ChatMessageBubbleItemNode` (our `import HardenedMedia` kept), our `AnimatedStickerNode` tests (rlottie variants dropped), `ExperimentalUISettings`/Debug Settings (`forceRLottieBackend` removed). `SoftwareAnimationRenderer`: our `DirectFrameImageView`, hidden from VoiceOver as upstream's `makeAnimationRendererImageView` is. The `submodules/rlottie` wrappers and the fork submodule stay: the macOS app shares them (`docs/mac-core-paths.txt`) and the Watch app builds RLottieKit from them. Nothing on iOS depends on them any more.
  - HLS. Upstream's native HLS player replaces the JS/WebView one: deleted (`HLSVideoJSNativeContentNode`, `WebViewNativeJSContextImpl`, `PlayerSource/*`, `HlsBundle`), which drops the code our HLS commits 7ddeada8, 8d5d69bf, 6f064a0f changed. `MediaDataReader`/`ChunkMediaPlayerV2`: our zero-copy `.mediaBoxRanges` parts now also take `startAt` (keyframe seek plus skipping non-reference frames), like upstream's temp-file parts. `FFMpegFileReader`: our initializer parameters plus upstream's `skipNonReferenceFramesBefore` and `probeOnlySelectedMediaType`, with our `findStreamInfo(withFullProbe:)`; `FFMpegBinding`: union of both header and method sets.
  - TelegramCore. `AccountStateManagementUtils.swift` deleted and `AccountStateManager.swift` taken from upstream (state sync is the new `State/Sync/` layout); our `StaleChannelDiagnostics` call sites in the manager and the three `UnchangedStateUpdates` call sites in the old file are gone for now (follow-up F1, F2). `MultipartUpload.swift`: upstream's header-closure/held-header design plus our over-10-MB restart (`hasHeaderPart` now `headerSize() != 0`; the restart also clears `heldHeaderLength` and `pendingHeaderOffsets`), our parallel parts, 128 KB small parts, and our written-prefix data signal for growing conversion outputs. Both over-10-MB paths stay: upstream's early switch to big parts (no part sent yet) and our restart (parts already sent as small). `MultipartUploadTests`: upstream's file plus our three tests with `headerSize:` closures. `EnqueueMessage.swift`: upstream's decoding of rich quotes (an empty fragment array from the beta "r" key becomes a flat quote), our rule that a rich quote's text and entities are always the flat form of its fragments (our `EngineMessageReplyQuoteFlatFormTests` still hold). Postbox `ChatListViewState`: upstream's topic-info refresh and our `ChatListViewFillCounters`/gated `fillSpace`; `SqliteValueBox`: ours.
  - Glass (owner left the call to me). iOS 27: upstream's luma clamp is installed through `_TtC5UIKit17UISDFBackdropView` in `UIViewController+Navigation.m`, still one lookup per OS (the 45 ms launch gain stays; the blur-radius `didMoveToWindow` hook stays iOS 26 only, as before). iOS 26: `GlassBackgroundView.adaptiveLumaRange` returns our fixed ranges (light 0.8/0.801, dark 0/0.15) when iOS 27 is not available, so the 26 look does not change; iOS 27 uses upstream's adaptive ranges (light 0.125-1.0, dark 0-0.874). This is the owner's "judge from recorded frames" item: the 27 look has not been compared with frames yet.
  - UI. `ChatListItem`: upstream's accessibility fields and `accessibilityCustomActionsKey` logic, our deferred label/value (`ListViewDeferredAccessibility`). `ChatListController`: `isSideList` initializer plus our launch signpost. `CoreVirtualListView`: our linear `firstDuplicatePair`; upstream's `beginModelWrites()` before our `promotedIdentities`. `ContainerViewLayout`: both fields (`windowControls`, `isBesideBottomTabBar`), our copy-based `withUpdated...`. `NavigationSplitContainer`: upstream's master-first layout and animated detail, our `windowControls` on the master layout. `TabBarContollerNode`: ours (`floatingBottomPanelsInset` is upstream's `tabBarFloatingBottomInset` rule). `TabBarComponent`: both. `MediaPickerScreen`: the picker uses upstream's grid host (the layer-based MediaGrid, default as upstream); our thumbnail prefetcher and fling gate run only with the legacy grid (`MediaPickerGridNodeHost.visibleItemsReported`). `LocationPickerControllerNode`: upstream's `locationPickerVenuesEnabled` with our read-once `initialLocation`. `PeerPresenceStatusManager`: ours (stronger than upstream's `max(1.0, ...)`). `PremiumLimitsListScreen`: upstream's rewrite without the reaction-override code we had removed. `PeerInfoRatingComponent`/`PeerInfoHeaderNode`: upstream's `accessibilityLabel`, no `debugLevel`. `AuthorizationSequencePhoneEntryControllerNode`: our QR login layout with upstream's fit check (only when the phone animation is the first item) and VoiceOver changes. `GlassBackgroundComponent`, `UIKitRuntimeUtils`: see Glass.
  - Camera. `CameraNeo`/`CameraLegacy` `CameraPreviewView`: our session-queue connection changes and tablet policy, composed with upstream's iPhone Duo mapping (scene/interface orientation first, then `CameraVideoOrientation(interfaceOrientation:screenPortrait:)`); the legacy `isEnabled` change also skips an unchanged value.
  - Audio. `ManagedAudioSession`: upstream's pure `audioSessionOutputs(route:hasLoudspeaker:)` with our "audio is on the loudspeaker" correction added into it.
  - App. `AppDelegate`: AppCenter imports and start removed (upstream dropped AppCenter); upstream's in-app update check is NOT taken (we removed the updater); upstream's "unlock your phone" notification check kept. `SharedAccountContext`: our display-link settings plus upstream's `MediaEditorVideoExport.forceLegacy*Writer` flags. `ChatController`: our raise-to-listen on/off plus upstream's side-list appearance forwarding. `ChatControllerNode`: input expansion by upstream's `inputNodeForState != nil` and our iPad rule. `ChatHistoryListNode`: our `extendedMediaProcessingManager.setPaused`, upstream's `attemptReadingMentions()`. `NotificationContentContext.swift`: stays deleted (we removed that extension). `ExperimentalUISettings`/`DebugController`: ours (curated list) plus upstream's `coreListScrollTrace`, `chatMediaEditorLegacy`, `mediaGridLegacy`, `sharedMediaGridLegacy`, `storyLegacyVideoWriter`, `chatLegacyVideoWriter`, `chatSideListLegacy` switches (ids 23 and 82-87, tests updated). Not taken: upstream's widget debug items, difference-limit override, remove-models, the pts limit debug entry, `CrashReportsController` (ours: no log goes to a fixed bot). `OpusBinding/opusenc.m`: upstream's rewrite (`beginAppendWithDataItem`, restorable encoder state); our 30-line continuation fix is covered by it (follow-up F9: run `OggOpusWriterContinuityTests`).
  Follow-ups after this merge (none done here): F1 re-apply `UnchangedStateUpdates` (channel pts, view count, participant count) inside `StateReplay+StateAdvance.swift`/`StateReplay+Messages.swift`; F2 re-add the `StaleChannelDiagnostics` hooks in `ChannelPolling.swift`/`SyncDifference.swift`; F3 MultipartUpload device tests (encrypted, growing, headered); F4 history-fill confirmation asks for 200 ids (cap 100?); F5 macOS telemetry (`allowsLegacyEvents`); F6 re-measure FetchV2 priority; F7 hold reads for strangers (`holdsReadsForSender`, `docs/subsystems/suspicious-peer.md`); F8 widgets (Lockdown gate, local kill switch, crash sentinel, wallet off, caps); F9 Opus tests; F10 native HLS part store onto `MediaBoxRanges` parts, report `isPlaying && !suspended` to the HLS controller, software-AV1-last-resort rule in `HLSQualitySet`; F11 tgcalls and webrtc ports (below); F12 group-call mic gate vs `reportMuteToCallKit`; F13 MediaGrid picker memory, network lane off, filler paced; F14 `WindowTouchRelease` guard on macOS; F15 Mac `core-bump`, Mac build, parity gate, `forceRLottieBackend` users on the Mac side; F16 reaction icon hand-off exists twice (ours `ReactionExpandedIconHandOver`, upstream `ReactionFlyInIconHandoff`): keep one after a device check. Upstream submodule changes to port into our forks: tgcalls 8d6dd4c6 -> c1de0882 (new CompatImpl 7/8/9 framing, `network_sctp_*` keys for engines 11.0.0, 14.0.0, 18/19, two new test mains for `Package.swift` excludes, BUILD hunks) into `nanogram-shared`; webrtc 6513c71d -> 590a8b23 (NetEq crossfade fix ff1be2e852 = CVE-2026-9119, duplicate of our 0ee3826, `neteq_normal_test`) into `nanogram-security-fixes`; the new upstream BUILD hunks for both must come with the pins. Full list and test labels: the merge report in the session scratchpad.
- **Main-thread stalls when opening the emoji/GIF/sticker panel, the photo picker and a chat** (branch perf/open-stalls, on top of int5; evidence: iPhone 17 Pro Max Release traces of 2026-11-06, hangs of 231/109/116/141 ms panel, 219/175 ms picker, 111 ms + 177/152 ms chat open). Commits, A = no visible change, VISUAL = judge from recorded frames:
  - A f80ff777: the panel data build (lazy since e5201a18) also starts on touch-down of the stickers/emoji button and when the text input gets focus (`InputDataStartGate.Trigger.prewarm`, `ChatTextInputPanelNode.inputPanelsPrewarmRequested`), so a panel opened within 4 s of chat open does not wait for a cold build.
  - A 810d19ee: `processStableItemGroupList` (14 ms on main at panel open) is linear (`StableGroupOrder`: set and dictionary instead of `contains`/`first(where:)` over `AnyHashable` in loops); a test compares it with the former code on random lists.
  - VISUAL 9320d1fe: GIF tiles (`GifVideoLayer(synchronousLoadOnlyFromCache: true)`, GIF panel only) fill synchronously only from `GifThumbnailCache` (decoded 93 pt pictures, 24 MB NSCache); uncached tiles read and draw off main. Was: resource read + `drawImage` per tile on tab switch (`GifVideoLayer.init` 92 ms, 113/132 ms hangs). First visit of the tab shows tile placeholders for a few frames.
  - VISUAL 1ca093d7: `InlineStickerItemLayer.updateFile(attemptSynchronousLoad: true)` draws the silhouette placeholder of an uncached item on the first-frame queue instead of main (never over a real frame or after a reload).
  - A 3563536d: `MediaAssetsContext.init` makes no PhotoKit call (observer registration lazy and thread-safe, `ChangeObserverGate`; `mediaAccess()` reads the status on subscription); `AssetPrimingWindow` loads the first and last 48 `PHFetchResult` objects.
  - A 175d1d9e: the picker's state pipeline (status, album and asset fetches) runs on a serial queue (`Node.stateQueue`) instead of inside `Node.init`; `ready` already waits for the first state.
  - VISUAL de53e34d: chat media loads synchronously only while a burst's 12 ms main-thread budget lasts (`SynchronousDecodeBudget.chatMedia`, always the first photo); later photos of the first window load off main behind the bubble placeholder. Was 30-44 ms per undecoded photo.
  Review fixes of int5 (review-ios-r5-ui.md), on this branch: 15fe4c22 (C1/C2 compile errors: gate read through `controller?.interaction`, `SwiftSignalKit.Timer`; I3 fling gate releases on touch-down and rest via `settle()`, subscribes on main), a6fdf3fc (I1 `AvatarNode.setPeer(avoidMainThreadDecode:)` opt-in, default false, only the share sheet passes true; unrounded semantics as before elsewhere), 5cfdf595 (I2 idle panel build 0.5 s after the panels load, postponed while the history scrolled in the last 0.3 s, at once when already in panel mode), 7beb5037 (I4 VISUAL, floating-buttons glass container hidden while no button is visible), c9bb322b (I5 VISUAL, badge `cornerRadius = 10` from init), c750c5ee (revert of 91778033), and the ChatListNode first-transition flag now set only when the transition inserts rows (M2).
  Not changed: `ChatControllerImpl.init` (79 ms; no safe deferral found without a profile), the 28 %-on-CPU emoji hang (no synchronous wait found on the chat path; `EntityInputView` has a semaphore but only the drawing screen uses it).
  Tests: `//submodules/TelegramUI/Components/ChatEntityKeyboardInputNode:ChatEntityKeyboardInputNodeTests`, `//submodules/TelegramUI/Components/GifVideoLayer:GifVideoLayerTests`, `//submodules/TelegramUI/Components/MediaAssetsContext:MediaAssetsContextTests`, `//submodules/Components/ReactionButtonListComponent:ReactionButtonListComponentTests`, `//submodules/Display:DisplayTests`.
- **Fix pass after the round-5 core review** (branch fix/r5-core-review; not built or run, tests added):
  - Camera (16ecab58). C1: `CameraNeo.deinit` without an invalidation now follows the order of `CameraSessionInvalidation` (`tearDownWithoutOwner`): stop connection changes, session queue stops the session, main detaches the preview views (held strongly by `PreviewHolder` until then, released on main), then the session queue removes inputs/outputs; no waits. I1: orientation changes made on the session queue run in `CATransaction` with actions disabled, skip a value already applied to the connection, and apply the latest request (`PreviewOrientationTracker`; a request made before the connection exists stays pending); `captureOrientation` returns the latest request. Legacy `connectionQueue` is lock-protected (written on the camera queue, read on main) and its orientation change has the same transaction/latest-wins behaviour. I2: the crash of 202611021745 (`TelegramUIFramework+15320500`) symbolicates, with that build's dSYM, to `closure #1 in closure #1 in CameraNeo.stopCapture(invalidate:)`, the old main-queue block `previewView?.invalidate()` (`layer.session = nil`), not `setVideoOrientation:`; so the preview detach on main is the culprit class and the orientation change was not. The detach already runs only after `stopRunning()` on the session queue and before the graph teardown; it is now logged before and after ("Camera preview detach: start / done in N ms") to attribute the next hang. Tests: `//submodules/Camera/CameraNeo:CameraNeoTests` (`CameraSessionInvalidationTests`, `CameraPreviewConnectionTests`).
  - Wallet (fc63515d): only `WALLET_UNAVAILABLE` is authoritative; other 4xx (`rejected`) are transient (backoff, 8/h cap). After WALLET_UNAVAILABLE one attempt per hour is made (`authoritativeRetryInterval`) instead of a stop for the whole session, so a wallet enabled mid-session is found; opening the wallet still asks at once. `.blocked` gate state removed. Tests: `//submodules/WalletContext:WalletStateRetryPolicyTests`, `//submodules/TelegramCore:TelegramCoreTests` (`WalletGetStateErrorTests`).
  - Logs (f3f6944f): the Notification Service flushes the log in `ExpiringProcessActivity.end()`/deinit, before the process may be suspended, so the pause and delivery lines are written (both the request and the placeholder-removal activity). `ShareProcessActivity.deinit` uses `Logger.sharedIfInstalled` (the shared logger does not exist yet on the early-return paths). Stale `maxLogBuffer` comment removed. SiriIntents and Widget were not changed (many completion paths, low volume). Tests: `//Telegram/NotificationService:NotificationServiceTests` (`ExtensionRequestCompletionTests`).
- **Liquid Glass usage fixes, per Apple's guidance** (branch fix/liquid-glass-ios; audit rules in the scratchpad `liquid-glass-audit.md`). Commits, A = no visible change, VISUAL = judge from recorded frames:
  - A 19ba4f4b: `ChatHistoryNavigationButtonNode.updateTheme` called `GlassBackgroundView.update` without `isInteractive: true`, so the default `false` replaced it after the first theme or glass-type change and the buttons lost their press feedback. Every other `update(...)` call site was checked for a first-setup/later-update mismatch; the others are different views (LivePhotoButton and its tooltip, two MediaPicker views, StarReactionButton vs its badge) or the intentional non-interactive morph glass of `animateShape`.
  - A b48296c1: `GlassBackgroundContainerView.update` re-assigned `overrideUserInterfaceStyle` and re-ran the effect view's frame block on every layout pass; `GlassContainerUpdateGate` now lets only a changed size or style through. `readaptToBackdrop()` was audited and left alone: it already runs only when the bar's glass tint changed (`NavigationBarNode.updatePresentationData`, `glassChanged`) or a capsule appeared in place (`glassNeedsReadaptation`), once per change after the transition, not per layout or scroll tick. `GlassBackgroundView.update` already skips the effect rebuild when its `Params` are equal.
  - VISUAL 670efdd0: the floating history buttons (scroll-to-bottom, mentions, reactions, poll votes) share one `GlassBackgroundContainerView` (UIGlassContainerEffect, spacing 7 pt vs 12 pt gaps) instead of five standalone glass views (Apple: group nearby glass, one sampling pass, one adaptive appearance). The node views sit in a plain stack view at the node's coordinate origin (`GlassStackHostGeometry`), so positions and animations are unchanged; hit testing walks the same buttons in the same order.
  - VISUAL ed664562: the count badge on those buttons is a tinted capsule (`actionControlFillColor`) instead of a second `GlassBackgroundView` over the button's glass (Apple: no glass on glass).
  - VISUAL (only with Reduce Transparency or Increase Contrast on) e962b94a: the legacy glass used before iOS 26 (opaque fill composited over the appearance's base; rim twice as wide) and the custom blur radius of the context menu and QR Studio (dropped) follow the system settings, live, through `GlassEnvironment` (observes both notifications). System glass already did.
  - A d870401e: Debug Settings ▸ "Glass groups" (main app, default off): three switches that replace the navigation bar's, the input panel's or the floating buttons' glass with a solid fill, for per-group GPU attribution. `GlassBackgroundContainerView.debugGroup` tags the containers; stored as `ExperimentalUISettings.glassDebugDisabledGroups`.
  Tests: `//submodules/TelegramUI/Components/GlassBackgroundComponent:GlassBackgroundComponentTests`, `//submodules/DebugSettingsUI:DebugSettingsUITests`, `//submodules/TelegramUIPreferences:TelegramUIPreferencesTests` (label of the existing ExperimentalUISettings test target).
- Logger buffers "Log to File" lines (e40fcc15, branch perf/log-buffer). Evidence: iPhone 14 Pro, 228,623 and 118,223 log writes of ~0.5 KB on two days (disk-write ledger, category "logs"): one write per line, two counting the newline. Lines now collect in a buffer on the logger queue and go out in one write: at 64 KB, 1 s after the first unflushed line (one timer, none while idle), on rotation (before the old file is closed or the oldest removed), in `sync()`, at the start of both `collectLogs`, when `logToFile` goes off, and from `flushPendingLogs()` (was `flushPendingShortLog()`, now flushes the short log too): `handleDidEnterBackground`, memory warning, `releaseBackgroundTime` end (suspension), `applicationWillTerminate` (waits), `NotificationService.finish` and `ShareProcessActivity.deinit` (wait). File names, format, 2 MB rotation, order and redaction unchanged; with file logging off nothing is buffered and no timer runs. DiskWriteAccounting counts the bytes once per flush. A crash or kill loses at most the last ~1 s of lines (up to 64 KB if the process dies inside a burst). The short log (`critlog-`) is unchanged (already batched, 0.5 s / 32 KB). Tests: `LoggerBufferedLogTests`.
- **Camera: no main-thread wait on the capture session** (branch fix/camera-main-thread; commit d0a3828f). MetricKit crash of 202611021745 (iPhone 14 Pro, iOS 26.5.2, 2026-11-03 01:04): 0x8BADF00D "scene-update watchdog ... stuck (deadlock)", app in the background, CPU 0%, main thread in a main-queue block blocked on an AVFCapture lock (no dSYM for that build; the cause is identified from code, not from symbols). Most likely: `CameraNeo.configureSession` ends with `DispatchQueue.main.async { previewView.updateOrientation() }`, and `CameraPreviewView.updateOrientation` (also run from `layoutSubviews`) read and set `videoPreviewLayer.connection.videoOrientation` on main, which takes the session's AVFoundation lock while the session queue sits in `startRunning()`/`stopRunning()` (they block for long when the app is going to the background and the camera is interrupted). Fix: the interface orientation is read on main, the connection is changed on the session queue (`CameraPreviewView.applyOrientation`, same path as `isEnabled`; skipped once the camera is invalidated). `CameraNeo.deinit` no longer does `sessionQueue.sync`/`mediaQueue.sync` (main waited for `stopRunning()`); it hands the clean-up (`cleanUpOnDeinit`: drop delegates, stop, remove inputs/outputs) to the queues with `async` on objects it owns. Legacy camera (`LegacyCameraSimplePreviewView`, used by round video via DrawingUI and the legacy path): `updateOrientation` and `isEnabled` now change the connection on the camera's queue (`connectionQueue`). Unchanged and reviewed: tgcalls `VideoCameraCapturer.mm` (all session work on the RTCDispatcher capture-session queue; only `previewLayer.session = ` in `addPreviewView` runs on main, at call setup), PGCamera/TGVideoCameraPipeline (own camera queues, async), FaceScanScreen (configures on main once at open). Tests: `//submodules/Camera/CameraNeo:CameraNeoTests` (`CameraPreviewConnectionTests`).
- Wallet: `wallet.getState` no longer repeats every 1-3 minutes for hours on accounts where the server answers `400 WALLET_UNAVAILABLE` (153 requests in 5 h in the owner's 13.1.2 log). Root cause: the backoff of 2e31d5c1/506047ab was reset every time the app went to the background or offline (`evaluateRuntimeDemand`), and every resume then requested the state immediately, bypassing the policy (55 restarts, longest wait reached 160 s of the 600 s ceiling). `WalletGetStateError` now carries the reason (`unavailable`, `rejected`, `floodWait`, `generic`); `WalletStateRetryPolicy` stops retrying after a definite answer until the wallet screen opens or the app relaunches, keeps its exponential backoff across foreground/network resume (only a visible wallet screen or a success resets it), honours FLOOD_WAIT, and caps attempts at 8/h (120/h with the wallet visible). The request itself is unchanged; upstream 13.1.2 retried every 5 s with no backoff at all, so this never sends more than upstream. (47abcbcb)
- Main-thread image work (branch perf/main-thread-images; evidence: share sheet hang 256 ms, 19 of 26 samples avatar JPEG decode on main; chat list update hang 103 ms; chat open 111 ms; owner traces 1606 items 3, 6).
  - 326598e7 (no visible change): `peerAvatarImage` gains `avoidMainThreadDecode` (default off), a cache-only lookup (`peerAvatarCachedImages`) and the pure decision `peerAvatarRendersOnCallingThread`; `prewarmPeerAvatarImages` decodes downloaded avatars into `PeerAvatarImageCache` at utility QoS without fetching; the share sheet prewarms rows 20-60 of its first page. Tests: `AvatarNodeTests` (`PeerAvatarRenderPolicyTests`).
  - b987d029 VISUAL: `AvatarNode` passes `avoidMainThreadDecode`: a synchronous load shows a cached avatar at once and otherwise decodes in the background (letters first). Also, for these nodes the unrounded copy follows `storeUnrounded` instead of `synchronousLoad`, so sync and async requests share cache entries.
  - ad25f6c8 VISUAL: chat list `PreferSynchronousResourceLoading` only for the first transition, a filter change, a scroll-to-item or more than 3 inserted rows (`ChatListSynchronousLoadingPolicy`); `PreferSynchronousDrawing` unchanged. Avatar cache hits skip the queue hop. Tests: `ChatListUITests` (`ChatListSynchronousLoadingPolicyTests`).
  - e5201a18 VISUAL: the chat's emoji/sticker panel data (`ChatEntityKeyboardInputNode.inputData`) is built on demand (`openStickers`, first `makeMediaInputNode`) or after 4 s idle (`InputDataStartGate`); a panel opened earlier waits for the build and appears when it is ready. Tests: new `ChatEntityKeyboardInputNodeTests`.

- **Photo picker energy (branch `perf/photo-picker-thumbs`, not built or run; the new tests in `MediaAssetsContextTests` were added but not run).** Evidence: Power Profiler, build 202611061452, picker scroll = CPU power impact 37 vs 5.8 average, 101 G instructions, CMPhoto/VideoToolbox/Photos threads. The grid already requested exact 256 px thumbnails (`thumbnailTargetSize`), one request per cell with cancel on reuse (IU7), and prefetched with `AssetThumbnailPrefetcher` (U6); what was left:
  - **Off main** (`397d9ac8`, no visible change): the prefetcher's `objects(at:)` and `start/stopCachingImages` run in order on the assets queue; the cell passes the `PHAsset` it already read to `assetImage(asset:)` instead of a second `fetchResult[index]` (`MediaPickerGridItem.setup`).
  - **VISUAL resizeMode .fast** (`91778033`): grid requests and the prefetcher use `resizeMode = .fast` (new `fastResize` parameter of `assetImageRequestOptions`/`assetImage`, grid only) so Photos resizes near 256 px instead of returning a larger stored thumbnail that is decoded and scaled. Edge sharpness may differ slightly. REVERTED in c750c5ee: `.fast` is already the default, the change was a no-op.
  - **VISUAL fling gate** (`aff2251e`): `ThumbnailScrollGate` + `ScrollVelocityEstimator` (fling above 2400 pt/s, ends below 1200 pt/s, a sample older than 0.12 s means stopped, 0.1 s timer releases waiters). During a fling cells keep the placeholder and do not start the Photos request (`gate.deferred(assetImageSignal)`); cells reused meanwhile dispose the wait. The prefetcher is not updated during a fling and catches up once. A calm grid starts requests synchronously as before. Tests: `ScrollVelocityPolicyTests`, `ThumbnailScrollGateTests`.
  - **VISUAL directional prefetch window** (`fa753c9e`): one screen ahead in the scroll direction (from the first visible index), half a screen behind (was a screen each side). Tests: `AssetCachingWindowTests` (directional window, direction).
- `StaleChannelDiagnostics` keeps its counters in a small lock-guarded `@unchecked Sendable` class instead of a static `Atomic`: `Atomic` is `Sendable` in the iOS build (so `nonisolated(unsafe)` is an error there, a84d1a50 did not build) but not in the Mac package build, where the plain `let` added two Swift 6 warnings to TelegramCore (561 -> 563).

## 13.1.2 (202611061452) — 2026-11-06 — 33d7d094

TestFlight upload (internal testers), Enhanced Security (soft mode) on the app, NotificationService and Share (checked in the built app and in the exported App Store build). Since 202611060203 (32bafb86..33d7d094): round 4 — Debug Settings exports on a serial queue (MetricKit fix), Send Logs ▸ Call Logs, CoreList scroll path membership/attachment work, round-video recovery after the background, stale-channel diagnostics (log only), 1:1 screen share by negotiated version with thermal frame-rate steps (tgcalls 57b914d on nanogram-shared). No upstream import: release-13.2 is not published on GitHub yet (latest tag release-13.1.2). Tests: tgcalls screen_share_policy and call_system_policy, DebugSettingsUITests, CoreList ScrollPath/AttachmentFlight/IdentityScan, UniversalMediaPlayerTests, plus the pre-push hook set.

> What to test in this build:
>
> 1. Round video messages: play one with sound, leave the app (Home or lock) for a while, come back. The picture should keep moving with the sound. Before, it could freeze on one frame until you restarted the video from the start.
>
> 2. Calls with a Mac: when the Mac shares its screen in a 1:1 call, small text should look sharper on the iPhone.
>
> 3. Scrolling long chats does a little less work per frame. Tell us if you see rows jump, date headers or avatars flicker, or anything out of place while scrolling.
>
> 4. Debug Settings > Send MetricKit Reports works again (it said "Could Not Prepare" in recent builds). Send Logs is more reliable too.
>
> 5. If some channels at the bottom of your chat list show old posts until you open them, turn on Debug Settings > Log to File, open one such channel, then send the logs: this build records what the app had before and after.
>
> 6. Debug Settings > Send Logs > Call Logs (new): sends the logs of your last 10 calls. After a test call with a Mac sharing its screen, send them so we can check the screen-share line.

- **Debug Settings ▸ Send Logs ▸ Call Logs** (new): exports the logs of the 10 newest 1:1 calls (`<call id>_<time>.log` plus its rotated `.log.1`, without the `.log.json` stats) zipped unchanged, through the same destination picker as every other export. Before, 1:1 call logs could only leave the device through the call rating (to Telegram); the `log-` collector never matched their names. They hold the new `Call: negotiated version …, screen share mode …` line. `debugCallLogFiles` in `DebugLogExport.swift`; test `DebugLogExportTests.testCallLogFilesAreTheNewestCallsLogs`.

- **Debug Settings exports no longer report "nothing to send" while files are prepared** (bcd26d3c; bug fix, since 76b150c4 on 2026-11-03): every export prepared its files after `deliverOn(Queue.concurrentDefaultQueue())`, which dispatches the files and the completion as separate blocks on a concurrent queue. The completion could overtake the files while they were still being copied and zipped; `runDebugLogExport` then took it as an empty result, showed "Could Not Prepare" and dropped the archive. It hit Send MetricKit Reports almost every time (dozens of JSON files) and could hit Send Logs, Send All Logs and Critical Logs. The four preparation sites now use one serial queue (`debugLogExportPreparationQueue`). The stored reports were never lost. Tests: `DebugLogExportTests.testPreparedFilesArriveBeforeTheCompletion`, `testMetricKitExportDeliversTheArchive` (both failed before the fix).
- CoreList scroll path, no behaviour change (branch perf/chat-scroll). `render()` removed stale rows with a nested `contains` (O(n^2) per window change); it now uses an identity set (`ListMembership.staleSubviews`), same removals and same z-order. The rebalance no longer hashes the whole window for crossing carries when there are none (`ListMembership.promotedIdentities`). (ab2ad724)
- `resolveAttachments` (every row a scroll loads) no longer builds an identity-to-index dictionary over the whole collection: only the previous runs' members are looked up, and only while each is still at its old index, otherwise the full map is used (`sameIndexLookup`, `AttachmentRuns.assignSerials(newIndexOfIdentity:)`). (759cbca1)
- **Stale-channel diagnostics (log only, no behaviour change)** (`StaleChannelDiagnostics.swift`, `AccountStateManagementUtils.swift`, `AccountStateManager.swift`, `Postbox.swift`, `SharedWakeupManager.swift`). Log tag `StaleChannel`: once per channel per launch, when its first poll starts, the chat-list top message id/date, the max cloud message id/date in the history table and the channel's pts/invalidatedPts, then pts before/after and the number of messages the poll added; every `updateChannelTooLong` with running received/skipped counts; every `getDifference` result kind (empty/difference/slice/tooLong) with seconds since the previous one. Postbox logs each `setCanBeginTransactions` change with a unix timestamp and the number of queued transactions; Wakeup logs foreground/background transitions with a timestamp. No message text; peer ids are cut to four digits while "Remove sensitive data" is on.

- **Round video frozen after returning from the background** (deb5900a, reworked in f7693d38; `ChunkMediaPlayerV2.swift`, new `ChunkMediaPlayerVideoRecovery.swift`; tests `ChunkMediaPlayerVideoRecoveryTests`). A video with sound keeps playing in the background (only muted ones are suspended, b70f75b9), so its picture can die there while the audio goes on: the display layer fails or asks for a flush, or the reader's VideoToolbox session does not survive and the reader runs dry. Pause and play touch neither the layer nor the reader (the video is marked finished for the rest of the reader's life), only a seek flushes and rebuilds them, which is why only a restart from zero helped. The player now rebuilds its video pipeline by itself (a forced seek to the current position, no seek callback) when the layer reports `.failed` / `requiresFlushToResumeDecoding` while it plays in the foreground, or when, 1.5 s after the clock is running again following a return to the foreground, the video renderer asked for frames, the reader had none and nothing was enqueued. Review fixes (I-1..I-3, M-3, M-4, M-6): a paused or buffering video never gets a forced seek (the grace counts from when the clock runs, and a full layer that never asked for data is not a stall); at most one recovery per return to the foreground and per user seek (a stream whose layer keeps failing is no longer re-seeked every 4 s); one `MediaPlayer` log line per recovery with the reason (`layerFailed` / `layerNeedsFlush` / `noFrameAfterForeground`), position and seconds since the last enqueue; "has sent video" is `lastVideoEnqueueTime != nil`; the layer status is read only after the cheap checks. The Mac `TelegramMediaPlayer` package (`Package.swift` in this repo) now excludes the new file and its test, like the other V2 files. See the report for what is established and what is inferred.
- **Mac 1:1 screen share as screen content, by protocol version** (branch `feat/mac-screencast-quality`; tgcalls branch `feat/mac-screencast-quality`, not built or run). `ScreenSharePolicy.h` (version to screen mode) with `screen_share_policy_test` (Bazel `//submodules/TgVoipWebrtc:screen_share_policy_test`, host `tgcalls/tests/screen_share_policy/run.sh`). `InstanceV2ReferenceImpl` (10.0.0/11.0.0) no longer drops a screen capture: it sends it on its one video channel with detailed content hint, maintain-resolution and a 2 Mbps ceiling. The macOS wrapper marks `desktop_capturer_` sources as screen captures. Every call logs `Call: negotiated version X, screen share mode Y`. Version finding (corrected in the fix pass, review I-4): `CallSessionManagerContext` reverses the version list (CallSessionManager.swift:575/586) and the caller takes the first common entry, so Mac-iOS, iOS-iOS and tdesktop calls negotiate 9.0.0 (InstanceV2Impl, separate screencast channel); 10.0.0/11.0.0 is a rare fallback. The visible change is the Mac wrapper passing the screen flag, which moves a Mac screen share to the separate screencast channel (`is_screencast`, MAINTAIN_RESOLUTION, no explicit max bitrate), the path iOS already uses for its own screen share. Expected log: `negotiated version 9.0.0, screen share mode separate-screencast-channel`. Unit fix: the camera cap `1200 * 1024` is 1200 Kibit/s. Thermal video ladder (on by default): a screen share is no longer exempt; it keeps its size and drops frame rate only (15 fps from level 2, 10 at level 3), tgcalls 57b914d, test `call_system_policy_test`; applies to iOS screen shares too at level 2 and above. iOS behaviour is otherwise unchanged (14.0.0 still drops screen captures). `screen_share_policy_test` now also pins the implementation class per version. Commits: d127ed2e (pin 3862bdc), tgcalls fix pass 57b914d.

## 13.1.2 (202611060203) — 2026-11-06 — 32bafb86

TestFlight upload (internal testers), the first with Enhanced Security (soft mode) on the app, NotificationService and Share (checked in the built app and in the exported App Store build). Since 202611052231 (9971ecaa..32bafb86): round 3 and 3b (calls fixes and audit, call defaults on with RNNoise only, CoreList scroll tick work, photo decode at display size, photo picker off main, emoji panel reuse, shared core WAL counter / MediaBox LRU / lookahead wake-ups / allocation cuts, extensions on the failable database open, glass lookup skipped on iOS 27, chat-open signposts), the speaker retry and the tg fixes. BUILD-INFO.txt is next to the dSYMs. The detailed entries are below. Mac counterpart: GitHub release 13.1.0 (see the Mac CHANGELOG). Tester notes (What to Test):

> What to test in this build:
>
> 1. Calls: the speaker button. On phones where it did nothing, it should now switch to the speaker. If it still fails, send the call log right after the tap.
>
> 2. Calls: muting from the lock screen or the system call screen now mutes the call, and the app's mute button stays in sync with it.
>
> 3. Calls: camera flip, and the camera preview before you turn video on, behave correctly; closing the preview turns the camera off.
>
> 4. Calls: noise suppression in group calls uses a better filter, also with Bluetooth headsets. Live streams use less battery and stay cooler. Your video is lighter on the phone, and gets smaller when the phone is hot. Please tell us how you sound to others.
>
> 5. Chats scroll more smoothly, with fewer stutters when new messages come into view.
>
> 6. Photos in chats and in the photo picker load with less work; scrolling the picker should be smoother.
>
> 7. The emoji panel opens faster and uses less memory.
>
> 8. Video playback uses less battery.
>
> 9. Extra memory protection (Enhanced Security) is on in this build. Please report any crash, with the time it happened.
>
> 10. Sharing to Nanogram and notifications no longer crash if the app's data can't be opened.

Detailed entries:

- `tg testflight` no longer stops between the build and the upload when CHANGELOG.md has a long Unreleased section: `record_build_info` piped `awk` into `head -1` (and `git branch -r` into `grep -q`) under `set -o pipefail`, so the writer died of SIGPIPE (141) and `set -e` ended tg (build 202611060158 was built but not uploaded). Both now read without an early-exit reader; regression test in `test_tg.sh`.
- **Calls: a missed loudspeaker request is applied once more** (owner report: iPhone 14, 1:1 call, no Bluetooth, the speaker button did nothing). `ManagedAudioSession.applyVoiceChatOutputModeInCurrentAudioSession` (the CallKit path every region but CN uses) re-applies `.custom(.speaker)` once, 0.3 s later, when the route still contradicts it and no newer request replaced it (`loudspeakerRequestShouldBeRetried` in `AudioRouteClassification.swift`, a generation counter; tests in `AudioRouteClassificationTests`). Complements the calls lane fixes (route read while the input list is empty, request and route log lines with "MISSED").
### Calls (lane ios-calls, round 3)

- **Defaults ON** (5f2b9404): `callsLivestreamHardwareDecoding`, `callsNativeCapturePath`, `callsSystemNoiseSuppression`,
  `callsThermalVideoLadder` are on, `callsGroupNoiseMode` is 2 (RNNoise only). Narrowband (Bluetooth HFP) processing is
  already unconditional in the fork (`GroupAudioCapturePostProcessor.cpp`), so RNNoise-only still suppresses on an HFP microphone.
  Advanced ducking stays off. How: `ExperimentalUISettings` is encoded as a whole struct, so installs that ever saved it hold
  an explicit false / 0 under the old keys; each changed value now has a new key (`callsLivestreamHardwareDecodingDefaultOn`,
  `callsNativeCapturePathDefaultOn`, `callsSystemNoiseSuppressionDefaultOn`, `callsThermalVideoLadderDefaultOn`,
  `callsGroupNoiseModeRNNoiseDefault`), the old keys are neither read nor written. Tests: `CallsSwitchesTests`.
- **Speaker button went dead after one ineffective request** (7aa6153d, iPhone 14 report): the request was dropped when it
  equalled the stored value, which is set at the tap; the session reports only changes. On the killswitch (non-shared) paths
  every later tap for the speaker was then swallowed for the rest of the call; on the default `SharedCallAudioContext` path
  only for one second (the stored value falls back to the reported route), so there the fix only covers a quick second tap. Now
  `audioOutputRequestDecision` (`AudioOutputRequestPolicy.swift`) skips a request only when the session itself reports that route
  (`PresentationCall`, `SharedCallAudioContext`, the non-shared `PresentationGroupCall` path). Also: CallKit puts the loudspeaker
  back on `didActivate` after an interruption, only while the route is the phone's own (`loudspeakerShouldBeReappliedOnActivation`;
  review fix: re-applying any last mode would have pulled the call off AirPods or a car that connected later); `updateCurrentAudioRouteInfo` reads the loudspeaker from the route
  outputs (`AudioRouteClassification.swift`) and not only from the built-in microphone input; the video call screen stops forcing
  the speaker after three attempts (`AutomaticSpeakerEnforcement`). Diagnostics in the short log: `audio output request ...` per
  request and `ManagedAudioSession output request ... -> route ... MISSED` after an apply; `audioInputStateDescription` lists route
  outputs, category, mode and options. Root cause on that phone is not proven; see the audit section of
  docs/subsystems/tgcalls/call-switches-and-review-fixes.md.
- **CallKit mute from the system UI did nothing** (7aa6153d): the handler passed `CXAction.uuid` instead of `callUUID`
  (`callKitMuteTarget`). An in-app mute is now reported to CallKit (`CallKitMuteSync`, `reportMuted`), so the lock screen and the
  Dynamic Island show the real state.
- **Review fixes (lane ios-calls, 211c6fea)**: the app's own CallKit mute action is recognised by its action id when CallKit performs it
  and is not applied a second time (a quick mute-unmute was undone by the late echo), and a refused transaction restores what
  CallKit shows (`CallKitMuteSync.appRequested/isAppRequested/appRequestFailed`). After a 1:1 call turned into a conference
  the CallKit call stays under the 1:1 id: the lock-screen mute now reaches the conference (`callKitMuteRecipient`,
  `PresentationCallManager`) and the conference reports its mute to CallKit (`PresentationGroupCallImpl.reportMuteToCallKit`).
  A request that waits for CallKit's `didActivate` is logged. The 1:1 camera preview releases the camera on every dismissal
  that is not a share, including the automatic one when the app goes to the background. Tests: `CallKitMuteSyncTests`,
  `CallKitActionTargetTests`, `AudioRouteClassificationTests`.
- **1:1 camera**: the flip belief is a `CameraFacingTracker` that follows the capturer (new capturer = front, off = reset, handed
  over on the back camera = back); the first flip after camera off/on or after a hand-over no longer does nothing. Cancelling the camera
  preview releases the capturer instead of keeping the camera on for the rest of the call (7aa6153d).
- **TestFlight builds ship with Enhanced Security (soft mode)** (RH1, 87ddfeb4). `tg device --app-store` now adds `--//Telegram:enhancedSecurity` when the development profiles of the app, NotificationService and Share grant `hardened-process` (it stops with the missing names otherwise; `TG_TF_ENHANCED_SECURITY=0` ships without). `device_signing.py testflight` checks the built app's signed entitlements before export and the exported App Store build (signed entitlements and embedded distribution profile of the three bundles) before the upload; any missing key or profile grant aborts with nothing uploaded. xcodebuild's own upload is refused while the check is on (the API key path uploads with altool after the check). Tests: `tools/dev/tests/test_device_signing.py`, `test_tg.sh`. Needs the Enhanced Security capability on the three App IDs; verified at the next TestFlight build. Review follow-up: a `test_tg.sh` check that `TG_TF_ENHANCED_SECURITY=0` reaches `device_signing.py testflight` as `--enhanced-security off`, the `tg testflight` header documents the requirement (it had been split off its function by `record_build_info`), `docs/build-and-test.md` no longer says the flag is always off.
- **Share extension and Notification Service use the failable database open** (RH3, spec I-26, 87ddfeb4). Both call `setupExtensionAccountManager` (`AccountManager.openForExtension`): Share shows the existing sign-in alert (default presentation data, since the settings live in the unreadable database) and ends the request; the Notification Service hands back the server's own alert at once instead of trapping or waiting for expiry. Only the app keeps the trapping open. Guarded by `tools/dev/tests/test_extension_database_open.py`; no Swift test seam for the extension entry points.
- `tg testflight` records the build's commit, branch and dirty/pushed state in `BUILD-INFO.txt` next to the build's dSYMs, and warns about a dirty tree, an unpushed commit and unreleased CHANGELOG entries (RH4, 87ddfeb4). The next changelog starts from that commit.
### Round 3, core lane (perf/r3-core)

- **CO1 / C12 Postbox write attribution**: the disk-write ledger says how many WAL bytes Postbox wrote, not which code wrote them. `SqliteWalWriteAccounting` now keeps the frames the last commit appended (`takeLastCommitAppendedFrames`, read right after `COMMIT`, where the WAL hook ran), and `PostboxImpl.internalTransaction` adds them per call site (`file:line` of the `transaction` call) in the new `TransactionWriteAttribution`. A Postbox log line at most every 10 minutes (only when something was written) lists the six call sites that appended the most frames (frames, commits, largest single commit). Counts and source locations only. A read-only transaction appends nothing and is not counted. Nothing about what is written changes. Tests: `TransactionWriteAttributionTests`, `testTheFramesOfTheLastCommitAreTakenOnce` in `SqliteValueBoxWriteAccountingTests`. Pure part ran in a scratch package (6/6). Commit d6090125.
- **CO2 / C6 MediaBox handle table is LRU**: `MediaBoxFileManager` evicted the handle with the lowest id (opened first), so a file written for the whole download (one handle, opened early) was closed and opened again whenever 16 newer files were opened. It now evicts the handle least recently used (`ItemContext.lastUse`, set on every `Item.access` from a counter in the manager). The cap stays 16: the process fd limit is 256 on iOS and the table shares it with sockets and SQLite. The `mediaBoxReopen` event and the once-a-minute `reopened=` log line still count what remains. Test: `testTheLeastRecentlyUsedHandleIsEvictedNotTheOldestOpened` (review: ran in a scratch package with the copied `MediaBoxFileManager`, SwiftSignalKit and ManagedFile, 2/2 with the existing re-open test; fails on the old FIFO eviction). Commit d6090125.

### Round 3b, core lane (perf/r3b-core)

- **Y2 / W1-S5 lookahead wake-ups**: every playing direct-fetch player sent a `perform(on: lookaheadThread)` ten times a second from `ChunkMediaPlayerV2.update` (via `updatePlaybackState`), and the thread re-ran its read pass each time. New `FFMpegLookaheadWakeGate` (MediaPlayer): the position is stored in the shared atomic on every update, but the wake is sent only when `readableTo < max(seekTo, position) + lookahead - margin` (margin = min(2 s, lookahead / 4), so with the 10 s lookahead the buffer stays at least 8 s ahead), never while a wake is already queued (the thread reads the newest position when it runs; `beginWake` clears the flag before that read) and never after the file ended. The reader publishes `readableTo` (the minimum over its streams, nil until each stream has produced a packet) and `isEnded` at the end of every pass. Tests: `FFMpegLookaheadWakeGateTests` (10, run in a scratch package), including a 20 s simulation that never leaves less than 7.9 s buffered. Review fix: the reader's advance counters ran for the whole pass, so after the first 0.1 s every packet sent a state report (a main-queue hop and `ChunkMediaPlayerV2.updateInternalState` each); with 2 s refill passes that was about 140 reports per pass, more main-thread work than the wake-ups saved. `FFMpegLookaheadReportStep` resets them after each report, so a pass reports once per 0.1 s of media (a file without audio or video still reports only at the end of the pass, as before). Tests: `FFMpegLookaheadReportStepTests` (4, scratch package). The direct-fetch source is not in the Mac build, so this item is iOS-only in effect.
- **Y3 / U7 videoPlayback activity interval**: `MetricActivity.videoPlayback` had no call site. `UniversalVideoContentHolder` (TelegramUniversalVideoContent) now follows its `statusValue`: playing, or buffering while playing (`VideoPlaybackActivityPolicy`, CrashReporting), keeps a `MetricActivityIntervals` token; pause, an idle status or the holder going away ends it. Several playing videos are one interval (the counter is reference counted). Tests: `ActivityIntervalCounterTests` (policy and two-videos-one-interval).
- **Y1 / MP6 allocation churn, core side**: `FFMpegMediaVideoFrameDecoder.convertVideoFrame` no longer builds the pool aux-attributes dictionary per frame (one process-wide `CFDictionary`), interleaves U and V straight into the pixel buffer when its row stride matches (no scratch plane, one full-plane `memcpy` less per frame; the scratch plane stays for padded strides) and caches the resolved colour-attachment strings (the per-frame tuple array and three code-point lookups). `UniversalSoftwareVideoSource` reads a fully downloaded video through one `pread` handle into FFmpeg's buffer (`CompleteResourceFileReader`) instead of `MediaBox.resourceData` per 64 KB (an open, a stat, a `Data` allocation and a copy per read); partial files take the old path. `MessageHistoryTable.readIntermediateEntry`, the fallback of `PostboxDecoder.decodeUtf8String` and `SqliteValueBox.stringAt` decode UTF-8 from the value box memory (`StrictUTF8String`): the old `String(data: Data(bytes:count:))` copied the text into a heap `Data` first. Same results for invalid input and byte-order marks. Tests: `StrictUTF8StringTests` (4, run in a scratch package; compared with Foundation on valid, invalid, truncated and BOM input), `CompleteResourceFileReaderTests` (4, scratch package). Not measured: the Allocations numbers need the next dSYM build.
- **Y4 / M6 thread stacks**: attributed only, no code change (see the round report): of the 999 distinct threads in `mac-session-153758`, 482 are unnamed Nanogram threads of which 411 are GCD workqueue workers, about 250 are CoreMedia threads, 100 are `NSScrollingConcurrentVBLMonitor`, and 101 are ours (`FFMpegMediaFrameSourceContext` 55, `FFMpegLookahead` 46) plus 17 `ThreadPool` threads.
- **Round 3b, iOS UI** (branch `perf/r3b-ios-ui`, not built):
  - **Z2 chat-open signposts** (Processor Trace section 7): three point events in category `Performance` (names appended to `PerformanceSignpostName` in both `NanogramSignposts.swift` copies, test list extended): `chatListRowTap` (first line of `ChatListController`'s `peerSelected` handler, before its `Task` hop), `navigationPush` (`NavigationController.pushViewController(_:animated:completion:)`, next to the `navigationPushToReady` interval) and `historyFirstContent` (first applied history transition with messages, `ChatHistoryListNodeImpl.dequeueHistoryViewTransitions`). Events, not user intervals: `UserSignpost.all` stays at 6 names. The file lives in the shared core (`share submodules/SSignalKit`), so the Mac gets it with the next core pin; it is not a `mirror` entry and needs no Mac copy. Nothing sent to Telegram changes.
  - **Z4 +load class lookup** (`UIViewController+Navigation.m`, `registerEffectViewOverrides`, iOS 26+): the private Swift class `UISDFBackdropView` was looked up twice by name (once for `didMoveToWindow`, again in a loop for `backdropLayer:didChangeLuma:`). A name that is not in the ObjC class table falls through to the Swift runtime's mangled-name scan (45 ms in `_contextDescriptorMatchesMangling` in the launch trace), so a miss paid for twice. It is now looked up once and the same class is used for both overrides. Same overrides, same order, same names; no behaviour change on a hit. Not measured (no build).
  - **Z4 follow-up, review: the iOS 27 class name** (`registerEffectViewOverrides`): UIKitCore 27.2 (simulator shared cache of the same OS build train) has no `_TtC5UIKitP33_ACD4A08F...17UISDFBackdropView`; the class is now `_TtC5UIKit17UISDFBackdropView` (the file-private discriminator belongs to `UISDFElementView`). So on iOS 27 the old name always missed: every launch paid the Swift mangled-name scan, and the `didMoveToWindow` glass blur radius and the `backdropLayer:didChangeLuma:` luma clamp (`EffectSettingsContainerView` `lumaMin`/`lumaMax`) were never installed. The lookup now uses the running OS's name only (`@available(iOS 27.0, *)`: the new name, else the iOS 26 name), never the other as a fallback, so neither OS misses. Visible change on iOS 27: glass views inside Telegram's effect containers get the configured blur radius on first attach and the clamped luma, as on iOS 26 (upstream 13.1.2 still uses the old name and misses on 27). Needs recorded frames on an iOS 27 device.
  - **Z4 integration ruling (2026-11-06)**: no visual change in this build. On iOS 27 the lookup is skipped instead of using the new name, so the glass looks as in every build so far and +load still saves the ~45 ms scan; turning the overrides on there (the new name) waits for the owner to judge recorded frames.
- **Round 3, iOS UI performance** (branch `perf/r3-ios-ui`, not built; pure logic ran in scratch packages: CoreList budget and debounce 8/8, `ImageDecodeSizing` 6/6, `DeferredAssetRequest` 4/4):
  - **IU1 CoreList row building per tick** (`9d1a682c`): `CoreVirtualListView.rebalanceActiveWindow` builds rows through `RebalanceRowBudget`. A row whose edge is inside the viewport (a visible gap) is always built in the tick; a row that only fills the preload band counts against `maxSpeculativeRowsPerTick` (chat backend: 1), the rest waits for the next tick (`rebalanceDeferredRows` keeps the bookkeeping skip off meanwhile). The preload band is unchanged (160): a wider band for scroll rebalancing only was tried and removed in review, because every transaction rebuilds its window against `preloadMargin`, so the extra rows were dropped (or carried as old-only crossing survivors) on the next pass and built again by the next scroll (the invariant is now written in the CoreList `CLAUDE.md`). Rows are still built on main; the off-main measure phase is not attempted (order and animation semantics, see the comments in `CoreListChatHistoryBackend.rebuild`).
  - **IU2 per-tick bookkeeping**: `handleUserScroll` returns before `renderAttachments`, `notifyVisibleRects` and `onVisibleWindowChanged` when a `ScrollTickSignature` (engine offset, attachment solve offset, `windowRevision` bumped by every `activeWindow` write, container origin, size, insets) equals the previous tick's, no scroll flight is active and no row was deferred. This only covers a repeated `onScroll` at an unchanged offset (programmatic halts and edge sets): the recording's 216 unchanged-window ticks are flight ticks (`PhysicsScrollEngine.sampleTick`) with a moving offset, and they still run the full bookkeeping. `renderAttachments` stays the same when the offset changes (it is a solve at the offset). `noteHeaderFlashingActivity` no longer invalidates and creates a `SwiftSignalKit.Timer` per tick: `ScrollActivityDebounce` keeps the last activity time, the 0.3 s timer re-arms itself for the remainder when it fires early (267 timer rebuilds in the recording).
  - **IU6**: `visibleRectNotifiedViews` is an array of weak boxes (no `NSHashTable` `probeGC`); `preRebalanceIdentities` (a `Set` of `AnyHashable` rebuilt per tick) is replaced by the pre-rebalance index range, since window indices are contiguous.
  - **IU3 `processDisplayedItemRangeChanged`** (`e7e423a0`): the scan over every filtered entry for the business-bot and guest-chat tooltips and the possible-reaction ids depends on the history view only, so it is cached on `ChatHistoryTransactionOpaqueState` (`ChatHistoryWholeScan`). The reaction ids are still handed to the manager on every range change, as upstream: a once-a-second limit was tried and removed in review, since `updateReactionsForMessageIds` is a server request and the limit changed when it is re-sent. The visible-range part (view counts, mentions, translation, fact-check, unseen reactions, prefetch) is unchanged: debouncing it would change which messages are requested from the server while scrolling, and delay prefetch and read state.
  - **IU5 chat photo decode** (`e7e423a0`): `chatMessagePhotoInternal` decodes a complete JPEG at the drawn size (`ImageDecodeSizing.downscaledImage`, `CGImageSourceCreateThumbnailAtIndex`, `kCGImageSourceShouldCacheImmediately`, orientation left to `drawImage`) when the source is more than 1.5x the pixel size the image is drawn at (`fittedSize * scale`, swapped for rotated orientations, both dimensions covered). Smaller sources and incomplete (incremental) data decode as before; gallery and zoom paths are other functions and are untouched, so nothing there gets a blurrier image.
  - **IU7 photo picker** (`a95d5a81`): `assetImage` issues non-synchronous `requestImage` calls on the assets queue (`DeferredAssetRequest` handles cancel before, during and after issuing); synchronous requests stay on the caller. Visible difference: Photos may deliver a cached low-quality image before `requestImage` returns; that first image used to land in the same main-thread pass as the cell setup and now arrives one main-queue hop later (all callers deliver to main: `ImageNode.setSignal`, `TGImageView`, `deliverOnMainQueue`).
  - **IU8 emoji panel** (`abf0e053`): the final `ItemGroup`s of the emoji panel go through an `EmojiItemReuseCache`, so a rebuild whose group is equal hands out the previous instance (and its items). The fingerprint (`EmojiReuseIdentityFingerprint`, review fix) also requires every item to be the same instance: `ItemGroup.==` compares items with `Item.==`, which checks only the file id, so a group keyed by value alone could have handed back items the item cache had just rebuilt for a changed file (`EmojiReuseIdentityFingerprintTests`, 5 tests). Static/unicode items are still built per pass.
  - **IU13 / U8 colour space** (`abf0e053`): two more PhotoResources draw functions create their context with `drawingColorSpace(forSourceImage:)` after decoding, as the chat photo one does (no per-pixel conversion to Display P3 for sRGB sources). Which two TelegramUI callers the 143 samples came from is not proven.

## 13.1.2 (202611052231) — 2026-11-05 — 9971ecaa

TestFlight upload (internal testers). Since 202611051247 (fb1b8101..9971ecaa): the keyboard-open reaction route, profiling waves 1 and 2 (shared core and iOS UI lanes), the signposts and performance tests, the Sendable fixes for the Mac and the module table; the detailed entries are the bullets that were under Unreleased until this upload, now below. Enhanced Security is not in this build: `tg testflight` leaves it off until the distribution profiles are checked (device builds since 202611052030 have it). Mac counterpart: GitHub release 13.1.0 (202611052243). Tester notes (What to Test):

> What to test in this build:
>
> 1. Reactions with the keyboard open: pick a reaction from the message menu while typing. The reaction animation and effect should now play in the chat (the last build only showed the badge).
>
> 2. Faster start: the app no longer scans the whole media cache on every launch (once a day at most), so the first seconds after opening should feel lighter.
>
> 3. Emoji and animated stickers play at the right speed in Low Power Mode and when the phone is hot (they used to run slow), and the emoji tick uses less battery.
>
> 4. Emoji panel opens and switches faster and uses less memory.
>
> 5. Photo picker (attach > Gallery): scrolling a big library should be smoother, with thumbnails ready sooner.
>
> 6. Animated stickers that fail to draw on the GPU fall back cleanly instead of being redrawn on every loop.
>
> 7. Cached media: files you still use are kept, even in chats with a short "keep media" setting.
>
> Please report any crash, freeze, slow scrolling or sticker that stops playing, with the time it happened.

Detailed entries:

- **`tg device` turns on Enhanced Security (soft mode)** when the app profile grants `com.apple.security.hardened-process` (`profile_has_enhanced_security`, adds `--//Telegram:enhancedSecurity`): the app, NotificationService and Share are then signed with hardened-process, checked allocations in soft mode. `tg testflight` (`--app-store`) leaves it off until the distribution profiles are checked.
- Reactions chosen in the context menu with the keyboard open now play their effect (build 202611051247 showed the badge only). The menu is then hosted in the keyboard window, another scene than the message, where the picker's flight and effect never showed and the message's reaction stayed hidden until the 2 s timer. When the menu is not in the chat's window the menu just fades and the chat plays the effect itself through `StandaloneReactionAnimation` (as for a double tap), after `dismiss()` has moved the bubble back into the chat. The check compares the menu's window with the chat's, not with the reaction pill's: while the menu is open the bubble, pill included, is extracted into the menu, so the pill is in the keyboard window too (the first version of this fix compared those and never left the old flight): `contextReactionLandingRoute` (ReactionSelectionNode), `ChatContextReactionStandaloneLanding.swift`, both `awaitingAppliedReaction` closures in `ChatControllerOpenMessageContextMenu.swift`. Keyboard closed: the flight is unchanged. The saved-messages tag tooltip is skipped on this route. Cause (a) cross-window geometry vs (b) overlay not drawing is still open; `ReactionKB` lines in the short log (menu callback, `dismissWithReaction`, flight start/end, timer reveal, cancel, picker window moves) tell them apart. Test: `ContextReactionLandingRouteTests`; the applied line also logs `targetInMenu`, and the standalone route logs `standalone targetInChatWin`.
- **Development profiles carry Enhanced Security** for the app, NotificationService and Share (`device_signing.py profiles`; the capability was enabled on those App IDs on 2026-11-05). Profiles regenerated the same day. Builds turn it on with `--//Telegram:enhancedSecurity` (soft mode).
- **`device_signing.py profiles` asks for Communication Notifications** for the main app: the app has been signed with `com.apple.developer.usernotifications.communication` since 2026-11-04, and a development profile without it makes the phone refuse `tg device` installs (0xe8008015 "A valid provisioning profile for this executable was not found"). Tests in `test_device_signing.py`.
- **Profiling: shared signposts, performance tests with baselines, MetricKit launch tasks** (2026-11-05, branch `perf/signposts-tests`; not built or run yet; only `swift test` of SwiftSignalKit and Postbox ran; the Mac needs `tg core-bump` to this commit):
  - **Signposts**: new `NanogramSignposts`, `UserSignpost`, `PerformanceSignpostName` and `LaunchTrace` in SwiftSignalKit (the same two files in `SwiftSignalKit/Source` for Bazel and `SwiftSignalKit2/Source` for SwiftPM, which the Mac consumes). `Display.PerformanceSignposts` is a typealias with the Mac's API. Six user-level intervals are also emitted in `.pointsOfInterest`: `launchToChatListReady` (AppDelegate), `chatOpenToReady` (navigateToChatController to ChatHistoryListNode ready), `navigationPushToReady` (NavigationController.pushViewController to completion), `chatListFirstFrame` (ChatListControllerImpl init to ChatListNode ready), `chatListFolderSwitch` (ChatListContainerNode.switchToFilter), `callConnect` (requestCall / answer until `.connected`). The composer keystroke interval uses the same begin/end API. No metadata.
  - **Performance tests** (clock, CPU, memory; skipped unless `TG_PERF=1`): `PostboxReadPerformanceTests` (Postbox), `ChatListDiffPerformanceTests` (ChatListUITests), `TextLayoutPerformanceTests` (DisplayTests), `SyntaxHighlightPerformanceTests` (TextFormatTests, libprisma). `SwiftSignalKitTests` (new host test target) pins the signpost names and the launch-task bookkeeping.
  - **`tools/dev/perfgate.py` and `tg perf run|check|record|model`**: compares measured values (XCTest log lines, `.xcresult`, JSON) with `tools/dev/perf/baselines.json`, keyed by `hw.model`; a missing baseline is recorded on the owner's devices (`Mac16,1`, `iPhone18,2`) and never fails; tests in `tools/dev/tests/test_perfgate.py`. Not in the pre-push hook.
  - **MetricKit launch tasks (iOS 27 only)**: `LaunchTrace.begin()` in `didFinishLaunching` opens `account-load` (context received), `chat-list-first-data` (ChatListNode first ready) and `first-frame` (root controller ready, "Launch to ready") with `MetricManager.trackLaunchTask`. Reports stay local (CrashReportCollector); nothing is sent.

- **Profiling wave 2, iOS UI lane** (2026-11-05, branch `perf/wave2-ui`; not built or run; only pure logic ran, in scratch SwiftPM packages; the Bazel test targets below were added but not run):
  - **U1 emoji speed** (bug in wave 1, W1-4b): `DCTAnimationTickScheduler.start` now passes the elapsed time to the tick. For `.fps(n)` links the driver hands out a fixed `1/n`, so on a 60 Hz link a 40 Hz tick advanced 25 ms of animation per 25 ms callback only when n matched; at 40/24/20 Hz the items played at 0.67-0.8x. `sharedDisplayLink` keeps the previous `SharedDisplayLinkDriver.lastTargetTimestamp` and passes `clamp(target - previous, interval, 4 * interval)` (`DCTAnimationTickPolicy.elapsed`; first callback = interval); `runLoopTimer` passes its interval; `animationTick(advance:)` forwards it as `advanceTimestamp`. Display is untouched. Tests: `DCTAnimationTickPolicyTests` (elapsed, clamp, first call, NaN), `DCTMultiAnimationRendererTickTests.testItemsAdvanceByTheElapsedTimeTheSchedulerReports`; the four manual tickers in the DCT tests now pass an elapsed time.
  - **U2 signposts end at the first committed frame**: new component `LaunchSignpostSupport` (`SignpostCommit.afterNextCommit`, `LaunchSignpostPolicy`). `LaunchPhase.firstFrame` ends after the root controller is assigned and a commit has passed (AppDelegate); `chatOpenToReady` ends the same way at both `ChatHistoryListNode` sites; a launch that is still in the background 5 s after `didFinishLaunching` ends every `LaunchPhase` at once (not read in `didFinishLaunching`: with scenes the state can read background on a normal launch) so the MetricKit extended launch measurement does not stay open; `resumeToFirstFrame` runs from `handleWillEnterForeground` to the next commit and uses `PerformanceSignpostName.resumeToFirstFrame`, which the core lane (C5) adds to both `NanogramSignposts.swift` copies (this branch does not compile alone until that name exists).
  - **U4**: `[TGMediaAssetsLibraryClass authorizationStatus]` removed from `TGMediaAssetsLibrary +load` (result was discarded; a TCC round trip in `load_images`).
  - **U5 emoji panel**: `emojiInputData` no longer rebuilds on an unchanged input (`distinctUntilChanged` on hasPremium, availableReactions, searchCategories, iconStatusEmoji, dismissedTrending; value predicates for the item-collections view and the featured packs; an unknown item kind counts as changed). The items of installed packs are reused across rebuilds through `EmojiItemReuseCache`, keyed by (collection id, item index) and fingerprinted by (file, icon, tint); only the last pass is kept. Reused items also hit the `===` shortcut in `Item.==`. Tests: `EmojiItemReuseCacheTests` (new `EntityKeyboardTests` target). Not done: reuse of `ItemGroup`s and of the static/unicode items.
  - **U6 photo picker**: `AssetThumbnailPrefetcher` (MediaAssetsContext) calls `startCachingImages`/`stopCachingImages` for the visible range plus one screen each side (`AssetCachingWindow`), with the cells' exact 256 px size (`MediaPickerGridItemNode.thumbnailTargetSize`) and the same options (`assetImageRequestOptions`, shared with `assetImage`); stops on a memory warning and when the grid goes away. It stops only the assets it started, not `stopCachingImagesForAllAssets`, because the manager is shared. `requestImage` stays where it is: Apple's page does not state the thread of the result handler. Tests: `AssetCachingWindowTests` (new `MediaAssetsContextTests` target).
  - **U7 MetricKit activity intervals**: `MetricActivityIntervals` (CrashReporting) emits `mxSignpost` begin/end through a MetricKit log handle (`MetricManager.logHandle` on iOS 27, `makeLogHandle` before) for `stickerPlayback` (DCT renderer playing state), `call` (`SharedAccountContext.hasOngoingCall`) and `roundVideoRecording` (`ChatController.videoRecorder`). One interval per activity while any holder is active (`ActivityIntervalCounter`, token based). Data stays in MetricKit. `videoPlayback` exists but has no call site yet (the video manager is in TelegramUniversalVideoContent). Tests: `ActivityIntervalCounterTests`.

- **Profiling wave 1, shared core and iOS copies** (2026-11-05, branch `perf/wave1-core`, on top of `perf/signposts-tests`; not built or run; only the pure logic ran, in scratch SwiftPM packages; the Mac needs `tg core-bump` to this branch, and the VP9 file must be identical in both repos):
  - **S3 cache walkers** (`19368e9d`): the `TimeBasedCleanup` scan and `AutomaticCacheEviction` stat every cached file at every launch (3.7 s of CPU on the iPhone). Each now keeps `{completedAt, fingerprint of the limits}` under the key `cacheWalker.<name>` in the account's `StorageBox` metadata table (new `StorageBox.cacheWalkerRecord/setCacheWalkerRecord`; no server traffic, nothing about the database key).
