The scheduled run happens only when the record is missing, a day old, from a different set of limits, or from the future (`CacheWalkerGate`, new `CacheWalkerPolicy.swift`), and starts at least 60 s after the process start (`kinfo_proc` start time). `rescan()` (`Account.rescanCaches`) always runs and records. `TimeBasedCleanup(gatesScans:)` is on in `MediaBox` and off by default, so the existing scan tests keep their behaviour. New signpost intervals `cacheCleanupScan` and `automaticCacheEviction`.
  - **S4 `stat` before `utime`**: `processScheduledTouches` calls `CacheTouchPolicy.touchNeeded`, which skips files that no longer exist and files whose mtime is fresh enough: under 5 min old in `short-cache` (removed 1 h after last use), under 1 h old everywhere else (the shortest keep-media choice is 1 day, so removal moves by under 5 %; 1 h covers the usual relaunch, which a 5 min skip missed). Interval `cacheTouchBatch`.
  - **S5 iOS copy**: `ChunkMediaPlayerV2` takes its timer interval and tolerance from `ChunkMediaPlayerUpdateSchedule` (new file, same rules as the Mac): 60 Hz only for a pending seek, before the first seek, buffering, starved renderers or near the end; 10 Hz while playing steadily; 5 Hz while paused and buffering; no timer for a settled paused player. Tolerance is always `max(10 %, 2 ms)`. The status throttle moved from 0.1 s to 0.09 s so a tick that comes early at 10 Hz does not halve the rate.
  - **S2 step 1**: `VP9HardwareDecoder.swift` (mirrored in the Mac repo) counts hardware starts, cap misses, session failures and ineligible stickers (`VP9HardwareDecoding.stats`), keeps the peak session count, emits the signpost events `vp9HardwareCapReached` and `vp9HardwareSessionFailure`, and calls a reporter at most once a minute when the counters changed (iOS logs it under `VideoSticker`). `VideoStickerDirectFrameSource.suspendHardwareDecoding()` gives the sessions back; `DefaultAnimatedStickerNodeImpl` calls it 2 s after pause or stop, and the next frame sets a decoder up again at the same frame (earlier frames are decoded and dropped; with no free slot libvpx continues from that frame). The cap itself is unchanged (step 3 sizes it from the counters).
  - **Review fixes**: the time-based cleanup gate compared a fingerprint built from the raw short-lived time with the one the scan recorded after `makeScan` set it to "forever" (no size limit), so with no size limit the gate never skipped; both now use `CacheWalkerGate.timeBasedCleanupFingerprint`. A video sticker whose frame cache became complete at the end of its first loop kept its idle hardware decoder (2 of 8 sessions) for as long as it lived, visible or not; it now gives the sessions back then (a later cache miss sets a decoder up from the first frame, as before).
  - **S10 `debug_measureTimeToFirstEvent`**: wraps and prints only under `#if DEBUG` in both SwiftSignalKit trees (10 wrapped signals per chat open in release before).
  - **S10 `.atomic`**: the sticker representation in `FetchCachedRepresentations` (`fetchCachedStickerRepresentation`, temp path that `MediaBox` renames) is written plainly; a failed write removes the partial file.
  - Tests: `CacheWalkerPolicyTests`, `CacheWalkerRecordStoreTests`, `TimeBasedCleanupGateTests` and a new touch test in `TimeBasedCleanupTests` (Postbox), `AutomaticCacheEvictionFingerprintTests` (TelegramCore), `ChunkMediaPlayerUpdateScheduleTests` (MediaPlayer), `VP9HardwareStatsTests` and extra `testSessionLimit` assertions (VP9Hardware), two suspend/resume tests in `VideoStickerHardwareSelectionTests`. Ran in scratch packages: policy 16/16, update schedule 6/6, VP9 stats 5/5 (Swift 6, warnings as errors). The rest need the Bazel test run of the combined build.
- Emoji and sticker animation tick (profiling plan item 10, S6): the renderer's tick ran on a `Foundation.Timer` on the
  main run loop, with no display-rate hint and Low Power Mode read once at creation. It now ticks from the shared
  `SharedDisplayLinkDriver` (`DCTAnimationTickScheduler.sharedDisplayLink`, new `DCTAnimationTickPolicy.swift`), at the
  same 60 or 30 Hz as before, so the system matches the screen's refresh rate to it and stops it in the background.
  Low Power Mode and the thermal state are now watched while the app runs: 30 Hz on Low Power Mode, on a serious or
  critical thermal state and on two-core devices; back to 60 Hz when they clear. Frame timing per tick is unchanged.
  Tests: `DCTAnimationTickPolicyTests`, power-state cases in `DCTMultiAnimationRendererTickTests`.
- `tools/dev/tg device` archives the build's dSYMs to `~/Library/Application Support/Nanogram/dsyms/<build>/` like
  `tg testflight` (UUID file included, TelegramUI UUID printed), so a device build recording can be symbolicated. A
  failed archive warns and does not stop the install.
- **Profiling wave 2, shared core** (2026-11-05, branch `perf/wave2-core`, on top of the wave-1 integration; not built; only pure logic and the Postbox, SwiftSignalKit and VP9 packages ran in SwiftPM):
  - **C1 cache walkers re-arm**: a walker that is not due at launch used to complete and never run again, so a long-lived process (macOS) could go days without enforcing the cache limits. `CacheWalkerGate.nextRunDelay` now waits until `completedAt + 24 h`, and the new `CacheWalkerSchedule.signal` (Postbox) re-arms 24 h (at least 1 h) after each completed run; `TimeBasedCleanup.resetScan` and `AutomaticCacheEviction` use it. The wait follows the wall clock (`suspendAwareDelay`). Launch behaviour is unchanged.
  - **C2 touch interval follows the keep time**: `CacheTouchPolicy.generalInterval` = 24 h for "keep forever", otherwise `min(24 h, max(1 h, keep time / 20))`; short-cache stays 5 min. The keep time it follows is the shortest of the general one and the per-chat ones that `AutomaticCacheEviction` enforces by the same modification time (categories, stories default 2 days, exceptions; `MediaBox.setShortestPerChatStoreTime`), 1 h until both are known; with default settings that is 2.4 h. `TimeBasedCleanupImpl` passes it into `touchNeeded`. A sticker cached three hours ago with a one-week keep time is no longer rewritten at every launch. An explicit eviction run (`rescanCaches`) continues with the daily schedule.
  - **C3 VP9 hardware stats** (`VP9HardwareDecoder.swift`, identical in the Mac repo): a `vp9HardwareIneligible` signpost event, a separate `resumes` counter (`VP9HardwareFrameDecoder(sticker:isResume:)`; `hardwareStarts` no longer counts resumes), and `VP9HardwareDecoding.loadSticker` which checks for a free session slot before reading and parsing the file (a full cap is counted as a cap miss without the read). Used by `makeVideoStickerHardwareStream` (now `(path, isResume)`) and the Mac `VP9HardwareStickerReader`.
  - **C4 `vp9ResumeCatchUp` interval** around the replay loop in `VideoStickerFrameSource.resumeHardwareDecodingIfSuspended`.
  - **C5 signpost names** appended to both `NanogramSignposts.swift` copies (`chatControllerCreated`/`Deinit`, `panelScrollTick`, `mouseMovedDispatch`, `modalOpenToVisible`, `launchToChatListUsable`, `resumeToFirstFrame`, `vp9ResumeCatchUp`, `mediaBoxReopen`) and `NanogramSignposts.event(_:)` for point events.
  - **C6 `MediaBoxFileManager` re-open counter** (counter only, the eviction policy is unchanged): `mediaBoxReopen` event per re-open and a log line once a minute with opened and re-opened counts (`MediaBoxFileManagerStats`).
  - **C7 GPU encode limits and GPU time counters**: `GPUEncodeRetryPolicy` (3 failures per frame or 8 in total stop GPU encoding for that frame or sticker; those frames, and frames of a size the DCT encoder cannot take, are stored by the CPU path of the same cache) in `AnimatedStickerDirectFrameSourceCache`, and `GPUTimeCounters` (GPU time and failures per command buffer in `AnimationCompressor` and `CompressedImageRenderer`, one log line a minute). Defaults unchanged.
  - **C8 `coalesceOnMain`** (both SwiftSignalKit trees): like `deliverOnMainQueue`, but the values of one run-loop turn are merged into the last one; completion and error flush it. No call site uses it yet.
  - **C10 `docs/profiling.md`** (identical in both repos): recording rules (toc after each recording, attach-only limits for Metal System Trace and Processor Trace, remodel, thermal/charger, ESET, second launch), the new signposts.
  - Tests: `CacheWalkerPolicyTests`, `CacheWalkerScheduleTests`, `MediaBoxFileManagerStatsTests` and a re-open test in `MediaBoxFileSystemEfficiencyTests` (Postbox), `OperatorCoalesceOnMainTests` (SwiftSignalKitTesting), `NanogramSignpostsTests`, `VP9HardwareStatsTests`, `GPUEncodeRetryPolicyTests`, `GPUTimeCountersTests`.

## 13.1.2 (202611051247) — 2026-11-05 — fb1b8101

TestFlight upload (internal testers). Since 202611041250 (daac83e2..fb1b8101). Tester notes (What to Test):

> What to test in this build:
>
> 1. Calls: answering an incoming call no longer drops it a moment later. Please call each other a few times (answer from the lock screen and from inside the app) and tell us if a call still ends by itself, with the time.
>
> 2. New setting: Privacy and Security > Voice Calls > "Show Calls in Phone Recents". Turn it off and check that calls no longer appear in the Phone app's Recents.
>
> 3. Coming back to the app after a while no longer freezes it for up to half a minute.
>
> 4. Reactions with the keyboard open: the animation plays, and no menu flashes up after you close the keyboard.
>
> 5. Group video calls: the expanded view shows every participant's picture. Screen sharing no longer shows an old frame after a pause.
>
> 6. Stickers and GIFs use less memory; a broken sticker can no longer slow the app down.
>
> 7. Text like "@7 (pm)" in photo and story captions is no longer lost.
>
> 8. iPad: opening a chat no longer pops up the keyboard, and dragging the messages hides it; photos only underline dates and links after you tap Live Text; the sticker and GIF panel is shorter and its tabs are bigger.
>
> 9. Lockdown Mode (iOS Settings): with it on, Nanogram stops auto-downloading and autoplaying media, link previews and code colouring, and keeps media out of notifications.
>
> 10. New app icon.
>
> Please report any crash, dropped call or freeze, with the time it happened.

Detailed entries:

- **Call stop closures are Sendable-clean**: `CallId` is `Sendable` (two `Int64`s) and both call contexts build `statsLogPath` as a `let`, so the restored call-debug upload closure captures no mutable or non-Sendable state (4 Swift 6 warnings in the Mac's TelegramVoip).
- **Attach > Location as upstream** (owner, 2026-11-05): nearby places come from Telegram's venue bot again (Foursquare data, same lists as official Telegram); reverts a1ab87c9 (MapKit venues, `VenueSearchPolicy`). The Lockdown notification-media tests in HardenedMediaTests stay.
- **Text like "@7 (pm)" is kept** (upstream Mac #205): the `@(\d+)\s*\((.+?)\)` alternative of `markdownRegexFormat` in `ChatTextInputAttributes.swift` matched but `convertMarkdownToAttributes` emitted nothing for it, so the text was dropped from media captions, story captions and message actions from search and profile. The alternative is removed. Tests: `MentionParenthesisMarkdownTests`.
- **Database key vault removed** (owner decision, 2026-11-05): the key is again the plain `.tempkey` file beside the database, protected only by the default key or the passcode, exactly as upstream. The vault bound the key to the device keychain (AfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly), so a backup restored to a new device or a device migration could not open the database. No device ever ran a vault build (never installed or uploaded), so no migration back exists. Removed: `NanogramKeyVault` (source, header, `NanogramKeyVaultTests`, its BUILD targets), the vault adoption in `BuildConfig.m` (`BuildConfig.m`, `BuildConfig.h` and BUILD are back to the state before 1b8add19), the `isEphemeral` throwaway-key flag through `DeviceSpecificEncryptionParameters`, `ValueBoxEncryptionParameters`, `SqliteValueBox` and `AppDelegate`, and its Postbox test (all from 1b8add19, 46485592, 086d16d0). Kept: the guard in `AppDelegate` that replaced the key force unwraps (lint, independent). The `.tempkey` creation lock and atomic write predate the vault and stay. Nothing deletes or overwrites an existing `.tempkey` or database. A `.tempkeyVault` file, if one ever existed, is ignored (upstream does not know it).
- **All of the 2026-11-05 privacy trimming is reverted** (owner: behave like official Telegram): the time zone offset is sent again in the push registration data; app log events (`help.saveAppLog`) are sent as upstream; network telemetry records and reports when the server enables it; the call stats JSON goes to `phone.saveCallDebug` after a call and the full log when the server asks. Reverts the rest of 5f0c8451, b9f14b70, eeb3f4c7 and 5c3edbb1; `tools/dev/tests/test_privacy.py` and `AppLogPrivacyTests` are removed. Earlier security-review changes (Lockdown, key vault, call-log retention) are untouched.
- **rlottie pin 083f19a** (one line for both apps): adds the guarded `LOTTIE_THREAD_SAFE` define the Mac needs; no change for iOS (its BUILD already defines it).
- **GPU sticker and emoji drawing stays the default** (owner, 2026-11-05: it works well in the latest build): reverts the defaults part of 3b641a27 (revision 2 back to CPU). The defaults revision stays 1 (Metal sticker renderer, YUVA emoji cache); revision 2 never shipped. The GPU-time counter from 3b641a27 is removed too (it wrote to the system log every 30 s even with app logging off; MetricKit already reports GPU time).
- **Channel statistics as usual** (owner, 2026-11-05): channel read metrics (`messages.reportReadMetrics`, `MessageReadMetricsTracker`, the `ChatHistoryListNode` hook) are restored from eeb3f4c7^ so channel owners see our readers in their stats. Music listening reports (`messages.reportMusicListen`, `MusicListenTracker`) are restored too, from eeb3f4c7^. The call-log upload guard (`tools/dev/source-guards/test_call_log_privacy.py`, upload ban) is removed, because the call stats upload is back as upstream (found by the pre-push gate); only its 7-day retention check stays.
- **Exact device back in the session list** (owner, 2026-11-05): `MTApiEnvironment` again sends the device model ("iPhone 17 Pro Max"), the full system version and the language with its region, as upstream; the 5f0c8451 trimming of these three is reverted and `MTApiEnvironmentPrivacyTests` removed. (The time zone offset followed in the entry above.)

### Night review fixes (2026-11-05, branch night/review-fixes; nothing built yet)
Private notes. Every item has tests that run at the combined build; none were compiled or run overnight.
- **Nightly cleanup (ios-core-1)**: the BGProcessingTask now passes a 256 MB live-data cap and a `shouldProceed` (not expired, app neither active nor in foreground; read from `Atomic` flags because it runs on the Postbox queue) to `Postbox.optimizeStorageIfNeeded`, which now goes through `beginInternalTransaction` (a paused database delays the check instead of a pause queueing behind a VACUUM). `Postbox.interruptMaintenance()` calls `sqlite3_interrupt` through `SqliteInterruptGate` (only while `vacuum()` runs, so it never hits an ordinary transaction) from the expiration handler and `handleWillEnterForeground`. `SharedWakeupManager.beginCleanupProcessingTask/endCleanupProcessingTask` keep writes enabled while the task runs and end it through `releaseBackgroundTime`. Tests: `SqliteValueBoxVacuumTests` (interrupt), `PostboxStorageOptimizationGateTests`.
- **Sticker/emoji renderers (ios-core-2)**: `nanogramDefaultsRevision` 2 puts `.cpu` and `compressedEmojiCache=false` back as defaults; only blobs written at revision 1 are migrated (revision 0 blobs keep their values). `CompressedImageRendererGPUStats` logs command buffers and GPU seconds every 30 s for the A/B day (F2). Tests: `ExperimentalUISettingsDefaultsTests`.
- **AccountManager gate (ios-core-3)**: `AccountManager.setCanBeginTransactions(_:afterTransactionIfRunning:)`; transactions queue while paused, `ignoreDisabled` ones and the view reads (account list, shared data, notices, access challenge) do not. `SharedWakeupManager` takes the account manager and pauses/resumes it with the Postboxes. Tests: `AccountManagerTransactionGateTests`.
- **Short log on suspend (ios-core-4)**: `Logger.flushPendingShortLog()` (plain async write, no fsync) in `releaseBackgroundTime`'s end closure, `handleDidEnterBackground`, `applicationWillTerminate`.
- **Key vault (ios-core-5 and keyvault-throwaway)**: `deviceKeyPayloadInRootPath:store:callerHoldsOuterLock:isEphemeral:`; `BuildConfig` passes `locked` so the vault does not take `.tempkeyVault.lock` inside `.tempkey.lock`; without the outer lock the vault's waits end 2 s after the call started. A throwaway key is reported (`DeviceSpecificEncryptionParameters.isEphemeral` -> `ValueBoxEncryptionParameters.isEphemeral`) and `SqliteValueBox` never recreates a database for it (open fails -> `.error` -> loading state until the next launch). The key is re-read twice (300 ms) first. Tests: `NanogramKeyVaultTests`, `SqliteValueBoxRecoveryPolicyTests.testThrowawayKeyNeverDeletesTheDatabase`.
- **Calls**: expanded group video tile requests every participant again (`VideoRequestPolicy.limitsRequestedTiles`, calls-2); thermal video release delay 30 s as on the Mac, 5 s for UI-only changes (calls-9); no automatic upload of call stats JSON / full log in 1:1 and group calls, logs kept 7 days at most (calls-4, security H2).
- **WebP (media-libs-webp-ios-bomb-leak)**: `WebP.convert(fromWebP:fittingPixelSize:)` decodes with libwebp's own scaler to premultiplied BGRA, rejects canvases over 4096 per side, frees everything on every path; `fetchCachedStickerAJpegRepresentation` always completes. Tests: `WebPDecodeLimitsTests`.
- **Video stickers**: frame cache streams to the output file and stops at min(2000, 10 s of frames); frame rate from the WebM header, FFmpeg opened lazily; WebP try only after the 12-byte signature check (vsticker-cache-in-memory, video-sticker-duplicate-open). Tests: `VideoStickerFrameCacheTests`, `VideoStickerWebPSignatureTests`.
- **YUVA (media-libs-yuva-per-frame-malloc)**: alpha expanded in place, no per-frame plane. **JPEG (jpeg-thumb-three-pass)**: `tjDecompress2` straight to BGRA. **AV1 (ios-av1-software-single-thread)**: `VideoRenditionCodecPreference`; without hardware AV1 another codec at the same quality wins.
- **Lockdown Mode**: Notification Service shows text only (no media download) when `HardenedMediaPolicy.current` is active, because it cannot tell contacts from strangers (`allowsNotificationMedia(fromContact:)`); `tools/dev/source-guards` (reader guard; the call-log upload ban was later removed, run by the pre-push gate for any Swift change).
- **Cleanup**: removed the TEMP iPad keyboard log; the "legacy H.264 check fixed" line below was wrong (upstream v1 codec offer stays, pending owner).

### Night review fixes: forks and tooling (2026-11-05, branch night/review-fixes-tooling; tgcalls changes on night/review-fixes in klaipher/tgcalls, base b499ac4)
Not yet built. Private notes. The tgcalls fork has no changelog of its own (public repo), so its changes are listed here.
- **Camera colour on iOS calls (calls-3)** (tgcalls 9eb8ed3): the H.264/H.265 encoders tag every I420 copy limited-range BT.601, but `selectCaptureDeviceFormatForDevice` could pick a 420f (full range) format at 1280x720 and the sample went out unconverted, so peers saw crushed blacks and clipped whites. `VideoCameraCapturer` now always asks `AVCaptureVideoDataOutput` for 420v on iOS (hardware conversion), and the legacy format sort orders equal widths 420v first and returns `NSOrderedSame` instead of "descending" for ties. Needs a device check: the `camera capture:` log line must say 420v, and an iPhone to iPhone H.264/H.265 call must show correct blacks and whites.
- **Low-latency encoder fallback (calls-6, calls-7)** (tgcalls f6a9dfb): an output-callback status of -12903 (kVTInvalidSessionErr, delivered for frames in flight after backgrounding) was an "async encode error" and latched low-latency off for the process; it is now the transient `SessionInvalid` kind (three in a row without an encoded frame). Real-time mode is set only for a session that really has low-latency rate control (`shouldUseRealTime`), not for Baseline or fallen-back sessions. Mac H.265 no longer forces low-latency rate control, real-time and ExpectedFrameRate 25 (the H.265 encoder has no latch). Not done: the H.264 `encoderLowLatency` switch (removed on purpose in ad0e3fb5) is NOT restored; whether H.264 low-latency stays on until a device A/B is the owner's call. Host test: `tests/encoder_mode_policy`.
- **Screen share pause/resume (calls-8)** (tgcalls eb170af): `ScreenCaptureKitCapturer` keeps `_activeStream` (touched on `_queue` only). Samples from a stream that was already stopped are dropped, and starting a capture resets the repeater and the last frame, so a resume never repeats the picture from before the pause.
- **Call log rotation (calls-10)** (tgcalls ac0ff1d; Swift in this repo): `LogSinkImpl` no longer reads 8 MB back under WebRTC's global log lock. At half of the cap the file is renamed to `<log>.1` and a new one starts with the marker; together at most `maxBytes`. `callLogNameForId` skips `.log.1`, and `saveCompleteCallDebugLog` uploads `<log>.1` first. The in-memory path keeps the substring approach. Host test: `tests/log_sink`.
- **One FFmpeg flag list (media-libs-ffmpeg-ios-config-drift)**: `submodules/ffmpeg/Sources/FFMpeg/ffmpeg-common-flags.sh`, sourced by `build-ffmpeg-bazel.sh` (and by the Mac `build.sh` through `submodules/telegram-ios`). iOS now has the Vorbis decoder, drops the nonexistent `libopus` demuxer/parser and the trailing comma, and disables network, SecureTransport, bzlib, iconv, AppKit, AVFoundation, CoreImage, Metal and SDL like the Mac; the Mac gains the native AV1 decoder. Platform extras stay explicit (iOS muxers and the darwin AudioToolbox/hwaccel block; Mac audiotoolbox, aac, alac_at, gsm_ms_at, hwaccels). `native-libs.json` probes read the shared file (`also`), and `test_ffmpeg_flags_parity.py` pins the allowed differences.
- **cachegc (our-changes-tooling-bazel-output-bases-not-collected, ...-cache-root-cap-unenforced)**: the Bazel output base of a merged linked worktree (HEAD an ancestor of a base branch, server dead, nobody working there, idle 2 h) is removed at once; new `low_free_space_gb` (60) runs the merged-base and worktree categories first, ignores the 30-minute throttle, uses 1 day instead of 14 for merged worktrees and a 120 s budget. New `cache-root` category enforces `caps_gb.cache_root_total` (LRU over DerivedData-*, spm-*, tgios-work). `du` moved out of planning: sizes are cached in `cachegc-sizes.json` by path+mtime and measured only where a cap needs them; freed bytes come from the volume's free space. Both policy copies are identical (Bazel cache cap 20 GB in both).
- **Upstream sync dry run (upstream-sync-no-sync-tooling)**: `tools/dev/upstream-sync/sync.py` (`tg upstream-sync --tag <tag>`). In a scratch bare repository (objects borrowed from this repo) it fetches the tag, builds synthetic commits OLD (the tree of `0650eac5`) and NEW (upstream tag) and runs `git merge-tree --write-tree --merge-base=OLD main NEW`; reports conflicts by area and upstream pin bumps against our fork branches. Changes and fetches nothing in this repo. `tg setup` now enables `rerere.enabled` and `rerere.autoupdate`.
- Tests (pure Python, run at the final combined build): `tools/dev/tests/test_cachegc.py`, `test_upstream_sync.py`, `test_ffmpeg_flags_parity.py`, `test_native_libs.py`.

### Build system: disk, servers and parallel worktrees (night/build-system)
Tooling only, nothing in the app. Private notes.
- `cachegc` (shared with the Mac repo, identical file and policy): stops the Bazel server of a deleted worktree and removes its output base at once, and removes the output base of an existing worktree after 3 days without a Bazel command (no server running); trims the Bazel disk cache by least recent use to 90% of its 20 GB cap before builds (Bazel's own collection needs 5 idle minutes and never ran between back-to-back builds); decides "in use" per directory instead of skipping SwiftPM scratch, `.build` and DerivedData whenever any xcodebuild or swift ran anywhere; removes `spm-<checkout>-*` and `DerivedData-<suffix>` of checkouts that are gone and enforces `cache_root_total` (40 GB), which no code enforced; plans from remembered sizes (`cachegc-sizes.json`) instead of `du` over everything, so the 25 s budget is no longer spent on one removal; reports unremovable Instruments traces once.
- `tools/dev/tg` runs `cachegc guard` before build, test, run, device, testflight and watch build/test: hard caps always, every rule below 50 GB free, and below 20 GB free the build does not start (`TG_ALLOW_LOW_DISK=1` overrides). Policy keys: `free_space_gb`, `guard`, `bazel_output_base_unused_days`, `bazel_max_idle_secs`.
- `.bazelrc`: `startup --max_idle_secs=1800` and `--shutdown_on_low_sys_mem` (idle servers used to live 3 hours). `tg cache-cleanup verify` checks the number against the policy. Comment in `tools/dev/tg` and `docs/local-development.md` said the disk cache cap was 30 GB; it has been 20 GB since bb19b795.
- `tg worktree add <name>` / `remove <name> [--force]` (`tools/dev/worktree/worktree.py`, same module in the Mac repo): shallow submodules and linked `dev-configuration.json` / `user.bazelrc` on add; remove refuses dirty or unpushed worktrees and ones holding release archives, then stops the Bazel server and deletes the output base. Tests: `tools/dev/tests/test_worktree.py`, `test_cachegc.py`. See `docs/cache-cleanup.md`.

### Shared code and tooling night (2026-11-05, branch night/shared-code)
Not built yet; checked by Python tests and at the combined build. Private notes.
- **Local voice transcription is on-device only** (`LocalAudioTranscription`, switch still off by default): the recognizer was told it supports on-device recognition for every locale; now the real flag decides, the request always has `requiresOnDeviceRecognition = true`, and a locale without an on-device model gives no transcript.
- **Parity check** (`tools/dev/parity/parity.py`, identical in the Mac repo): `mirror ios=<file> mac=<file>` lines in `docs/mac-core-paths.txt` compare the blob ids of files kept as copies in both repos (VP9HardwareDecoder.swift, WebMStickerDemuxer.swift, docs/feature-parity.md; a warning on iOS, an error on the Mac side; an iOS push touching one says so), and the `.gitmodules` branch of every fork both apps pin from the same repository must match (tgcalls `nanogram-shared`; rlottie soft until 2026-11-18). The Mac has to take the new paths file with one `tg core-bump` after this lands on main.
- **Cache cleanup** (`tools/dev/cachegc`, identical in the Mac repo): output bases of deleted worktrees are removed even while their Bazel server still runs (the server gets SIGTERM, then SIGKILL, only when its command line carries `--output_base=<that base>`; a base whose workspace exists is never touched). New category `bazel-disk-cache` trims the disk cache to the cap in the policy by least recent use, never during a Bazel build, never entries newer than 15 minutes, and further when free space is below `free_space_floor_gb` (25), where `auto` also skips its 30 minute throttle. `tg cache-cleanup report` shows free space against the floor. The Mac policy keeps its own 30 GB cap value.
- **`tg build-drift`** (`tools/dev/lint/buildpkgdrift.py`): reports where BUILD and Package.swift of the 41 core modules that have both disagree in dependency modules or excluded sources. Report only; 12 modules differ today (MediaPlayer, MetalEngine, TelegramVoip, TelegramCore, MTProtoRustEngine, ... ), some on purpose.
- `tools/dev/native/native-libs.json`: the Mac probes for libvpx and opus read the core's copies (the Mac no longer vendors them).
- New `docs/feature-parity.md` (iOS, Mac, official Telegram) and a spec status fix (R7 done, 1907a285).

### Networking and crypto review (2026-11-05, night/net-crypto)
Not yet built into a TestFlight build. Private notes. Shared core: reaches the Mac with the next core bump.
- **MtProtoKit timers share wake-ups** (029d7df3): `MTTimer` used zero leeway for every network timeout, retry and back-off; it now follows SwiftSignalKit's `.automatic` rule (`MTTimerLeewaySeconds`: min(10%, 1 s) for timeouts of 1 s or more, none below).
- **Bounded per-session message-id tracking** (2459826e): `MTSessionInfo` kept every processed incoming id, every sent-once outgoing id and every container mapping for the life of a session. Processed and sent-once ids are now a sorted window of the newest 2000 (an older id counts as seen once the window is full, as in tdlib's `MessageIdDuplicateChecker` and the Rust engine's `DuplicateChecker`, so replays are still ignored; security review L-1). Container mappings an hour older than the newest container are dropped, checked when the map doubles.
- **DH prime check order** (6829b30f, 744572cb): `MTCheckIsSafePrime` checks the 2048-bit length and top bit before it reads the keychain's cached verdict (security review L-2).
- **tde2e hardened** (1a420298): `third-party/td/build-td-bazel.sh` adds the `.bazelrc` hardening (zero-init, strong stack protector, libc++ fast hardening) to the CMake target build of tde2e/tdutils, which never saw Bazel's copts. Host TL generators stay plain.
- Tests: `MtProtoKitTests` gains `MTTimerLeewayTests`, `MTSessionInfoWindowTests`, `MTSafePrimeCacheTests` (not run: they need the simulator test build); `tools/dev/tests/test_net_crypto_builds.py` (3 pass).

### Privacy: stop telling Telegram how the app is used (night/privacy, 2026-11-05)
Not yet built. Private notes. Evidence: `secreview-A-privacy.md`.
- **No channel read metrics** (`messages.reportReadMetrics`): `reportPeerReadMetrics` in `TelegramEngineMessages.swift` returns `.complete()` and sends nothing. `MessageReadMetricsTracker` (TelegramCore) is deleted and `ChatHistoryListNode` no longer measures visible posts, time in view or viewport ratios on scroll.
- **No music listening reports** (`messages.reportMusicListen`): `reportMusicListened` is a no-op; `MusicListenTracker` is deleted and `MediaManager` no longer feeds it player state.
- **Calls keep their logs on the device**: `_internal_saveCallDebugLog` (phone.saveCallDebug, route IP:port) and `_internal_saveCompleteCallDebugLog` (phone.saveCallLog) never touch the network; the 1:1 and group call contexts no longer call them. Local log files and the in-app debug log value stay.
- **Smaller connection fingerprint** (`MTApiEnvironment.m`, `BuildConfig.m`): `tz_offset` is no longer in the push registration data; `langCode` is the language without region ("en", "zh-Hans"); `systemVersion` is the major number ("18"); `deviceModel` is the family ("iPhone"/"iPad"/"Mac") instead of the exact model. The signing certificate data (`signatureDict`) and device token fields are untouched.
- **No analytics events** (`help.saveAppLog`): `appLogEventMayLeaveDevice(type:)` is the only gate, backed by `appLogEventTypesAllowedToLeaveDevice`, which is empty. Events are not queued and anything already queued is removed unsent in `sendAppLogEventsBatch`. `networkTelemetryShouldRecord` is false in release regardless of the server flag and the telemetry reporter does not start.
- Tests (run at the combined build): `AppLogPrivacyTests` (TelegramCore), `MTApiEnvironmentPrivacyTests` (MtProtoKit); `tools/dev/tests/test_privacy.py` checks the sources and runs without a build.
- Review fix: `OngoingCallContext.stop` no longer keeps `let callId = self.callId`, which the removed upload left unused; TelegramVoip builds with `-warnings-as-errors`, so it would have failed the build. `test_privacy.py` (`test_call_stop_leaves_no_unused_locals`) checks both call contexts' `stop`.

### iPad media panel (night/ipad-panel)
- **GIF/sticker/emoji panel is shorter on iPad.** It was as tall as the system keyboard plus the suggestions bar from the device profile (`DeviceMetrics.standardInputHeight`: 491 pt of a 13" iPad's 1032 pt landscape screen, 48%), which left one message visible. `tabletMediaPanelHeight` (Display `TabletLayoutRules.swift`) now caps the resting height at 40% of the chat's height and at the system keyboard height when one is on screen, with a 280 pt minimum, never above the old height; portrait is unchanged (40% is more than the old height there). `ChatControllerNode.restingInputNodeHeight` applies it to `ChatEntityKeyboardInputNode` only (not the bot-keyboard node), including the drag-to-expand distance. Dragging up still expands to full height. iPhone unchanged.
- **Larger GIFs / Stickers / Emoji switcher on iPad.** Same floating glass lens, text 17 pt instead of 14, cells 36 pt, 20 pt side insets, 48 pt row (`mediaPanelSwitcherMetrics`, used by `EntityKeyboardBottomPanelComponent`). Tabs at the top were not chosen (see decisions in the branch report). iPhone unchanged.
- Tests: `TabletLayoutRulesTests` (media panel height and switcher cases), run by `//submodules/Display:TabletLayoutTests`. Not built or run, no Bazel run or simulator in this session.

### Documentation and change records (2026-11-05, night/docs)
No build impact; private notes.
- `CLAUDE.md` (1821 lines) is split into `docs/` topical files (index and old-section map in `docs/README.md`); `AGENTS.md` is the lean every-session file. `submodules/TgVoipWebrtc/CLAUDE.md` is split into `docs/subsystems/tgcalls/`.
- New `docs/development-pipeline.md`, `docs/fork-records.md`, `NANOGRAM-CHANGES.md`; `tools/dev/lint/changelog_reminder.py` makes the pre-push hook warn when product code is pushed without a change record (`TG_CHANGELOG_STRICT=1` blocks).
- Removed finished plans and gate records; fixed stale numbers in `docs/local-development.md` and dead spec links in `docs/instantpage-richtext.md`.

### Security review follow-up (2026-11-04, merged from sec/hardening, sec/platform, sec/db-keys)
Not yet built into a TestFlight build. Private notes.
- **Lockdown Mode and code highlighting** (7002c268, 41799c70, a1ab87c9, 19defdcb, a31481dc, 7250fa8e): one shared libprisma engine (upstream 94640c6 plus stats/limits hooks) with a depth cap of 32 and a per-block search budget; worst adversarial block 649 ms. New `submodules/HardenedMedia` (policy: no auto-download from non-contacts, no autoplay or looping, no link previews or highlighting under Lockdown). `SharedAccountContext` rebuilt the live auto-download settings without the policy, which undid the launch hardening at the first emission; `effectiveMediaAutoDownloadSettings(stored:autodownload:policy:)` in TelegramUIPreferences now applies the policy after the server presets. `LockdownMode` counts `com.apple.developer.lockdown-mode.state` only when it is boolean true (a false value led to `os_lockdown_mode_enabled()`, which aborts). Attach > Location no longer sends the position to a venue bot (MapKit venues, `VenueSearchPolicy`; secret chats and stories never query bots).
- **Platform hardening** (ec9beb6d, 0f87fa14, 073e3ae4, 65888574): every app extension signs with explicit minimal entitlements (app group plus own application-identifier, no keychain-access-groups, so the private wallet/biometric keychain group is main-app only). The Notification Service does not request communication notifications (its profiles do not grant it). Enhanced Security entitlements (hardened-process, version 1, checked allocations, soft mode) sit behind `--//Telegram:enhancedSecurity` (off); dyld-ro and platform restrictions behind `--//Telegram:enhancedSecurityRestrictions` (off). `.bazelrc` adds `-ftrivial-auto-var-init=zero`, `_LIBCPP_HARDENING_MODE_FAST` and `-fstack-protector-strong` to every C-family compile; FFmpeg gets the stack protector.
- **Calls and Siri** (8fa4b246, 859ba454): Privacy and Security > Voice Calls has "Show Calls in Phone Recents" (`VoiceCallSettings.showCallsInRecents`, default on, mirrored by `CallKitPrivacy`). It drives `includesCallsInRecents`; outgoing-call `INStartCallIntent` donations need no passcode and Recents on; the Notification Service skips its ringing-call donation when the switch is off.
- **Database key vault** (REMOVED again, see Unreleased) (1b8add19, 46485592): `.tempkey` is now `.tempkeyVault`, format NGKV v1 (random 32-byte keychain wrap key, AfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly, access group `group.<base bundle>`; optional PBKDF2-SHA256 600k passcode mix; AES-256-CBC plus HMAC-SHA256; crash-safe migration that reads the vault back before deleting the legacy file). A lost or damaged vault is moved to `.tempkeyVault.lost` and replaced by one new key (before: a throwaway key on every launch). Unreadable files and later-format vaults are never replaced. A transient keychain error is retried 3 times, 50 ms apart. One-way: builds without the vault create a new key and discard the local database.
- Tests: `bazel test //third-party/libprisma:SyntaxHighlighterTests //submodules/HardenedMedia:HardenedMediaTests //submodules/BuildConfig:NanogramKeyVaultTests` pass; `tools/dev/tests` 129 pass. The ios_unit_test targets (TelegramUIPreferencesTests, TelegramCallsUITests, NotificationServiceTests) are not run (they need simulator and signing).

### App icon
- New default icon, the Sphere (a glossy blue sphere with a light beam on a night sky), replaces the Comet as the primary icon. `Telegram/Telegram-iOS/Nanogram.icon` is now layered: Background (gradient, vignette, stars; full-bleed, opaque), Light (glow and beam), Sphere (glass layer plus a raster sheen), Sparkle. Dark has a deeper background; tinted swaps the sphere, light and sparkle for grayscale layers. No SVG filters (Icon Composer ignores them; the blurred core and sheen are 1024 px PNG layers).
- iOS 15-25 get the fallback actool renders from the same `.icon` (rules_apple refuses an `.appiconset` next to a `.icon`, and actool ignores it).
- Settings > Appearance > App Icon lists Sphere first and selected by default (`NanogramSphere`, previews in `NanogramSphere.alticon` from `AlternateIconSources/NanogramSphere.svg` via `render.swift`); the Comet is a normal alternate now. The watch app icon is re-rendered from the same SVG (`render-app-icon.swift`).
- Policy test `tools/dev/tests/test_app_icon.py`.

### iPad gallery
- **Photos in the viewer no longer get dates, times and links underlined on their own.** The system Live Text on gallery photos (A-16, `GallerySystemLiveText`) attached `.textSelection` and `.dataDetectors`; VisionKit draws data-detector highlights as soon as the analysis lands (about 0.5 s after opening). Now only `.textSelection` is on (`galleryLiveTextInteractionTypes(userRequestedRecognition:)` in Display `TabletLiveTextRules.swift`), with `selectableItemsHighlighted = false`, so nothing is drawn until a long press selects text. The background analysis stays lazy (dwell 0.5 s): selection needs a ready analysis, and an analysis is invisible. Data detectors are only returned for `userRequestedRecognition: true`, not wired to Telegram's text button because its own overlay already draws recognised links and codes. Tests in `TabletReviewFixesTests`.

### iPad keyboard
- **Opening a chat on iPad no longer raises the on-screen keyboard.** Every row tap in the chat list asks the chat to focus its input (`peerSelected(..., activateInput: true)` in ChatListNode, upstream behaviour). On iPhone that is invisible; on iPad the chat is already laid out beside the list, so the field took focus at once. `shouldActivateInputOnChatOpen` (Display `TabletSystemRules.swift`) now lets an iPad focus only with a hardware keyboard (`GCKeyboard.coalesced`); iPhone stays upstream. Wired in `ChatListController` where the row tap navigates.
- **Dragging the message history dismisses the iPad keyboard** (`shouldDismissKeyboardOnHistoryDrag`, new `ChatHistoryListNode.beganUserDragging`): the window's interactive keyboard gesture is phone-only (`Window1`, `recognizer.isEnabled = type == .phone`), so on iPad only the tap dismissed it. Not with a hardware keyboard. Tap on the history and the keyboard's dismiss key already worked (end of editing resets the input mode to `.none`).
- Unit tests in `TabletSystemRulesTests`. Not verified in an iPad simulator (none installed) or with a Bazel run (no signing password in the worktree).

## 13.1.2 (202611041250) — 2026-11-04 — daac83e2

Uploaded to TestFlight; What to Test notes set by `tg testflight` after processing. dSYMs: ~/Library/Application Support/Nanogram/dsyms/202611041250.
Two earlier attempts the same day did not reach testers: 202611041214 was built but not uploaded (the export is named Nanogram.ipa since the branding change; the upload looked for Telegram.ipa, fixed in cc0f711f), and 202611041227 was uploaded and rejected by App Store Connect with ITMS-90626 (Siri vocabulary phrases still named INStartAudioCallIntent after the extension moved to INStartCallIntent; fixed in b923700d).

Changes since build 202611031553 (a449bd87): `git log a449bd87..daac83e2`. Private notes; not shown to testers.

### Shared core with the Mac (core merge)
- The Mac core (`mac/core`) is merged into `main`; Mac-only behaviour stays behind `#if os(macOS)` (48 kHz audio, AV1 decode threads, untagged frames, `nw_path_monitor`, ThreadPool QoS — the QoS mapping was briefly on iOS and is Mac-only again, 0e6cf57d). Ports that now run on iOS too: SQLite connections opened `NOMUTEX` (single-queue), share-extension 256-page cache, VACUUM never crashes and skips huge databases, FTS self-heal, cheap `exists()` (R2-38..43); 10-bit/HDR decode, passthrough colour from the stream's VUI (895eaaef), scrub and cached previews tagged sRGB instead of Display P3 (86064f5e via the merge); Ogg Opus resume continues the stream; local translation falls back to the server; history preload stops replaying once a chat has no hole.
- WebRTC: 12 tg_owt security and crash fixes on `third-party/webrtc` (`klaipher/webrtc` branch `nanogram-security-fixes`, 2c6b83a); allow-list of the 7 differing seam files.
- Parity gate (`tools/dev/parity`, `docs/mac-core-paths.txt`), shared native-library versions (`native-libs.json`), module settings table, shared cache cleanup (`tools/dev/cachegc`, Bazel disk cache cap 30 GB), pre-push hook; the hook now drops inherited `GIT_*` variables (3534f52c) after a push from a worktree turned the main checkout bare.
- `.gitmodules`: webrtc from `klaipher/webrtc`, tgcalls tracks `nanogram-shared` (afdc8092).

### Calls
- tgcalls pinned to the merged `nanogram-shared` branch (54220d6c): one switch class `CallsRuntimeSwitches`, one Streams & Calls Debug Settings section, low-risk fixes always on; batch C (call screen stops drawing at rest, livestream render loop follows what plays, thermal video ladder, background video), group video frames outlive their command buffer and only visible tiles are requested, screen-share frames copied per plane; H.265 start bitrate and decoder/pool reset caps; legacy v1 calls keep the upstream codec offer (H.264 only on x86_64; arm64 hardware H.264 for v1 peers waits for the owner's decision, tgcalls 270a17d); review fixes (still screen keeps reaching the encoder, ducking off by default, request hysteresis).
- Incoming call notifications: caller photo from `INStartCallIntent`, none while locked or with names hidden, call back from the right account and on the main queue; Phone Recents and Siri call back 64-bit ids as video when it was.

### Sending, media and storage
- Pending sends never wait forever on a missing thumbnail, transform or lost answer; failures go to the critical log.
- HLS video writes each byte once; MediaBox and SQLite count their writes (`DiskWriteAccounting`, hourly and background reports); database writes stop before a background task ends (0xdead10cc).
- GIFs: MPEG-4 Part 2 GIFs play instead of freezing; GIF tiles cover video stickers on the reused pixel-buffer path.
- MultipartUpload 128 KB parts for small files and six in flight; FetchV2 adaptive window; TCP keepalive 30 s idle.
- Wallet state requests back off and retry until the server answers.

### iPad
- Resizable chat-list column, window controls, minimum size, accent tint, text size, Live Text, translation and share anchors; in-app cameras keep running in Split View and Stage Manager; Power Saving follows the system (also on iPhone: heat, Low Power Mode, iOS 27 reduced resource usage); keyboard band and sticker panel fixes.

### Extensions, privacy, branding
- Share extension: attachments load as files, large videos no longer freeze share-to-story, failed sends reported, Send in HD.
- Widgets hide text and counts while passcode-locked; Spotlight indexes no contacts while a passcode is set; Proxy no longer reads the clipboard; Location asks for Always only for live location.
- Branding: Nanogram in Siri phrases, bundle names, language packs, QR login, welcome screen and calls; a lint keeps Telegram's name and logo out.
- Voice messages: correct progress on the first play.

### Siri
- Vocabulary phrases name INStartCallIntent in all 20 languages, matching the extension (b923700d); `test_siri_vocabulary.py` keeps every phrase within the extension's IntentsSupported.

### Tooling
- `tg testflight` uploads the exported `.ipa` whatever its name (cc0f711f); Postbox TimeBasedCleanupTests wait for the database to open (bca4f362); lint fixes for the merged Mac core code (57a3839a); TgVoipWebrtc host tests take NDEBUG from the library's flags (daac83e2).

## 13.1.2 (202611031553) — 2026-11-03 — a449bd87

Uploaded to TestFlight; App Store Connect processing: VALID; What to Test notes set. dSYMs: ~/Library/Application Support/Nanogram/dsyms/202611031553.

Changes since build 202611031231 (afac00d5). Private notes; not shown to testers.

### Calls
- **Incoming calls ring as a notification without the filtering entitlement** (33dc472b). The Notification Service extension gave every call push to CallKit with `reportNewIncomingVoIPPushPayload`, which iOS only allows with `com.apple.developer.usernotifications.filtering`. Nanogram doesn't have it, so iOS refused every call and the push was dropped. Without the entitlement, or when CallKit refuses, the call now shows as "<caller> is calling you" with the ringtone, and tapping it opens the caller's chat on the right account. Whether the extension has the entitlement is the Info.plist flag `NanogramNotificationFiltering`, which Telegram/BUILD writes from the same condition that grants the entitlement.
- **Call pushes are never quieted or removed** (a21301a7). If the extension leaves a call push empty, the server's alert and sound are passed through unchanged instead of becoming a silent placeholder that removes itself.
- **Every VoIP push reports a call to CallKit** (7736c798). Exits with no report (no payload, decode or decrypt failure, unknown key, no account state yet, bad JSON) now report a placeholder call and end it at once, so iOS keeps delivering VoIP pushes. The rule lives in `VoIPPushCallReport` (TelegramCallsUI).
- **The call notification stops ringing once the app rings** (0e50191c). The marker key moved to `IncomingCallNotifications`. The VoIP handler removes the caller's notifications when CallKit accepts the report. With CallKit off, SharedAccountContext removes them when the call stops ringing. The app's sweep drops any older than 90 s.
- **registerDevice failures are logged** (074bc0ff): the error, token type and sandbox flag (never the token). The behaviour is unchanged.
- Known risk, not covered by a test: if both a regular push and a VoIP push arrive for one call, the extension's notification may arrive after the app has already removed it, and ring over CallKit.

### App extensions (Siri, widgets, Share, notifications)
- **No crash when the account manager's state is missing** (87b20553). Extensions open with `removeDatabaseOnError: false`, which hit `preconditionFailure()` in AccountManagerImpl.init when `accounts-metadata/atomic-state` was missing or couldn't be decoded. They now open with the legacy tables' records, held in memory only.
- **An extension's in-memory state is never written** (583e7b97). `isStateInMemoryOnly`: no atomic-state sync, no legacy table writes, and the access challenge resolves without migration. Before this, the Share extension could write a keychain passcode, and the app's first launch then didn't count as a fresh install.
- **Only a missing file falls back to the legacy tables** (587800e6). A file that exists but can't be read or decoded opens with no accounts (keeping the legacy passcode so the extension still locks) and is never overwritten. The app keeps upstream's rebuild.

### Reactions and stickers
- **Reactions draw on the CPU renderer, as upstream does** (269bdd54). Under "GPU for large", reactions (240–320 px) used Metal and often set it up on the main thread when tapped, which left them empty for a few seconds.
- **Animation cache: converted items go into place on a dedicated store queue** (63a5b341), not behind every queued conversion on the fetch queue.
- **Sticker frames survive a view resize** (e821543a). A UIImageView subclass restores contents that were set directly.
- **Metal renderer setup moved to a utility queue after launch** (503e429a), via `AnimatedStickerMetalPrewarm`.
- **Reaction hand-off waits for the button's first frame** (1e7e0bc8, then f9b90fe3 with no timeout). `ReactionIconContentGate` runs the hand-off on `InlineStickerItemLayer`'s first real frame (the silhouette placeholder doesn't count; a449bd87 adds a test). It runs at once when the button switches to another reaction, and is dropped on reset.
- **Missing effect file: hand-off at once** (ac48011c). The log line is written only when the missing file changed the outcome (888f112b).
- **Double tap waits for the new button's first frame** (393ba10e), through `ReactionIconView.handOffAnimation(from:)`.
- **Long-press and stars reactions bridge a button with no frame yet** (1783031d). A snapshot of the reaction node, or the star itself, stays in the button until the first frame (`handOffAnimation(from:adoptingAt:)`, `ReactionContextNode.makeEmptyButtonBridge`).
- **A new animation layer starts hidden while a stand-in is in place** (e3a1503b).
- Known cosmetic issues on a cold cache:
  - A long-press built-in reaction may briefly show both the snapshot and the reaction node.
  - The bridge snapshot is about half the final size.
  - The stand-in star is a live SCNView until the first frame.
  - A double tap with a custom emoji, and `StandaloneReactionAnimation.cancel()`, can still show an empty button.
  - There is no timeout: if no frame ever arrives, the stand-in stays as long as the button exists.

### Tooling
- tg testflight: keeps dSYMs for every build (08de7474) and archives only this build's dSYMs, keeping the newest 20 builds (9cc7d387, 0241f014). Notes are required (7c546dfd). Exit 3 means uploaded but notes not set (747d6629); exit 4 means Apple rejected the build (0241f014). Fixed a SIGPIPE on large ipa listings (45fd0e07).
- tf_notes.py: retries rate limiting and timeouts (dca4c9bb).
- tg device: warns that a development-signed install gets no pushes or calls (58ef9dc6).
- Docs: d0fc02b9.
